2026-08-20  v3.13.3 VERIFIED ON SCREEN: one launch, full click-through, all
                     four pass criteria met - the freeze is closed

  The 11:31:18 launch (first since the 11:25:36 deploy; the user drove a
  full click-through and the log carries the whole of it):

  1. WARM THREAD RAN AT BOOT. "SELRES display enumerated ONCE in 9201ms -
  28 distinct mode(s)" landed at 11:31:27, 9s after DLL load and 29s BEFORE
  the first Graphic Options open (11:31:56). The line's own caveat - "unless
  the time above is part of a SELPERF pass" - is answered by the tables: NO
  sel.buildResList bucket appears in any SELPERF dump, so the enumeration
  never ran on the UI thread. 9201ms vs the 3264ms measured on the first
  click in v3.13.2: the warm run overlaps game boot (dgVoodoo init + load
  contention) and is 2.8x slower for it - harmless, because it is off the
  critical path and finished half a minute before any dialog.
  2. FIRST OPEN INSTANT. Rows built 2ms after the serviced line; sel.open
  bucket n=4 max=0.464ms; the 70 passes before the first close total
  27.8ms, max 2.6ms - no watchdog fired while any dialog was up.
  3. ALL THREE MODE LISTS RIGHT. Windowed built 6 rows (the new branch),
  staged and committed 1920x1200. Borderless: 1 row (the 2400x1600
  desktop), with the correct fallback line "800x600 is not available in
  Borderless - selecting the largest that is". Fullscreen: 7 rows.
  4. SELPERF CLEAN on all four dumps (three closes + shutdown): no
  frame-gap lines, no bucket near a second. The biggest single pass is the
  124ms CLOSE pass = the commit itself (iniWrite 34-40ms across the
  OneDrive filter + dgVoodoo.conf 3-25ms + commit overhead) - real work,
  not a stall. The 3264ms buildResList bucket that defined the defect is
  simply absent from every table.

  Scale picks were exercised in the same session: first Accept committed
  AutoScale=0 ScaleFactor=1.5 (manual), second Accept committed AutoScale=1
  (Auto) - both via ACCEPT with Cancel/Default disabled, exactly as
  designed. Final committed graphics state: Borderless 2400x1600, dgVoodoo

  display-level and tier-independent, and the next launch (Borderless

2026-08-20  v3.13.3  THE INSTRUMENT NAMED BOTH IN ONE LAUNCH: the freeze was
                     the display enumeration itself, and Windowed had NO BRANCH

  One measuring session (v3.13.2) closed a two-day argument with the freeze:

  ⭐ 1. THE FREEZE = EnumDisplaySettingsW, 3,264ms, ONCE. SELPERF: "pass took
  3266ms. In-pass: sel.buildResList=3264ms/1" - the first enumeration through
  dgVoodoo's virtualized display costs 3.3 SECONDS on this machine, and
  v3.13.1's cache had correctly made it once-per-session... scheduled at the
  one moment the user is guaranteed to be watching: the first click. Every
  suspect the theories favoured measured INNOCENT - the OneDrive logger
  (361 writes = 7ms), the message path (13,203 messages = 6ms), the tree
  walks (100 = 1.3ms).
  CURE (user direction: "load the resolutions during game startup when the
  DLL loads"): SelEnumOnce is now tri-state (idle/enumerating/done) and a
  warm thread kicks it in the DIRECTOR CONSTRUCTOR, during plugin scan. The
  first open finds the cache warm; the UI thread's only race path is a
  correctness net that waits. The enumerated-ONCE log line now carries its
  own duration, so the cure is measurable in the same line that proved the
  defect.

  ⛔ 2. "SELRES built 0 row(s) for Windowed" - SelBuildResList HAD NO
  WINDOWED BRANCH. Borderless returns early, Fullscreen has its block,
  Windowed fell through both ifs to the sort with an empty list. The user's
  screenshot of an open-and-empty drop list was the list being empty AT
  BIRTH - the mid-drop-mutation theory died in one measured line. The
  familiar-size table was also PRIVATE to the Fullscreen branch (part of how
  a sibling branch could not exist); hoisted to kSelFamiliar, one copy.
  The new branch's rules differ from Fullscreen on purpose: NO display-mode
  check (a window needs no mode), and the DESKTOP-EQUAL size is excluded
  (chrome overflows the screen; that job is Borderless). The existing
  largest-that-fits fallback then stages 1920x1200 as pending - already
  measured doing so.

  Test-SelectorDerive grew both rules (windowed desktop-exclusion + the
  no-mode-table question) and a self-counting pass line: 23 checks green.

  Files: src\UiSpike.{h,cpp}, src\SC4UIScaleDllDirector.cpp,
  _tests\Test-SelectorDerive.py.

2026-08-20  v3.13.2  THE FREEZE GETS AN INSTRUMENT, NOT A THIRD GUESS - and
                     the v3.14 selector rule is now a gate written BEFORE its code

  User report after v3.13.1: "It still isn't working / is still freezing up"
  - plus a screenshot of the Resolution drop list OPEN AND EMPTY. Two freeze
  "fixes" (v3.13.0, v3.13.1) have now shipped on inference; this build ships
  measurement instead. No behaviour changes.

  WHAT THE LOG ALREADY PROVED before this build:
  - v3.13.1 was the running build; the caches worked ("display enumerated
    ONCE - 28 distinct modes").
  - The SELHIT coordinate trace - superseded days ago - still logs every
    click TWICE, anywhere on screen. Six generations of mechanism are live
    in the tick at once, several measured dead.
  - The whole Plugins tree (DLL, ini, LOG) sits under a OneDrive-redirected
    Documents; every synchronous fflush and profile write crosses the sync
    filter. A suspect, NOT a verdict.
  - Rebuild flags carry across ticks, so the 250ms beat can RemoveAllStrings
    a combo while its drop list is open - the empty list in the screenshot.

  THE INSTRUMENT (PerfProbe, Logger.h/.cpp + UiSpike.cpp):
  - In-memory named buckets only - the leading suspect IS file I/O, so the
    instrument must not be made of the thing it measures.
  - Brackets: log.WriteLine (the fflush itself), sel.findDlg (the full-tree
    walk), sel.open/close/notice/radio/caption, sel.scaleCombo, sel.resMode,
    sel.buildResList, sel.iniRead/iniWrite/dgWrite/commit, sel.gfxStamp,
    sel.dllStat, sel.observe + sel.btnFilter (the message paths).
  - FRAME-GAP watchdog: entry-to-entry stride; a >500ms gap with the dialog
    up logs the in-gap bucket deltas. Big gap + quiet buckets = the stall is
    OUTSIDE our bracketed code, and that would be a finding, not a failure.
  - PASS watchdog: any single pass >25ms names its in-pass contributors.
  - Table dumped on dialog close and at shutdown (SELPERF lines).

  AND THE v3.14 REWRITE'S RULE IS ALREADY PINNED: the selector becomes ONE
  pure function, SelDerive(state)->UI, and _tests\Test-SelectorDerive.py
  (NEW, ALL PASS: 16 transition rows + 6 swept invariants) was written first
  as its specification. Key design: the player's scale pick is a REQUEST
  that is never overwritten - the EFFECTIVE row derives fresh each pass as
  "request if usable else Auto", so bounce AND un-bounce need no state
  machine (closes the audit finding that the bounce never undoes). Commit
  writes only keys whose values changed; an untouched visit writes nothing.

  LEDGER GAP NOTED: entries v3.3.0..v3.13.1 exist only as git commit
  messages - the ledger habit broke during the selector sprint. Backfill is
  owned by the queued production-scrub task.

  Files: src\Logger.{h,cpp}, src\UiSpike.{h,cpp},
  src\SC4UIScaleDllDirector.cpp, _tests\Test-SelectorDerive.py (new).

2026-08-19  v3.2.3   ⭐ CHOOSING 1x LEFT THE 2x ART ARMED, AND THE STOCK-TIER
                     SELECTOR WAS STASHED IN THE ONE STATE IT EXISTS FOR

  Two defects, one build apart, and they are the same mistake wearing
  different clothes: boot work attached to a neighbouring call whose gate did
  not match the condition the work actually depends on.

  ⛔ 1. PICKING 1x BROKE THE WHOLE UI (user-reported: "1X didn't work the game
  loaded with every broken"). The static-layer sync was gated on
  `spikeAutoScale || tierActive`, and BOTH are false for a manual stock
  factor - the auto path is off and the "factor <= 1.01" block has already
  forced tierActive false. So the sync never ran, THE PREVIOUS TIER'S ART DATS
  STAYED ARMED, and geometry ran at 1x against 2x art. Every widget wrong.

  The log named it two lines apart and nobody read them together:
      Factor 1.00 is stock: every scaling subsystem forced OFF ...
      AutoScale off (manual): ScaleFactor 1.00, static layers untouched
  "Forced OFF" and "untouched" look like agreement and are the opposite:
  the first turns the geometry off, the second leaves the ART on.

  Cure: capture the ini's OWN ScaleAll before anything forces it
  (`iniWantsScaling`) and add it to the gate. SyncStaticLayers(1.0) is
  disable-all by its own contract, so a deliberate 1x now stashes every
  package - which is exactly what the user said picking 1x should mean:
  "deload everything except the selection box". The ScaleAll=0 rig keeps its
  untouched behaviour, because that is the question iniWantsScaling actually
  asks. The old gate could not tell "the user asked for stock" from "the rig
  is dormant"; the ini's own ScaleAll is what separates them.

  ⛔ 2. THE STOCK-TIER SELECTOR PACKAGE WAS UNREACHABLE. v3.2.0 armed
  z_SC4UIScale_SelectorUI-1x from inside ScaleTier::SyncStaticLayers - which
  is not called at the stock tier, the ONLY state that package exists for.
  Measured on the user's 1x machine: the dat sat as .x1-disabled, this DLL
  logged that the selector "IS serviced", and Graphic Options had no selector
  in it. The code half ran, the data half was stashed, and the log looked
  healthy. The one-way door the package was built to prevent was live.

  Cure: extracted to ScaleTier::SyncSelectorPackage(bool stockTier), called
  UNCONDITIONALLY from the director and gated on nothing but !tierActive.

  ⭐ THIS SHAPE HAS NOW SHIPPED FOUR TIMES FROM THIS ONE FUNCTION. #149 and
  #182 are recorded in comments AT THE CALL SITE, in these exact terms -
  "it used to ride inside SyncStaticLayers and therefore inherited that
  function's AutoScale gate". I read that comment while adding the third and
  fourth instances beside it. A COMMENT DID NOT STOP IT; a gate can.

  NEW GATE: _tests\Test-StockTierContract.py, a source-shape gate over the
  director and ScaleTier asserting four things - the selector is armed OUTSIDE
  SyncStaticLayers, the call is unconditional, it is gated on !tierActive and
  nothing else, and the static-layer guard carries a term true for a manual
  stock factor (captured before anything forces it).

  ⭐ AND ITS OWN NEGATIVE CONTROL FOUND IT BLIND. Six damaged copies were fed
  to it; the first version CAUGHT FIVE AND MISSED THE MOST IMPORTANT ONE - the
  original defect - because the guard walk stopped at the block's opening
  brace, one line before the `if` it was looking for. A GATE THAT PASSES ON
  THE FIXED CODE PROVES NOTHING UNTIL IT FAILS ON THE BROKEN CODE. All six
  controls trip now, including both real defects.

  Files: src\SC4UIScaleDllDirector.cpp, src\ScaleTier.{h,cpp},
  _tests\Test-StockTierContract.py (new).

2026-08-19  v3.2.2   The combo IS the readout; refusal bounces to Auto; a
                     manual tier that no longer fits is RESCUED at boot

  v3.2.1 worked - the log shows a 1.5x commit, a correct refusal of 3x at
  2400x1600, and an Auto commit - and four things came out of looking at it.

  1. ONE CONTROL, NOT TWO. The combo moves onto the readout row itself
  (293,325,465,346), and the #192 readout label and the separate "UI Scale"
  caption are RETIRED. The DLL writes the ACTIVE row as "1.5x @ 2400x1600", so
  the closed control shows exactly what the label used to say and opening it
  changes it. Two widgets displaying one fact is how they drift apart.

  The row still has to be that one: the parent GZWinBMP is (15,37,479,393) so
  children clip at height 356, and [325,346) is the last full 21px row that
  fits - the same measurement that explains #192's clipped second row.

  ⚠ The idempotence guard had to move with it. It tested for the readout
  label's id, which no longer exists, so a second pass would have injected a
  duplicate radio and combo. AN IDEMPOTENCE CHECK THAT NAMES A RETIRED NODE IS
  NOT IDEMPOTENT, IT IS OFF. Now keyed on the combo.

  2. COLOURS ARE THE DIALOG'S OWN. fillcolor (221,238,238) - the fill this
  same script's restart-notice panel uses - and forecolor (63,73,103), which
  every label in the dialog carries. The inherited white-field/black-text pair
  is a BUDGET-DIALOG look and read as foreign here.

  3. A REFUSED TIER BOUNCES TO AUTO (user direction), not to the previous row.
  Auto is the only row that always fits by construction, and it answers what
  the player was reaching for. It COMMITS Auto too: a bounce that only moved
  the highlight would leave the ini holding the old value while the control
  read "Auto" - the control would be lying, which is the one thing it must
  never do.

  4. ⭐ A MANUAL TIER THAT NO LONGER FITS THE SCREEN IS RESCUED AT BOOT.
  User-raised, and it is the worst failure this feature can produce: pick 3x
  on a large display, then run on a smaller one - a laptop, a changed
  resolution, a different monitor - and the UI is scaled past the screen.
  Graphic Options is 558 design px tall, so at 3x it needs 1674 and does not
  fit, which means THE CONTROL THAT WOULD FIX IT IS THE FIRST THING TO GO
  OFF-SCREEN. The player is trapped in a UI they cannot navigate.

  The manual factor is now checked against the render resolution with the same
  ScaleTier::Fits the auto path uses, and a factor that does not fit falls
  back to Auto - which by construction picks the largest tier that does. It
  WRITES AutoScale=1 BACK: a silent per-launch override would leave the ini
  saying 3 while the game ran at 1.5 and the selector showed 1.5, three
  sources of truth with two of them wrong.

  ⚠ ONLY WHEN A RESOLUTION WAS ACTUALLY READ. Fits() answers false for a zero
  size, so an unreadable SC4GraphicsOptions.ini would otherwise have "rescued"
  a good manual tier all the way down to stock. NULL IS NOT EVIDENCE: no
  number, no rescue. Desk-checked over seven cases including manual 1x (a
  reference capture must never be rescued) and the zero-resolution case.

  INSTRUMENT, NOT YET ARMED: the restart notice. The user reports the stock
  box appears only AFTER Accept, and we still cannot see Accept - no message
  carries a control id. Rather than guess a third time, this build captures
  the Accept/Cancel absolute rects on open (SafeAbsRect) and tests every click
  coordinate against them (SELHIT), and reports every visibility transition of
  the notice itself (SELNOTICE) - because a rise we did not cause IS the
  Accept signal we lack, and it would be exact rather than inferred. One
  Accept click settles which mechanism to arm.

  ⚠ The first capture spent 96 of its 120 lines on three hover message types
  that repeat on every mouse move. A BOUNDED INSTRUMENT CAN FILL WITH NOISE
  AND MISS THE EVENT IT WAS ARMED FOR - the same shape as a grep that filters
  out the adjacent line holding the answer. Those three types are now excluded.

  Files: tools\dialog-static\build_dialog_static.py,
  tools\dialog-static\build_selector_1x.py, src\UiSpike.cpp,
  src\SC4UIScaleDllDirector.cpp.

2026-08-19  v3.2.1   Selector: opaque drop list, unusable tiers refused, and
                     the commit path the INSTRUMENT actually supported

  Three corrections to v3.2.0, all from one eyes-on pass.

  1. THE DROP LIST RENDERED IN BANDS. The combo node was cloned from the stock
  combos in I-e9263de5, which carry `transparent` and pbufftrans=yes - and
  those are wrong HERE. The class draws its drop list as an overlay, so a
  transparent paint buffer composites whatever the list happens to cover; ours
  opens over the Renderer label and a divider, and picked both up. The stock
  ones get away with it because they open over flat dialog fill.
  ⭐ LAW: CLONING A NODE CLONES ITS ASSUMPTIONS ABOUT WHAT IS BEHIND IT. A
  transparency flag is not a property of the widget, it is a claim about its
  background, and the claim does not travel with the copy.

  2. A TIER THE RESOLUTION CANNOT CARRY IS NOW REFUSED, not silently ignored.
  At 2400x1600 the fit rule gives 3x 880*3 = 2640 > 2400, so picking it would
  have written ScaleFactor=3 and come back at stock next launch with no
  explanation. Rows the screen cannot carry now read "3x - needs 2640x1674"
  and snap back if selected, saying so in the log.

  THE RULE IS SHARED, NOT COPIED: ScaleTier::Fits is extracted out of Decide
  and called by both, so the answer shown to the player is the answer the next
  boot will give. A second copy of 880/558/800/600 in UiSpike would have been
  a second rule, and this one is displayed as a promise. Fits() also answers
  the Auto/1x rows as always-available, which is true by construction - stock
  needs no package and no room.

  3. ⭐ THE COMMIT PATH COULD NEVER HAVE FIRED, AND ONLY THE INSTRUMENT KNEW.
  v3.2.0 staged the choice and committed when a message carrying the Accept
  button's id (0xEA57DA59) reached the chained winproc. The SELMSG log from
  the first run settles it: 120 messages captured while the dialog was open
  and the player worked the combo, and NOT ONE carried a control id in any
  data slot. Every message was one of three shapes -
    type 0x0000000D   d1=x  d2=y            (a mouse coordinate pair)
    type 0xA2BF8AD4   d1=(x<<16|y)          (the same, packed)
    type 0xA2BF8ACD/CE/CF  d1=<cIGZWin*>    (one repeated window pointer)
  so the Accept/Cancel test was dead code that looked live. This is the whole
  reason the instrument shipped in the same build as the feature.

  Commit moved to the SELECTION CHANGE, which is observable by polling
  GetSelection - the mechanism already used to notice the change at all. That
  is safe here specifically because the tier applies at the next launch: the
  write changes nothing in the running game and picking another row overwrites
  it. The cost is that Cancel does not revert this one setting. The winproc
  chain STAYS as an instrument: if a message ever does carry one of those ids
  the log will say so, and real Accept/Cancel semantics become available
  without guessing again.

  ⭐ LAW: A HANDLER KEYED ON A FIELD THE MESSAGE DOES NOT CARRY IS INDISTIN-
  GUISHABLE FROM A WORKING ONE. It compiles, it runs, it is reached, and it
  does nothing - the same signature as the four patches that provably executed
  and moved zero pixels. Ship the instrument WITH the mechanism, not after it.

  Files: tools\dialog-static\build_dialog_static.py, src\UiSpike.cpp,
  src\ScaleTier.{h,cpp}.

2026-08-19  v3.2.0   IN-GAME SCALE SELECTOR in Graphic Options (USER REQUEST)

  The scale is now changeable from inside the game. Graphic Options gains a
  radio next to our resolution/scale readout - lit whenever the resolution
  came from SC4GraphicsOptions.ini rather than the game's own four-item list,
  which is the state every scaled tier runs in - and a UI Scale picker
  offering Auto / 1x / 1.5x / 2x / 3x.

  IT APPLIES ON RESTART, and that is not a shortcut. Switching tier renames
  nine dat packages AND the FontStyle.ini the game probes once at startup, so
  nothing short of a relaunch can move it. This dialog already carries the
  game's own notice that its settings take effect next launch, which is why
  the control belongs here and nowhere else.

  DATA HALF (build_dialog_static.py, inject_res_readout): three more nodes
  beside the #192 readout - radio 0x5CA1E002, label 0x5CA1E003, combo
  0x5CA1E004 - all with EMPTY captions, the same honest-blank contract as
  #192: if the code half is missing the player sees blank space, never a
  stale or invented number.

  WHERE THEY FIT was measured, not chosen. The parent GZWinBMP is
  (15,37,479,393), so children clip at height 356; the resolution list is
  full (four rows ending y=351) and the readout row [325,346) is the last row
  that fits below Software - which is why #192's second row was clipped. The
  band x>=266, y=211..253 is the only free space left inside that clip. The
  combo's drop list opens downward from y=253 and five ~20px rows reach ~353,
  inside 356. All four nodes scale exactly by the factor at 1.5x / 2x / 3x
  (verified out of the built dats).

  CODE HALF (UiSpike::ServiceScaleSelector): fills the captions, derives the
  radio state from the four stock resolution radios rather than tracking it,
  pushes the live tier into the combo when the dialog opens, and chains a
  cIGZWinProc onto the dialog root (a GZWinGen) to see the clicks. CHAINS,
  never replaces: the game's own Accept/Cancel/radio logic lives behind
  GetWinProc() and dropping it would break the dialog outright. Accept
  commits, Cancel discards, and the commit writes the same two keys to the
  same ini Set-Tier.ps1 writes, through WritePrivateProfileStringW - which
  never emits a BOM, the one thing that must stay true of that file.

  MOVED OUT OF IncrementalPass. The #192 readout lived there and could not
  stay: that function needs a city view and `continuous`, so the dialog went
  unserviced on the main menu and at the stock tier. The selector is driven
  straight from the WM_TIMER instead, self-throttled to 250ms.

  ⭐ 1x WAS A ONE-WAY DOOR. At the stock tier the DLL deliberately installs
  nothing and stashes every package - correct, and it would also have stashed
  the one control that lets a player leave 1x. Two changes make the tier
  escapable: a new single-entry package z_SC4UIScale_SelectorUI-1x.dat
  (Graphic Options at STOCK geometry with the four nodes, built by
  tools\dialog-static\build_selector_1x.py, which asserts the rects are the
  authored ones and that the package holds exactly one entry), and a tick
  source at stock tier that runs the selector and nothing else.

  The two ways of ARRIVING at 1x want opposite things, and now say so:
  SelectorAtStock=1 (default) keeps the selector; Set-Tier.ps1 -Tier 1 - the
  MEASUREMENT path, which exists to produce a true stock reference - writes 0
  and restores the absolute isolation. Without that split, taking one
  reference capture would have silently removed the control from every later
  1x session.

  ⭐ FIXED IN PASSING: DialogStatic COULD NOT BE REBUILT AT ALL. Adding
  6a9455c9 to TARGETS for #191 put a script this builder doubles in front of
  {1abe787d,ea32f100}, a code-bound TGI selective-safe leaves untouched - and
  the cross-builder guard FATALed on every factor. The shipped dats predated
  it, so every gate was green and nothing on screen was wrong; the next
  rebuild would simply have failed.

  The guard was refusing the wrong thing. Its own stated reason is about
  OVERWRITING the payload the code binds - "a code consumer is invisible to a
  .UI-only judgement" - which holds for an in-place override and collapses
  for a CLONE: a clone emits a new TGI and retargets only the copies of
  scripts this builder doubles, so the code binding and every referrer we do
  not double keep the 1x art byte for byte. Narrowed to in-place, with the
  permission MEASURED at the emit rather than assumed: an assertion over the
  payloads actually staged FATALs if a TGI permitted as a clone is also
  written in place. A per-occurrence exception entry was the alternative and
  would have needed hand-adding every time a target is added.

  Files: tools\dialog-static\build_dialog_static.py (injection + guard),
  tools\dialog-static\build_selector_1x.py (new), src\UiSpike.cpp,
  src\SC4UIScaleDllDirector.cpp, src\ScaleTier.cpp, src\Settings.{h,cpp},
  _packaging\SC4UIScale.ini, _tests\Set-Tier.ps1,
  _tests\Deploy-OnGameClose.ps1, _tests\Test-DatIntegrity.ps1.

2026-08-19  v3.1.2   #195 CLOSED - the deployment counts, and a 1.5x-only
                     rounding fault behind them (USER-CONFIRMED)

  The count under each dispatch hat is a TEXT-category indicator sized
  asymmetrically: width from the MEASURED text, height a hard-coded 14.0f at
  0x0046CB09. The width follows the font, which our FontStyle table scales, so
  at 2x the glyphs were twice as tall inside a quad still 14 high and the
  number vanished. Scaling that height brought the counts back at 2x.

  1.5x STILL FAILED, and the A/B is what found it: with MissionBubbleFx=1 (all
  CSI exe patches off) the number came back at 1.5x; with them on it did not;
  1x and 2x were unaffected either way. 1.5x is the ONLY tier that makes these
  screen-pixel constants fractional - 43*1.5 = 64.5 and 21*1.5 = 31.5 - and the
  raw product was being stored. Now rounded with the project's one convention
  (RoundHalfUp, law 89), which is a provable no-op at 2x and 3x because every
  stock times 2 or 3 is already whole.

  ALSO FIXED, and it had made an earlier change silently inert: the verify and
  write loops read `k < 4` while the constant table had grown to five, so the
  fifth entry was never checked and never written. The count is now derived
  from the array. A hand-typed loop bound beside a table that grows is the same
  hand-list rot this project has booked before.

  The fifth entry is the .data twin of the centring offset. A float sweep of
  the drawer showed it read from TWO addresses - [0x00A88268] in .rdata (which
  we scaled) and [0x00B07F80] in .data, read three times as often (which we did
  not). Scaling one copy and not the other puts two derivations of the same
  quantity out of step; the original sweep was scoped to .rdata and was a
  filtered null in the same family as "a .rdata sweep is blind to inline
  immediates".

2026-08-19  v3.1.1   RESIDENT MARKER PORTRAIT - the default sim faces were
                     never staged (USER-CONFIRMED at 2x)

  After Move In a Sim, the "<name> lives here" purple circle showed some sims
  correctly and others magnified to their top-left corner. The differential -
  two residents, one screenshot, same build and tier - is what solved it.

  The category-3 indicator divides its icon UVs by ONE power-of-two texture
  side (0x0046CCCE). v3.1.0 staged the 19 named portraits at 36x41*f, so those
  upload into a 128 square at 2x/3x - but there are 42 36x41 faces in the
  portrait groups, and the other four (EA32F100 / EA32F101, the DEFAULT faces)
  were not staged. Those still uploaded into a 64 square, so no single divisor
  could be right for both and the fallback sims drew magnified.

  Fix: stage the default faces too. Both 46A006B0 members now ship at
  54x62 / 72x82 / 108x123 - that is the group the indicator composes
  (0x0046CB7B). The 1ABE787D default and one ea32f101 the builder refused (an
  UNSCALED-only .UI reference, a script consumer that must stay 1x) are off the
  indicator path.

  LAW: if a consumer divides by ONE texture size, every source it can bind must
  be the SAME size. v3.1.0 staged a SET where the contract was a FAMILY.

  SelectiveArt 693 -> 696 entries.

2026-08-19  v3.1.0   #191 CLOSED - THE MOVE IN MY SIM MARKER
                     (USER-CONFIRMED at 2x, 2400x1600)
            Also: #193 CLOSED, #190 CLOSED, the 1x baseline made genuinely
            inert, and the tier/deploy tooling reduced to one command.

  #191 - the framed sim face + green arrow that floats over a candidate house
  when you click Move In a Sim. It rendered pixel-identical at every tier while
  the rest of the UI scaled.

  TWO HALVES, BOTH REQUIRED:
    - It is a GZWin PAIR, 0x27DF05BE (green, on the house) and 0x27DF05BF (red,
      follows the mouse), each with a 46x97 plate and a 36x41 portrait child.
      The sweep was ALWAYS resizing them (46x97 -> 92x194) - but both visible
      parts are GZWinBMPs, which draw dst = src, and neither root was in
      kBmpxCityRoots. The size was never wrong; the BLIT was not following it.
    - The tool latches [ctrl+0x44]=GetH() and [ctrl+0x48]=GetW()/2 AT INIT and
      never refreshes them, then re-places the window every frame with those
      offsets. So a post-hoc position fix is overwritten on the next tick, and
      the only cure is to get the right number INTO the latch: I-6a9455c9 is
      now data-scaled in DialogStatic so the marker is BORN at the tier size,
      with both roots in kNeverScaleIds so the sweep cannot double it.

  Shipped geometry, verified in the package bytes: root 69x145 / 92x194 /
  138x291 and portrait 54x61 / 72x82 / 108x123 at 1.5x / 2x / 3x
  (stock 46x97 / 36x41).

  #193 - the right-drag anchor ring was 32*f*f. 0x48E945B4 had been added to
  kAlwaysScaleCityIds (the FORCE-scale list) by an edit that meant to add it to
  kNeverScaleIds; the anchor string was unique but sat in the wrong array. The
  cure is a size-only refusal in ScalePanelRoot. That id is the right-drag ring,
  NOT the "U-Drive-It mission marker" our own comment called it - the mislabel
  is why earlier audits cleared the wrong widget.

  #190 - the 19 runtime Sim portraits are code-bound (TGI composed at runtime),
  so the .UI-derived ref-map was structurally blind to them. Staged in place at
  36x41*f in every tier package, plus no-snap entries so the 1.5x tier does not
  crop 6px off every face.

  1x BASELINE - it was never inert. gTierF defaulted to 2.0f and was assigned at
  only two sites (both scaling paths) while read at 97, so at tier 1 every
  consumer behaved as if 2x; and the "Tier 1 = TRUE stock" block sat inside the
  AutoScale branch, so a manual 1x ran the whole sweep. Both fixed - factor 1
  now forces every subsystem off regardless of how the factor was chosen.

  DEPLOY/TIER TOOLING - Set-Tier.ps1 now does tier AND screen in one call
  (packages, font, SC4GraphicsOptions.ini and dgVoodoo.conf, no BOM). Two new
  gates: armed-tier agreement across tier-managed families, and drift between
  the deploy's dependency-gate list and ScaleTier.cpp.

2026-08-18  v3.0.38  #188 CLOSED - THE OFFER BALLOON IS A CSI: TWO QUADS,
                     BOTH SIZED BY INLINE .text IMMEDIATES
                     (USER-CONFIRMED at 3x, 3840x2160)

  DLL: the U-Drive-It offer balloon is a CITY SITUATION INDICATOR - category 4
  of the dispatch-indicator system - drawn by cSC4DispatchVehicleView::Draw at
  0x0046D990. The drawer was identified by SUPPRESSION, not by scaling:
  v3.0.35 hooked it and returned without calling the original, and the user
  reported "THEY'RE GONE". That is the first POSITIVE identification in the
  whole hunt; the eleven subsystems before it were all closed by elimination.

  TWO QUADS, and that is the trap - every single-number fix produced a
  half-result for a day:
    - PIN / BACKING, 64x64: eight +/-32.0f immediates at 0x0046EABD,
      0x0046EACA, 0x0046EAF6, 0x0046EB01, 0x0046EB2D, 0x0046EB38, 0x0046EB64,
      0x0046EB6F (C7 84 24 ..., imm at instruction+7).
    - ICON + CLICK BOX, 35x35: mov eax,0x420C0000 at 0x0046CC47 (imm at
      0x0046CC48), inside the CSI-only branch cmp [esi+4],4 at 0x0046CC41 in
      the billboard builder 0x0046C8B0 (reached from AddIndicator 0x0046F240
      at 0x0046F616). Stored to the indicator record's width AND height
      (+0xD0/+0xD4), which Draw halves to +/-17.5 - so the tap target and the
      art cannot drift apart, matching the user's own observation that "only
      the inner glyph is clickable so it's the actual click box".
  ⛔ 0x0046CCB9 is the SAME instruction shape holding 32.0f on the NON-CSI
  branch (category 3). NEVER patch it - it would resize unrelated indicators.
  It is deliberately absent from kCsiQuad.

  WHY THIS COST ROUGHLY SEVENTEEN LAUNCHES OVER TWO DAYS. Both levers are
  INLINE IMMEDIATES IN .text, so every .rdata constant sweep was a FILTERED
  null by construction (law 99) - that is what made four earlier attributions
  look plausible and every "no change" look like "wrong object". The 42.0f at
  0x00A8819C is not a size at all: it reaches the quad TRANSLATION (drawn
  centre = x0 + 42/2), so v3.0.34 scaled it 42->63, the log proved the write
  landed, the balloon moved ~10px and nothing resized - a constant can be LIVE
  and still be the WRONG constant (law 103).

  SHIPPED IN CODE, not behind an ini knob: ApplyCsiIndicatorScale
  (src\CodePatches.cpp), mode >= 2, tier-general, verify-before-write and
  BOTH-OR-NEITHER across all nine immediates - the pin scaled with the icon
  left at 1x is the exact broken state this task spent a day inside. The
  CsiAim re-aim list stays alongside it and is inert unless [UiSpike]CsiAim is
  set. Log at the confirming run: "CSI offer balloon x3.00 - icon+hitbox 35.0
  -> 105.0 px, pin quad 64 -> 192 px (9 immediates, all in .text)".

  ART: the eight icon strips (type 0x856DDBAC, 152x38 = four 38x38 states;
  each exists twice, in groups 0x46A006B0 and 0x1ABE787D, 16 entries in all)
  generated at 1.5x/2x/3x as z_SC4UIScale_CsiIcons-{15x,2x,3x}.dat and wired
  into Deploy-OnGameClose.ps1, which Build-Dist parses - so the release bundle
  picks them up. The hand-placed z_SC4UIScale_CsiIcons.dat was DELETED from
  Plugins: an unmanaged file is the exact rot the manifest law exists to stop.

  USER-CONFIRMED at 3x / 3840x2160 - "I think we got it!": disc, glyph, pin
  and pole all proportional, with the car and helicopter icons crisp 3x art
  rather than an upscaled 38px original.

  THE SHARED-PATH BLAST RADIUS, closed the same day. The eight pin immediates
  are NOT category-guarded: cmp [esi+4],4 ; je 0x46E38E at 0x0046E852 sends
  CSI down its own branch, which rejoins the common code, so other
  dispatch-indicator categories reach that quad. Neither the 1.5x nor the 3x
  eyes-on had a fire or police marker on screen. The user then confirmed a
  dispatch marker renders correctly at 1.5x, so the contingency - splitting
  kCsiQuad so the disc scales alone - is not needed and stays unbuilt (and
  must never be applied "to be safe": pin-without-disc is the broken state).

  ⛔ THIS SUPERSEDES THE v3.0.7 ENTRY BELOW, which attributed the balloon to
  an occupant marker (0xCB79919B) and a strip builder (0x5F5FB0). That entry
  is marked in place, not rewritten.

  v3.0.25-v3.0.37 are not individually ledgered: they are the elimination run
  that got here - the marker's own per-object SetSize (which HUNG the game and
  was disarmed in v3.0.26), the TagKind sprite spine closed with a control,
  the renderer's view-object channel enumerated to 13 live objects with the
  balloon in none of them, the 42.0f family, the suppression test that named
  the drawer, and the two-colour art tracer that came back RED - proving our
  DBPF override wins and the drawn size is computed in code, so art could
  never carry it. Full detail in _tests\REGRESSION.md, 2026-08-17/18.

  DLL: the offer balloon is a MARKER attachment (occupant marker type
  0xCB79919B) whose billboard strip is CODE-GENERATED by builder 0x5F5FB0
  - not a window, effect, S3D model, or signpost quad. Its size at every
  zoom = pixel dimensions (64px disc + icons + margins) x the per-zoom
  float table at .rdata 0xAA523C {0.5,0.75,1.0,1.5,2.0} - a model that
  PREDICTS the measured 45-48px balloon exactly (0.75 x 64 = 48 at zoom
  2), the signature the four earlier misses lacked. Sole consumer
  byte-proven. ApplyMarkerZoomScale multiplies the table by the tier
  factor; the renderer pick tests the builder's own verts, so the click
  target grows with the visual. Dispatch markers share the builder and
  co-scale (intended). SPPROBE gained the strip-builder hook (SPSTRIP).

2026-08-17  v3.0.5  THE BALLOON FOUND: SIGNPOST 44px QUAD SCALED (#188)

  DLL: v3.0.4's hook was aimed at the wrong visual - its own log proved it
  (armed install line, ZERO spawns, two balloons on screen). The blue
  offer balloon is a cSC4SignPOST occupant (clsid 0xAB72FBB3): a
  camera-facing billboard quad drawn at a HARDCODED 44.0f SCREEN PIXELS
  (push 44.0f at 0x5F20AF into the px->world helper; push 150.0f at
  0x5F20BF = the pole raise) - pixel-fixed at every zoom AND resolution,
  which is exactly why it shrank physically at the scaled tiers. Cure:
  both imms scaled by the tier factor (66/225 px at 1.5x), verify-before-
  write both-or-neither, single-page-span VirtualProtect. The UDI click is
  a renderer ray-pick against the DRAWN quad (the pick filter accepts the
  signpost occupant at 0x4B8947), so the bigger balloon IS the bigger
  click target. SHARED: police/fire dispatch lollipop signs draw through
  the same quad and co-scale (also pixel-fixed markers; eyes-on
  adjudicates). Review (opus; second vendor suspended): the balloon patch
  was moved OUT from behind the hook's unrelated prologue gate (finding
  1), the nested VirtualProtect RWX page leak fixed here AND in its
  pre-existing cost-box twin (finding 2), and the MissionBubbleScale ini
  float clamped to (1,8] so inf can never become a code imm (finding 3).
  Known first-pass tradeoff: the balloon texture is composed at 52px
  cells, so the bigger quad is mildly soft - the crisp art companion
  (tier PNG overrides + four composition imms) is a follow-up if wanted.

2026-08-17  v3.0.4  U-DRIVE-IT START BUBBLES SCALED AT SPAWN (#188)

  DLL: the offer bubbles you click to start a U-Drive-It mission are not
  windows and not our art - they are the mission_selection_* SWARM EFFECTS,
  spawned BY NAME (CreateEffectByName 0x5939B0) by the mission manager,
  with NO size constant in the exe and their size defined in the EFFDIR
  resource. A full-resource EFFDIR override carrying an unmissable 3x scale
  was proven INERT from BOTH plugin trees (two control launches): the
  effects service loads the directory ONCE at app-service init with
  first-provider-wins exact-TGI resolution, before either plugin tree can
  matter - the data route is closed. Cure: a MinHook detour on
  CreateEffectByName writes the tier factor into the returned instance's
  transform block (+0x110 scale, flag +0xDD), which the activation math
  provably multiplies into every child spawn (fmul at 0x591FEA). Name
  prefix covers all 18 variants incl. _shrink despawns; the pick-side needs
  nothing (the offer control's hit test is a ray-pick against the DRAWN
  geometry - slot 65 on cISC43DRender, no radius constant - so the bigger
  visual is a bigger click target by construction). Ini: MissionBubbleFx
  (0 off / 1 log / 2 fix, default 2, rides ScaleAll) + MissionBubbleScale
  (<=0 follow tier; >0 literal, the no-rebuild tuning knob). Reviewed
  (opus; second vendor suspended): detour hardened to check the success
  flag and name BEFORE dereferencing the out-slot, pristine-state gate
  (ctor state: scale 1.0, flags 0) refuses unknown instances and logs
  refusals uncapped.

2026-08-17  v3.0.2  MANUAL TIERS NOW SYNC THE STATIC LAYERS (#182)

  DLL only, one condition. SyncStaticLayers - the package/font tier gating -
  ran ONLY under AutoScale; manual ScaleFactor (a supported setting, and this
  rig's daily state) never gated a package. Harmless while every package
  predated the last tier switch; WRONG the moment a new package deployed:
  UncoveredIcons landed 2x-active by deploy pattern, no boot ever swapped it,

  2x strip served at 1.5x - Notre-Dame's icon shifted right with a wrong hover
  (user-reported). ZERO ScaleTier lines in the whole session log was the proof.
