  gated on HOW the factor was chosen instead of WHAT the factor is. Gate is now
  (AutoScale || tierActive); manual+ScaleAll=0 keeps the untouched behaviour
  (Set-StockCompare owns dormant rigs with its own suffixes).

2026-08-16  v3.0.1  MAYOR RATING BAR: THE SETIMAGE CROP LATCH IS NOW CARRIED
                     ACROSS OUR RESIZE (#176 mechanism found + RELATCH fix)

  DLL only. THE MECHANISM, byte-verified: the HUD rating handler (sub_7E8510)
  does not draw the .UI sheet - it COMPOSES a bitmap (one filmstrip row of
  {46a006b0,14015549}, row = artH*(rating+100)/200, replicated to every row)
  and pushes it through cIGZWinBMP::SetImage on EVERY rating tick. SetImage
  (0x9BC57E -> 0x9BC447) rewrites the live imagerect [win+0xE8] to
  (0,0,min(areaW,imgW),min(areaH,imgH)) FROM THE WINDOW'S AREA AT THAT
  MOMENT; GZWinBMP::SetArea never touches it; the draw (0x9BC325) is
  dst-follows-src. The handler's first bind lands ~0.8s BEFORE our city
  sweep, so the crop latches at 102x11 and the enlarged 153x17 groove draws
  102x11 content - green fill 6 rows short of the bottom, right third (the
  green ticks) missing - until a sim rating tick (~once per sim month)
  happens to re-run SetImage. The tier split was NEVER real: 61 captures
  show the same single pre-sweep firing at every tier; sessions that looked
  right (the whole good month, both 2x checks today) were sessions where a
  tick landed before the eyes did. 2x's historical "half bar" was this same
  latch: 102/204 = exactly half. No constant in the fill chain is 1x pixel
  data - the only wrong number was the latch.

  THE FIX: ScaleSubtree's resize now re-derives a latch-following crop.
  Guard = the latch's own signature (crop == (0,0,oldW,oldH) exactly), so a
  deliberate sub-crop or a staged pre-scaled crop can never match (law 94:
  keyed on the derived condition, not an id list). The rewrite mirrors
  0x9BC447's min() clamp verbatim - no arithmetic of ours. Deliberately
  tier-general: the same stale latch fires at 2x/3x whenever no post-sweep
  tick lands. Log line: "RELATCH id=... imagerect ... (#176)".

  Dead attributions closed with it (all measured, none re-runnable): the
  #175 smoothing, the CellUnit snap, cSC4WinAuraBar tiling (wrong widget),
  "imul 7 is the segment pitch" (arrows only), "window/crop wrong at 1.5x"
  (both correct live), the entire DLL delta v3.0.0->today, and the art -
  the 1.5x fill raster is pixel-identical from v2.85.0 through v3.0.0.

2026-08-15  v3.0.0  CUSTOM THIRD-PARTY MENU ICONS ARE HANDLED AUTOMATICALLY
                     (#149) - USER-CONFIRMED at 2x and 1.5x on four real
                     Simtropolis downloads

  DLL + a new tool. THE PROBLEM: this mod scales the menu strip's state cell,
  so ANY custom lot a player installs after our art packages were built drew
  its icon doubled at rest and blank on hover. Not "unimproved" - BROKEN, and
  broken by us. Stock control 2026-08-14 confirmed it: our layer off, one icon
  visible on hover; our layer on, two icons that vanish.

  THE CURE, and it costs the player nothing. At boot the DLL indexes every DBPF
  under Plugins (index reads only, ~50-190ms), diffs the ItemIcon TGIs against
  the ones our own packages supply, and enlarges whatever is left over BEFORE
  any menu asks for it - via the resource cache, and via a wrap on the resource
  factory for consumers that mint their own copy. A well-formed third-party
  icon therefore needs NO download, NO extra package and NO action.

  Confirmed automatic on Binface Billboard and DBSSY Notre-Dame de Paris, the
  latter at BOTH 2x and 1.5x:
      BORN CORRECT (in place) {D6482A2C} 176x44 -> 264x66 (cell 44 -> 66 at f=1.50)
      VERIFY 0/24 sampled pixels WRONG

  FOR MALFORMED ART there is an offline escape hatch,
  tools\itemicons\build_uncovered_icons.py, which rediscovers the uncovered set
  from the player's own Plugins tree and rebuilds a real override package. It is
  needed only when a mod's own strip is broken - e.g. Lighted Palm Plaza, whose
  four states sit at a FRACTIONAL ~45.36px pitch while the engine reads
  imageWidth/4 = 44, and which ships no hover border at all.

  NEW GATE: tools\uimap\emu\sim_itemicon_states.py reproduces the engine's own
  crop - SRC = (state*stride, 0, +stride, h) - and sweeps tier x icon x state
  over the DEPLOYED dats. States 0-2 must not move; state 3 must carry the white
  hover border (81.8%, the value measured across 450 known-good icons). The
  untouched mod art is kept as a positive control that MUST still fail.

  THIS RELEASE ALSO FIXES THREE DEFECTS THE TESTING ITSELF EXPOSED, ALL OF
  WHICH WOULD HAVE REACHED USERS:
    - the icon scan was gated on AutoScale, so AutoScale=0 (a supported user
      setting) silently disabled the entire cure;
    - the deploy hard-copied the generated package, so a CLEAN INSTALL - which
      has nothing uncovered and therefore no package - failed the deploy;
    - Test-DatIntegrity asserted a fixed icon count, turning a correct rebuild
      red the moment a player adds one lot.

2026-08-14  v2.99.0  CELL-ALIGNED SAMPLING FOR STATE STRIPS, SCOPED BY A
                     DERIVED LIST (#156) - USER-CONFIRMED "lines are gone,
                     region screen is clean"

  Data + DLL. USER-REPORTED: three bright slivers at the right-hand end of the
  region bubble's three population rows, at 1.5x only.

  CONFIRMED ON THREE POINTS BEFORE ANY CODE CHANGED - stock 1024x768 with our
  layer off: CLEAN. 2x: CLEAN. 1.5x: dashed. Both controls run by the user on
  screen. That triple is what made the arithmetic below a diagnosis rather than
  a theory.

  THE MECHANISM. ScaleDim snaps the SHEET so its cell count still divides
  evenly (#143, correct, unchanged). The SAMPLER then mapped the whole sheet
  globally, sx = ox / factor. Once the snap moves the output off w*factor the
  two disagree and the CELL BOUNDARIES DRIFT:

      1x   sheet  84 wide, cell 21   states at 0 / 21 / 42 / 63
                                     ink begins exactly at 42 = state 2
      1.5x sheet 132 wide, cell 33   states at 0 / 33 / 66 / 99
           out 63 -> src 42  |
           out 64 -> src 42  |  state 2's ink, drawn INSIDE state 1
           out 65 -> src 43  |

  Three columns of the next state hard against the previous cell's right edge.
  Three rows, three slivers.

  THE FIRST CURE WAS BUILT, MEASURED AND BACKED OUT - and that is the lesson,
  not a footnote. Scoping cell-aligned sampling by CellUnit changed 1186 of
  2206 sheets and displaced an advisor frame's flood-filled aperture by a
  pixel; the #152 seat guard stopped the build. CellUnit is a HEURISTIC: any
  width divisible by 3 or 4 is treated as a strip. Safe while it only PRESERVES
  divisibility, unsafe the moment it RE-TIMES pixels. Backed out and proven
  exact at entry level - 0 of 941 entries differed.

  WHAT SHIPPED. tools\upscale\find_cell_strips.py derives the list from the
  .UI scripts that BIND each sheet:
      (a) window height == sheet height and width divides it -> states measured
          directly from the window;
      (b) a GZWinBtn binds it but the window does not measure the cell -> 4,
          the four-state divide compiled into the button blit, and only where
          the sheet divides by 4. This is the population rows' case (84x21 art
          in a 94x16 window).
  EXCLUDED: sheets a plain GZWinBMP also draws (mixed consumer - an art change
  is scoped to the whole game), and 4 sheets whose scripts disagree on the count.

      scripts read            341
      art-bound button sheets 197
      PROVEN state strips     193   (191 four-state, 2 eight-state)

  Upscale2x --cell-strips then samples those sheets PER STATE:

      factor 2   : 2206 PNGs, 0 CHANGED    <- measured, not argued
      factor 3   : 2206 PNGs, 0 CHANGED
      factor 1.5 : 2206 PNGs, 77 CHANGED   (1186 under the heuristic)

  Verified in the art: 14015586 first ink column 63 -> 66, the state-2 boundary.

  ONE GUARD RELAXED, DELIBERATELY. build_selective_safe.py G1 asserted the
  flood-filled aperture EQUALS ScaleRound(offset). Per-cell sampling scales a
  55px cell to 83 (not 82.5), so a source column legitimately lands one pixel
  later. The art is right; the equality encoded the OLD sampler's rounding.
  G1 now bounds it at +/-1px and stays FATAL beyond - still the #151 class.

  THE LAW. A HEURISTIC THAT IDENTIFIES A STRUCTURE IS SAFE FOR PROTECTING IT
  AND UNSAFE FOR REWRITING IT. Count what it fires on before promoting it from
  a guard to a transform, and get the real list from whoever actually knows -
  here, the scripts that bind the art. COROLLARY: assert the MEASUREMENT with a
  tolerance, never the MODEL; a guard encoding one sampler's rounding fires on
  every future sampler change whether or not anything is wrong.

2026-08-13  v2.98.0  THE #148 CURE LIVED IN THE RUNTIME PATH ONLY - STATIC
                     DIALOGS NEVER GOT IT (#155)

  Data + DLL. USER-REPORTED, region screen: "some more tearing the play button
  and to the right of the population" on the city bubble.

  MEASURED, from the live tree and the shipped bytes - no inference:

      RGKID 11.0    id=0x0A551C50  (1049,509 387x375)   the bubble
      RGKID 11.0.6  id=0x4A560000  (275,237 82x69)      the PLAY BUTTON

      play button art {46a006b0,14416326}  220x46 -> staged 332x69
      four-state strip, so the CELL is 332/4 = 83 wide
      the WINDOW shipped 82 wide.  83 into 82: the leftover column is the tear.

  THE CAUSE IS A CURE THAT ONLY REACHED HALF THE PIPELINE.
  #148's fix was the LEAF SIZE-DERIVED rule: a leaf has no children to keep
  flush, so its scaled size comes from its own size, not from its neighbours'
  edges. That rule was implemented in `UiSpike::ScaleSubtree` - the RUNTIME
  sweep - in v2.94.1, and `build_dialog_static.py` never got it. And a
  statically-served dialog is DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED from that sweep (kNeverScale;
  running both double-scales it, which is the Establish-City 4x), so nothing
  downstream ever repairs it.

  The same control therefore came out 83px wide at runtime and 82px in a static
  dat. TWO PATHS THAT MUST AGREE, SILENTLY DISAGREEING - and the divergence is
  invisible at 2x and 3x, where ScaleRound is exact and both rules coincide.

  THE CHANGE. `build_dialog_static.py` gained `leaf_art_sized()` +
  `scaled_area()`: a leaf that BINDS ART AND CARRIES NO `imagerect` takes
  l' = scale_len(l), r' = l' + scale_len(r-l). Position never moves; only the
  size changes, by at most a pixel.

  ⚠ SCOPE, and why it is not "every leaf" as the DLL does it. Widening it to
  TEXT leaves would move a wrap point in dialogs that are already user-confirmed
  for no defect - the blast-radius rule. Nodes WITH an `imagerect` are excluded
  too: their crop is registered against their own l,t (ENGINE 3.3 pattern 3) and
  already scales with the art, so resizing the window would break a relationship
  that is currently right.

  47 art leaves across 19 scripts moved at 1.5x. The bubble's own three:

      0x4A560000  play button   82x69 -> 83x69   (= its 83x69 cell)
      0x4A560001  small button  19x20 -> 20x20
      0x4A560003  icon          54x43 -> 54x44

  THE INTEGER NO-OP IS ASSERTED IN THE BUILDER, not assumed: for integer N,
  N*l + N*(r-l) == N*r, so the rule must land on the identical number, and the
  build STOPS if one pixel ever moves at 2x or 3x. Measured on the packed bytes:

      z_SC4UIScale_DialogStatic.dat   262 -> 262 entries   +0 -0 ~0

  THE GATE MISSED IT TWICE OVER, AND THAT IS THE PART TO REMEMBER.
  `gate_btn_undercover.py` exists precisely to assert "an art-sized button's
  scaled window == its art cell". It did not catch this because:

    1. SCOPE - it scans `selective-safe\stage-*` and had never looked at
       `dialog-static\` at all.
    2. RULE  - it MODELS the DLL's leaf rule and then reports the 1.5x residual
       instead of failing, on the stated grounds that "the parity class is
       repaired by the leaf size-derived rule". True where it looked. Not true
       where it did not.

  A gate excused a defect using a repair that does not run in the path it was
  not scanning. It now has a STATIC half that models nothing: it reads the
  shipped `area=` verbatim, asserts the builder applied its own rule, and FAILS
  at every tier - because nothing repairs a static dialog later.

      15x  460 art-sized buttons   rule violations 0   residual {(0,2):347, (0,6):3, (0,1):1, (1,0):1}
      2x   460                     rule violations 0   residual none
      3x   460                     rule violations 0   residual none

  NEGATIVE CONTROL against the REAL artefact: the bubble script extracted back
  out of the DEPLOYED v2.97.1 dat and fed to the fixed gate ->
  "FAIL - 3 art-sized button(s)", naming 0x4A560000 first.

  ⚠ A RESIDUAL IS REPORTED, NOT CLOSED, AND IT IS A DIFFERENT CAUSE.
  347 of the 460 have an art cell 2px TALLER than their window. That is not the
  edge-parity class this release fixes - it is `Upscale2x::CellUnit` snapping
  the HEIGHT of a HORIZONTAL four-state strip (a 20px-tall sheet: 20 % 4 == 0,
  so 30 became 32) although a horizontal strip has no vertical divide. The lever
  for exactly that already exists - `--height-exact-group` - and is NOT applied
  to the stock art group; doing so globally would move 791 of 2280 sheets and
  put #143's white-seam fix back in play, so it is a measured decision to make
  deliberately, not a change to slip into this release. Recorded with its exact
  shape rather than excused.

  THE LAW. WHEN A CURE LANDS IN ONE PATH, NAME EVERY OTHER PATH THAT NEEDS IT.
  The runtime sweep and the static builder scale the same windows for different
  reasons; a rule that belongs to the geometry belongs to BOTH. And a gate may
  never excuse a finding on the strength of a repair without first asserting
  that the repair RUNS in the path it is looking at.

2026-08-13  v2.97.1  CORRECTION - v2.97.0 SCALED THE WINDOW AND THE BITMAP
                     AND LEFT THE CROP BETWEEN THEM AT 1x (#154)

  ** USER-CONFIRMED on screen 2026-08-13: "perfect". **

  USER-REPORTED against v2.97.0, with a screenshot: "It's not correct when
  compared to the original." The dialog was the right size and every label read
  in full, but every coloured row stripe stopped two thirds of the way across
  its row, leaving bare panel behind it.

  THREE NUMBERS DECIDE A blttype=normal BLIT AND v2.97.0 SCALED TWO OF THEM.

      window     285 -> 428   scaled  (edge-derived, from the .UI)
      bitmap     285 -> 429   scaled  (Upscale2x, CellUnit 3)
      imagerect  285 -> 285   NOT SCALED   <- the crop between them

  The game slices `imagerect` out of the bitmap and blits that slice at the
  window origin. A 285px slice in a 428px window leaves 143px of window bare.
  MEASURED on the shipped artefact, not inferred from the screenshot: the
  deployed dat's own script still carried `imagerect=(0,0,285,30)`.

  WHY THE BUILDER SKIPPED THEM. `imagerect` is scaled only for a control whose
  art the build actually scaled, and that test reads `art_plan` - which is
  computed from the STOCK upscale preview set alone. A bitmap the MOD supplies,
  which we upscale through thirdparty-art\, is therefore classified "left1x",
  `control_art_doubled` stays False, and the rect is left alone. The mechanism
  for exactly this case already existed - RUNTIME_BOUND_2X, "the ref does not
  change but its PIXELS are scaled, so the rect must scale with them" (#55) -
  and mod-supplied art is the same statement with a different supplier. It now
  rides the same parameter rather than a parallel one that could drift.

  ⚠ SCOPED TO THE OWNING PACKAGE. A rect may only scale when the scaled bitmap
  ships in the SAME mod-gated dat as the script. Scaling a rect in the root
  DialogStatic package on the strength of art that only exists in a CAM-gated
  package would break the moment CAM is removed: the gate takes the art away
  and leaves a doubled crop behind.

  ⛔ AND THE BUILD HAD ALREADY SAID SO. Every v2.97.0 build printed

      TP-EDIT ...I-9b868f68.ui  areas=116  rects2x=0  fonts=90  refs=27

  `rects2x=0` on a file with 24 imagerects. The number was on screen, in my own
  output, and I read past it to the areas count. Law 54 in its purest form: the
  instrument reported the defect and nobody read the line.

  THIS ALSO FIXES TWO PANELS THAT HAVE BEEN WRONG SINCE v2.38.3.
  `2a554f6d` (6 rects) and `aa8b999e` (1) are CAM query panels we have been
  shipping for two weeks with 280px stripes inside 420px windows, at EVERY
  tier. Nobody had reported them; the same one-line rule repairs them.

      z_SC4UIScale_DialogStatic.dat   262 -> 262 entries   +0  -0  ~0
      z_SC4UIScale_SaveWarningUI.dat    2 ->   2 entries   +0  -0  ~0
      z_SC4UIScale_CamUI.dat           10 ->  22 entries   +12 -0  ~1
                                       (the ~1 is 2a554f6d, the old defect)

  THE GATE MISSED IT, AND THE GATE HAS BEEN FIXED TOO - which matters more
  than the build fix, because gate_tp_bmp_fit.py PASSED the broken build.
  It read the window and it read the bitmap and it never read the crop between
  them: two of the three numbers.

  The first repair asked the wrong question - "does the rect still cover the
  same fraction of the BITMAP?" - and that flags the m3 glyph, whose bitmap
  snapped 20 -> 32 while its rect and window both went 20 -> 30. Two pixels of
  transparent padding go undrawn there and nothing is wrong. What decides the
  screen is how much of the WINDOW gets painted:

      stripe, shipped : slice min(285,429) = 285 in a 428 window  -> 143 bare
      stripe, fixed   : slice min(428,429) = 428 in a 428 window
      m3 glyph        : slice min(30,32)   =  30 in a  30 window

  So the assertion is: the drawn slice must still cover its window as fully as
  it did at 1x. A crop the mod itself made smaller than its window stays legal;
  losing coverage we had at 1x does not.

  NEGATIVE CONTROL, RUN AGAINST THE REAL ARTEFACT rather than a synthetic one:
  the script was extracted back out of the DEPLOYED v2.97.0 dat and fed to the
  fixed gate.

      FAIL: 48 finding(s)
      "the drawn slice paints 285 of the 428px window width where 1x painted
       285 of 285 - about 428 expected. 143px of window is left bare."

  On the repaired build: PASS, 93 nodes across 3 tiers. The `--selftest`
  control still fails all 27 testable nodes as required.

  THE LAW. A BLIT HAS THREE NUMBERS - SOURCE, CROP, DESTINATION - AND SCALING
  ANY TWO OF THEM IS NOT A PARTIAL FIX, IT IS A NEW DEFECT. Same family as the
  coupled-pair law (#143: art and its rect must move together); this is the
  third member of that pair finally being named. When a gate checks a blit,
  make it read all three, and state which one it is NOT reading if it cannot.
  A gate that measures two of three will pass the build that ships wrong.

2026-08-13  v2.97.0  THE DIALOG NO GATE EVER LOOKED AT - CAM's OWN WINDOWS
                     WERE NEVER SCALED AT ALL (#154)

  Data + DLL. USER-REPORTED with a screenshot: CAM's Village Hall / Town Hall
  info screen, captioned "MZ v1", with its labels cut mid-word - "Residen",
  "Commerc" - and the percentages printed on top of them.

  MEASURED FIRST. The log had already recorded it, the moment it was opened:

      MWKID  0  id=0x10000005  (150,38 600x525)  vis=1

  which is the .UI's own 1x area=(150,38,750,563) TO THE PIXEL, on a 2400x1600
  screen. Nothing had ever scaled that window - not the offline packages, not
  the runtime sweep - while FontStyle-15x.ini scaled its text globally. 1x
  fields under 1.5x glyphs: the labels had to clip. No arithmetic on the
  screenshot, no inference from the picture. One log line.

  THE BLIND SPOT, which is the part worth carrying forward.
  build_dialog_static.py has a winner assert, and it is a good one. It asks:
  "is one of OUR targets owned by a plugin?" - because doubling a script the
  game never loads is a whole class of wasted fix. It has never asked the
  mirror question: "is a PLUGIN'S OWN dialog scaled AT ALL?" A mod-ADDED window
  is in no TARGETS list, has no stock twin to diff against, and is never built,
  so verify_doubled never sees it. The dialog rendered at 1x for the entire
  life of the project and EVERY GATE STAYED GREEN. That is the signature of a
  check whose scope excludes the failure, not of a passing build.

  ALL THREE of CAM's own dialogs are now scaled, not just the reported one:
  the city info screen 9b868f68, and the civic + school query panels 12121201
  and 12121205 ("# of Students", "Grade", "Local Funding"), which a player
  meets by clicking a school or a library and which had the identical defect.
  All three had been sitting in WINNING-CORPUS.md as third-party holders since
  that report was first generated, under a "What to do" heading describing
  exactly this fix.

      info screen   600x525 -> 900x788 / 1200x1050 / 1800x1575
      civic panel   292x260 -> 438x390 / 584x520  / 876x780
      school panel  292x287 -> 438x430 / 584x574  / 876x861

  They FIT at the minimum resolution of every tier - worth stating, because a
  dialog that scales off-screen is a worse bug than the one it fixes.
  ScaleTier::Decide gates on min(w/800, h/600), so the smallest admitted
  screens are 1200x900 / 1600x1200 / 2400x1800 and the info screen's bottom
  edge lands at 845 / 1126 / 1689.

  Nine of CAM's own bitmaps ship with them, all blttype=NORMAL - drawn at their
  own size and CLIPPED by the window, never stretched - so a 1x strip in a 1.5x
  row covers two thirds of it. CamUI goes 10 -> 22 entries per tier.

  NEW GATE, and the version of it that was WRONG.
  tools\uimap\emu\gate_tp_bmp_fit.py. The first draft asserted "no ink may be
  clipped" and reported 27 failures on a build that is correct. Art and window
  provably do not scale to the same number and cannot be made to:

      art  285x30 -> 429x45   Upscale2x CellUnit(285)=3 snaps 427.5 up to 429
      win  285x30 -> 427x45   edge-derived; and by the OFFSET-PARITY LAW an ODD
                              left edge gives 427 where an EVEN one gives 428,
                              in the SAME dialog

  One bitmap cannot be both 427 and 428 wide. The overhang is STRUCTURAL. Had
  that gate been believed, the next move would have been to "fix" Upscale2x -
  which is #149 repeating on the same lever. What ships instead asserts the
  question that decides what the screen looks like: the pixels the window cuts
  must be a REPEAT of the last pixels it keeps. Cut a flat stripe anywhere and
  nothing changes; cut through an icon or a border and it shows. Evaluated at
  1x as well, because CAM crops several of these itself, so we fail only where
  OUR scaling loses something 1x kept.

      PASS: 93 nodes across 3 tiers, nothing visible cut that 1x kept
            (69 pre-existing mod crops ignored)
      SELFTEST: 27 testable nodes, all failed as required

  The negative control puts the window edge on the rightmost column that
  differs from its neighbour. 22 of the 31 bitmaps are perfectly uniform and
  CANNOT be made to fail - they are counted and named as untestable rather than
  passed, because 31/31 "passing" a control most of them cannot participate in
  is a green that means nothing.

  A SECOND DANGLING REF IN CAM'S DATA. 12121205 draws {46a006b0,b5cfffff} into
  a 33x33 slot, and that TGI exists NOWHERE - who_owns_tgi.py reports no holder
  across the 9 archives AND the whole Plugins tree, find_tgi.py agrees for the
  archives, and the same run resolved the info screen's 12 refs to 17 holders
  as a positive control. Nothing draws at 1x, so nothing can fail to draw at
  any tier. It is recorded in TP_ART_DANGLING WITH that evidence; the bar for
  that list is deliberately high, because v2.38.3 put a TGI there on a
  stock-only null and shipped a splash tiled 2x2. Second CAM data bug found
  here after the 0xFF5D2E9F graph-label typo (#147).

  The guard that caught it FATAL'd with "references art that EXISTS at 1x" -
  and the condition it tested is an OR, so that was true of the other branch,
  not this one. #153's law repeating inside a different guard. The message now
  splits its branches and says which one fired.

  THE 2x REBUILD, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED. PACKAGES.md warns the untagged
  2x build "was deliberately NOT run" because those dats embed a timestamp.
  Running it was unavoidable - 2x users have the same defect - so the risk was
  measured ENTRY-LEVEL, never by whole-file hash (DbpfPack is non-deterministic
  and that has already produced one false alarm):

      z_SC4UIScale_DialogStatic.dat    262 -> 262 entries   +0  -0  ~0
      z_SC4UIScale_SaveWarningUI.dat     2 ->   2 entries   +0  -0  ~0
      z_SC4UIScale_CamUI.dat            10 ->  22 entries   +12 -0  ~0

  Twelve additions, nothing changed, nothing lost.

  DLL VERSION STRING, for real this time. v2.96.0's own notes claimed the
  string was corrected to 2.96.0. It was not - UISCALE_VERSION_STR was still
  "2.93.1" when that release shipped, so the note over-claimed and the log
  header kept lying. It now reads 2.97.0, VERIFIED IN THE DEPLOYED BINARY'S
  BYTES rather than in the source: the shipped DLL contains the literal string
  "SC4UIScale v2.97.0". The comment above the macro now says to bump it in the
  same commit as this file.

  THE LAW. A GATE THAT ONLY ASKS ABOUT YOUR OWN WORK CANNOT SEE WORK YOU NEVER
  STARTED. Coverage checks phrased as "is what we built still correct?" are
  blind by construction to "is there something we never built?" Ask the census
  question in the other direction too - enumerate what EXISTS and subtract what
  is handled - or the answer stays green while a whole dialog renders at 1x.

2026-08-13  v2.96.0  THE 1.5x OFFSET FAMILY CLOSED - ODD OFFSETS DIE AT A
                     FRACTIONAL FACTOR (advisors + My Sim, USER-CONFIRMED)

  DLL + data. The DLL version string is corrected to 2.96.0 in this release: it
  had read 2.93.1 since that build while shipping v2.94/2.95 behaviour, so the
  log header was lying about what was loaded. A version string that does not
  match the binary is the same class of defect as a setting that does nothing.

  2x and 3x untouched and PROVEN, not asserted: every seat pass is a measured
  no-op at an integer factor (0 of 22 and 0 of 14 windows moved) and both
  affected packages came out 0 of 262 / 0 of 655 entries changed.

  ⭐ THE LAW THIS RELEASE IS BUILT ON - closed form, and it names the failing
  AXIS in advance:

      For f = p/q in lowest terms, edge-derived rounding preserves a child's
      1x offset d from its frame IFF q | d, because
          round((t+d)*f) - round(t*f) == d*f
      exactly when d*f is an integer, and otherwise depends on the PARITY of
      the frame's own coordinate t.
      At f = 1.5 (q = 2): EVEN offsets always survive; ODD offsets are a
      lottery on t. At an integer factor q = 1 and every offset survives -
      which is the whole reason 2x and 3x have never shown this family.

  Verified on three panels, and it called the axis right on all three BEFORE
  anything was looked at:

      advisor faces      offset (2,1)   x even safe, y ODD  -> 1px HIGH
      My Sim portraits   offset (3,2)   x ODD  fails, y even -> 1px LEFT
      advisor detail     offset (2,2)   both even            -> never fails

  The user's own words for the two defects were "high" and "left".

  THE CURE - SEAT THE CHILD, DO NOT NUDGE IT.
  Both builders gained seat_faces_on_apertures: place the face at
  frame + ScaleRound(offset) instead of at its own independently-rounded edge.
  TRANSLATES ONLY - width and height are never touched, and the delta is capped
  at 1px. Advisors: 14 windows, anchored on the frame's flood-filled art
  aperture with five fatal guards. My Sim: 22 windows (21 move; the selected
  face is already correct and hits delta (0,0) - a number that is nowhere
  hardcoded).

  REJECTED ON MEASUREMENT, NOT TASTE. An ungated anchored rule inside
  double_subtree_areas moves 456 dashboard windows and 3 in the live balance
  bar; floor() for positions moves 373 of 531 budget+graphs children and 718 of
  957 dashboard. Either would have re-broken the Monthly Budget that was
  confirmed fixed hours earlier. Three independent adversarial reviews were run
  against the chosen rule and 0 of 3 landed.

  THE INSTRUMENT THAT SETTLED THE LAST ONE - #153 SEATPROBE.
  src\UiSpike.cpp BmpCtxBltThunk gained the two numbers the existing BMPX line
  never printed: the destination ORIGIN and the window's own L,T. One launch:

      SEATPROBE id=0x1234000n win L,T=(93,57) 54x62
                | dst origin=(0,0) src 36x41 -> dst 54x62 (x1.50)

  dst origin=(0,0) on EVERY draw - the blit is in the window's OWN local space,
  so the #47 hook contributes NOTHING to placement; it scales the portrait to
  exactly fill whatever window it is handed. Placement is the window's alone.
  That measurement eliminated the hook, CORRECTED a builder comment that had
  stopped the work ("runtime-generated portraits that stay 1x" - they do not,
  the hook scales them; that warning is about imagerect), and licensed a fix
  that had already been abandoned once.

  TWO SELF-INFLICTED FAILURES ON THE WAY, BOTH WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX

  (a) A CORRECT FIX WAS REVERTED ON A TRUE ERROR MESSAGE WITH A FALSE
      IMPLICATION. The first My Sim attempt died on "id 0xAA243E23 occurs 0
      times", read as "the pairing came from the wrong source". It had not:
      that function had been machine-generated through a Python string
      template, where \b in the regex became a literal BACKSPACE byte (0x08),
      so the pattern demanded a control character after every id. A five-second
      diagnostic proved the ids were all present in exactly the text being
      searched (frame=1, face=1, 34 face ids).
      ⚠ DO NOT MACHINE-GENERATE CODE CONTAINING REGEXES. Both copies of
      _seat_one_tag now say so in their docstring.
      ⚠ AND: when a build fails loudly, verify the failure MESSAGE before
      believing its IMPLICATION. A guard that fires proves something is wrong;
      it proves nothing about what.

  (b) verify_doubled REJECTED THE FIX, CORRECTLY, AND WAS TAUGHT RATHER THAN
      BYPASSED. It requires every area to be an exact scale (the #55/#56 guard
      against a doubled frame over 1x art) and could not tell a deliberate 1px
      seat from an accident. It now takes a `seated` ID LIST and applies a
      STRICTER rule to those windows - size still exactly scaled AND the origin
      a translation of at most 1px per axis. Ids, not a flag: a blanket "skip
      verification for this script" is how a real defect rides in behind a real
      fix.

  VERIFIED IN THE SHIPPED BYTES, NOT THE BUILD LOG:

      LIVE 1.5x top-row portrait x:  94, 172, 250, 328, 406, 484, 562
      pre-fix                        93, 171, 249, 327, 405, 483, 561

  1.5x STATUS: every reported defect in this tier is now closed and confirmed
  on screen - fonts, cell-divide seams, Set-Tier, the fake-terrain ring, the
  dashboard minimap, the reverse L, flyout thumbnails, the Monthly Budget, menu
  icons, the CAM "Exported" caption, the advisor portraits and the My Sim grid.

2026-08-09  v2.95.0  THE 1.5x ART FAMILY CLOSED - STALE PACKAGES, A HEIGHT
                     SNAP THAT SHOULD NOT EXIST, AND A RE-TIMING SAMPLER
                     (three causes, all measured; user-confirmed)

  DATA-ONLY. No DLL change since v2.94.1. 2x and 3x untouched, PROVEN twice by
  regenerating them and hashing: NamIcons 0 of 392 entries changed, and the
  entire 2x art set 0 of 2206 files changed.

  USER-CONFIRMED FIXED THIS RELEASE: disaster flyout thumbnails (the hover
  slide and wrap), the Monthly Budget rows, menu icons generally, and - from
  v2.94.x - the reverse L and the CAM "Exported" caption.

  CAUSE 1 - SIX OF NINE PACKAGES NEVER GOT THE v2.94.2 FIX.
  When kCellCounts was corrected to {3,4}, only SelectiveArt, ThirdPartyUI and
  WarriorUI were re-emitted. Six sat at their 15:03 build carrying art made
  under the broken LCM rule:
      REBUILT 17:10-17:11  SelectiveArt . ThirdPartyUI . WarriorUI
      STALE   15:03-15:04  ItemIcons . ItemIconsSub . NamIcons
                           DialogStatic . CamUI . SaveWarningUI
  That is exactly why the budget was fixed and the rest were not: the budget's
  row art is in SelectiveArt; the flyout thumbnails are in ItemIcons and the My
  Sim frames are in DialogStatic. Repacking the six took gate_namicons from
  392 wrong to 122 wrong with no rule change at all.

  A GATE WAS ALREADY RED FOR TWO HOURS AND NOBODY RAN IT.
  tools\uimap\emu\gate_namicons.py had been failing since 15:04 -
  "FAIL 15x all 392 entries are exactly x1.5 (392 wrong)" with 2x and 3x green
  as a built-in positive control - while a dozen theories were hand-derived.
  RUN THE GATE SUITE BEFORE FORMING A HYPOTHESIS.

  CAUSE 2 - A HEIGHT SNAP ON A HORIZONTAL STRIP.
  The remaining 122 were the 176x44 four-state family: width 264 correct,
  height 66 snapped to 68 (CellUnit(44)=4, 66%4=2, tie -> UP). A FOUR-STATE
  STRIP IS CUT HORIZONTALLY; snapping its height satisfies a divide the engine
  never performs. Upscale2x.cs gained --height-exact-group <hex>: for the named
  TGI group, snap the WIDTH and take the HEIGHT exactly.
  The licence for touching art dimensions here is that this repo's OWN standing
  gate already specifies the answer - gate_namicons.py:131 asserts
  (w,h) == (4*round(w0*f/4), lround(h0*f)) = 264x66. The package violated a
  rule we wrote down long ago.
  SCOPED BY GROUP: dropping the height snap globally moves 791 of 2280 sheets
  and reopens #143's white seams.

  CAUSE 3 - THE SAMPLER RE-TIMED EVERY SHEET'S CONTENTS.
  UpscaleNearest mapped output to source by the ACTUAL SIZE RATIO
  (sx = ox*w/ow) instead of by the FACTOR (sx = ox/factor), to guard against
  ScaleDim snapping a sheet DOWN and the factor map then cropping.
  THE PREMISE WAS FALSE: measured over the 284 distinct dimension values in the
  2280-PNG corpus, ScaleDim snaps down in ZERO cases and produces an output
  below v*1.5 in ZERO cases. Ties go UP. The factor map can only duplicate a
  trailing edge pixel; it can never crop.
  What the guard bought instead was a one-pixel re-registration of every
  fractional sheet - same dimensions, different pixels. Keyed-aperture origin,
  same 528x143 sheet: 1x (2,2) . BROKEN 1.5x (3,4) . FIXED 1.5x (3,3) .
  2x (4,4). The ratio map is kept as a guarded fallback and is never taken.
  ZERO DIMENSIONS CHANGE - only which source pixel each output pixel copies.
  That is why this is safe where an art-DIMENSION change is not (#148).

  STILL OPEN, AND HONESTLY SO: the advisor and My Sim portraits sit ONE PIXEL
  HIGH. The art is now measurably correct, so the remaining error is the WINDOW
  POSITION. The face-minus-frame offset is (2,1) at 1x, (4,2) at 2x - both
  matching their art apertures - but (3,1) at 1.5x against an aperture at
  (3,2). Window offsets come from rounding two ABSOLUTE coordinates and
  subtracting; art offsets come from scaling the RELATIVE offset. They agree at
  an integer factor and are parity-dependent at 1.5x.
  A position rule is under offline validation against the budget, graphs,
  dashboard and variable subtrees before anything is written. See #151.

  THE LAWS THIS RELEASE PAID FOR
  - A FIX IS NOT SHIPPED UNTIL EVERY PACKAGE THAT CONSUMES IT IS REBUILT.
    "I fixed the rule" and "the artefacts carry the fix" are different claims.
    This is the #58 / #116 / #139 failure class a FOURTH time, in a new costume:
    not a missing manifest entry, a missing REBUILD.
  - A GUARD WRITTEN FOR A HAZARD YOU NEVER MEASURED IS A CHANGE YOU DID NOT
    INTEND. Measure how often the defended-against case occurs. If the answer
    is zero, the branch is not protection - it is an unrequested behaviour
    change.

2026-08-06  v2.94.2  THE OVERSIZED-ART FAMILY - CellUnit TOOK AN LCM AND
                     OVERSHOT (#149). The user was right: "these issues
                     weren't there when we first started on 1.5x."

  DATA-ONLY (1.5x art set regenerated). 2x and 3x untouched BY CONSTRUCTION -
  ScaleDim returns early at an integer factor, so no snapping of any kind
  happens there and only the 1.5x preview was rebuilt.

  THE REPORTS: after v2.94.1, "Thumbnails still broken, MySim still broken,
  budget still broken, advisors still broken. Reverse L still okay."

  THAT SENTENCE FALSIFIED THE WHOLE ATTRIBUTION. Both v2.94.0 levers had been
  reverted and the four defects did not move, so they were never caused by
  them - the reports had merely ARRIVED just after a deploy. The cause was a
  change made EIGHT HOURS EARLIER, in #143's follow-up.

  THE MEASUREMENT. A "Select A My Sim" face: 1x art 200x49, a FOUR-state strip,
  cell 50, in a 50x49 button.

      window at 1.5x  = ScaleRound(50 * 1.5) = 75
      correct sheet   = 4 * 75               = 300
      SHIPPED sheet   =                        304   -> cell 76, a pixel wide

  300 was ALREADY a clean multiple of 4. It was pushed to 304 because
  CellUnit(200) = LCM(2,4,8) = 8 - and the 8 came from 200 happening to divide
  by 8, NOT from the sheet having 8 states.

  THE WRONG REASONING, RECORDED. #143 correctly found that the game's integer
  cell divides stop being exact at 1.5x, and snapped scaled dimensions to
  preserve them. The divisor was then widened to the LCM of every count in
  {2,3,4,6,8,12,16,24} that divides the width, on the reasoning that a bigger
  common multiple makes ANY divide safe. It does - and it makes every sheet
  whose width merely happens to divide by a large number BIGGER THAN ITS
  CONSUMER'S WINDOW. It moved 1678 more dimensions. The note written at the
  time - that the symptom "got worse rather than better" - was the whole
  answer, eight hours early, and was not followed up.

  MEASURED over the 255 art-sized four-state buttons (cell != window):

      LCM{2,3,4,6,8,12,16,24}   152   <- what shipped: the WORST option
      LCM{2,3,4}                 98      except doing nothing
      LCM{3,4}                   34   <- CHOSEN
      {4} alone                  19      (rejected - drops NineSlice /3)
      no snap at all            104

  Upscale2x.cs::kCellCounts is now {3,4}. Those are the divides that are
  load-bearing: NineSlice takes img->Width()/3 (VA 0x00794100) and a button
  state strip takes width/4. /12 for cGZWinScrollbar::SetImage still falls out
  on its own, since a sheet divisible by both 3 and 4 gets LCM = 12 from this
  same list.

  ⚠ 34 REMAIN, REPORTED NOT SILENTLY CARRIED. Closing them needs each sheet
  sized from its CONSUMER's window (states * ScaleRound(w,f)), which this tool
  cannot know - it runs over a directory and never sees a .UI - and which was
  tried in the builder and reverted, because art binds BY TGI and some
  consumers are created at runtime and appear in no .UI at all.
  gate_btn_undercover.py prints the count every run.

  ⚠ THE LAW: A "SAFE" OVER-APPROXIMATION IS STILL A CHANGE, AND IT IS PAID FOR
  IN PIXELS. LCM-of-everything is safe against CUTTING and unsafe against
  FITTING. Measure the overshoot before choosing a wider constraint.

  ⚠ AND: "THIS WASN'T LIKE THIS BEFORE" IS A BISECTION BOUNDARY, NOT AN
  OPINION. Two reverts were spent on changes that had nothing to do with these
  four defects. When a revert does not move the symptom, the attribution was
  wrong - stop reverting and go bisect.

2026-08-06  v2.94.1  SAME FIX, CORRECT LEVER - and the CAM caption CAM forgot
                     (v2.94.0's diagnosis was right and its LEVER was wrong.
                     Read that entry, then this one.)

  DLL CHANGE (first since v2.93.1) + data. 2x and 3x unaffected: the new branch
  cannot fire at an integer factor.

  WHAT v2.94.0 GOT RIGHT: the diagnosis. At 1.5x an ODD LEFT EDGE costs a
  control one pixel of width, because ScaleSubtree is edge-derived
  (newW = ScaleRound(l+w,f) - ScaleRound(l,f)), so the scaled SIZE depends on
  the POSITION. Art cell 71 in a 70px window -> uncovered right column and
  bottom row -> the REVERSE L.

  WHAT IT GOT WRONG: both levers. Within minutes of shipping, four regressions:
    * disaster flyout thumbnails slid right and WRAPPED on hover, with a light
      border on the top and left
    * "Select A My Sim": the whole 21-face grid slid left inside its frame
    * the advisors sat "slightly left and high in their boxes"
    * the Monthly Budget rows and the bottom dock came out misaligned

  LEVER 1, REVERTED - parity_nudge_btn_areas (moved the BUTTON).
  Moving a control onto an even edge does fix its width, and it is up to 2px at
  1.5x. That is invisible on the Landscape flyout (five buttons, 50px of air
  between them) and obvious in the densest grid in the game (aa1f1f57 took 24
  and 28 nudges, the most of any script).
  ⚠ LAW: A FIX THAT MOVES THINGS IS JUDGED BY ITS DENSEST NEIGHBOURHOOD, NOT BY
  THE CASE THAT REPORTED THE BUG.

  LEVER 2, REVERTED - fit_state_strips_to_windows (resized the ART).
  It rebuilt 61 sheets at exactly states*window. The arithmetic was right and it
  still broke the flyout thumbnails, because THE FLYOUT STRIP ITEMS ARE CREATED
  AT RUNTIME AND APPEAR IN NO .UI. They bind art by TGI like anything else, so
  the builder's conflict check - which only ever compared .UI consumers - said
  0 conflicts and was wrong.
  ⚠ LAW: EDITING GEOMETRY IN A .UI HAS THE SCOPE OF THAT .UI. EDITING ART HAS
  THE SCOPE OF THE WHOLE GAME. Not the same blast radius, not the same evidence.
  (The first suspicion was a stale imagerect elsewhere. MEASURED AND REFUTED:
  of 115 art-sized strips in scope, ZERO are also referenced by an imagerect.)

  THE CORRECT LEVER - src\UiSpike.cpp, in ScaleSubtree: for a LEAF window
  (GetChildCount() == 0) take the scaled size SIZE-DERIVED, ScaleRound(w,f),
  instead of edge-derived. The position never moves and the size changes by at
  most one pixel, so the art cell and the window agree.

    * LEAVES ONLY, because edge-derived rounding exists so abutting pieces stay
      abutting - #143's white seams are what happens when they do not. A window
      WITH CHILDREN is a panel whose edges are load-bearing; a LEAF is a
      discrete icon with nothing butted against it. Containers are untouched, so
      the seams cannot come back.
    * NO-OP AT AN INTEGER FACTOR BY CONSTRUCTION: ScaleRound(l*2) is exact for
      every l, so the two formulas already agree and the branch cannot fire.
    * It announces itself: a LEAFSIZE log line, up to 8 per city, giving id,
      1x rect, the edge-derived size and the size-derived size. Law 54 - no log
      line means it did not run.

  ALSO IN THIS RELEASE - #147, THE MISSING CAM GRAPH CAPTION.
  Graphs -> Power (and Water) showed a 4th legend row with a working checkbox, a
  cyan swatch and NO CAPTION. CAM's chart exemplars declare four series and bind
  label LTEXT 0xFF5D2E9F for the fourth; that id exists in NO installed archive
  (0 hits in 118,896 records across 107 DBPF files, with 0x0A5D2E9D, 0xFF5D2E98
  and 0xFF5D2E9E found in the same scan as positive controls). It should be
  0xFF5D2E98 = "Exported" - a single-nibble typo.

  We SUPPLY the missing resource rather than edit CAM's file:
  zzz-SC4UIScale\z_SC4UIScale_CamGraphLabels.dat, one 20-byte LTEXT.
  The payload format was READ OFF THE SHIPPED BYTES, not assumed - u16 CHARACTER
  COUNT, u16 0x1000, UTF-16LE, size = 4 + 2*count (the first draft hardcoded the
  count and was correct only by luck).
  Deliberately WITHOUT the trailing CRLF that CAM's 0xFF5D2E98 carries: the row
  directly above ours in the same legend, "Imported" (0xFF5D2E9E), has no CRLF
  either, so this matches its own siblings rather than a preference. Copying the
  CRLF would render the row two lines tall.
  In BOTH Deploy-OnGameClose.ps1 AND Test-DatIntegrity.ps1, per the standing
  law. Inert without CAM. Reported upstream: UPSTREAM-CAM-REPORT.md section 4.

2026-08-06  v2.94.0  1.5x REVERSE L - RIGHT DIAGNOSIS, WRONG LEVER (SUPERSEDED
                     BY v2.94.1 THE SAME DAY; it shipped four regressions)
                     (USER-CONFIRMED. Two bugs, found by one screenshot that
                     happened to contain a CONTROL.)

  DATA-ONLY. No DLL change - the DLL still reports v2.93.1, same as v2.93.2.
  2x and 3x are BYTE-IDENTICAL, proven twice (once after each of the two
  fixes): the SelectiveArt 2x package came out entry-identical, 0 of 655
  entries changed, compared against the previously deployed build.

  THE REPORTED DEFECT (#148). At 1.5x, a line down the RIGHT edge and along
  the BOTTOM of certain buttons - a "reverse L". First reported on the god-mode
  Day/Night sun and moon. 2x and 3x perfect. TEN theories were tried and all
  ten failed; they are recorded as dead ends in _tests\REGRESSION.md #148.

  WHAT ACTUALLY BROKE IT OPEN: a screenshot of Mayor mode -> Landscape showing
  ONE button of FIVE affected. A systematic rounding rule would hit all five or
  none, so the cause had to be a property of that one button. Every earlier
  report lacked a working sibling to compare against, which is precisely why
  ten wrong answers survived a full day.

  CAUSE 1 - AN ODD LEFT EDGE COSTS THE WINDOW ONE PIXEL.
  The five Landscape buttons are identical 47x37 controls on identical 188x37
  four-state sheets. Only their left edges differ:

      Raise Terrain    (68,  8,115, 45)   l=68  even
      Gouge Valleys    (68, 58,115, 95)   l=68  even
      Level Terrain    (69,108,116,145)   l=69  ODD   <-- the broken one
      Plant Flora      (68,158,115,195)   l=68  even
      Signs & Labels   (68,208,115,245)   l=68  even

  UiSpike::ScaleSubtree is EDGE-DERIVED on purpose (src\UiSpike.cpp:15546) -
  newW = ScaleRound(l+w,f) - ScaleRound(l,f) - so siblings that abut before
  scaling still abut after. That makes the scaled WIDTH depend on the LEFT
  EDGE:

      l=68 :  68*1.5 = 102 exact    ;  115*1.5 = 172.5 -> 173  ;  w = 71
      l=69 :  69*1.5 = 103.5 -> 104 ;  116*1.5 = 174   exact   ;  w = 70

  The art cell is 284/4 = 71 for all five, so only the odd-edge button gets a
  71px cell in a 70px window. THE SAME ARITHMETIC RETRO-EXPLAINS THE DAY/NIGHT
  FLYOUT: all three of its buttons sit at l=79 (odd), which is exactly why the
  artefact was reported on the sun AND the moon rather than on one of them.

  FIX: build_selective_safe.py::parity_nudge_btn_areas moves such buttons onto
  an edge the factor divides evenly (l*FACTOR integral; for f = p/q in lowest
  terms, l must be a multiple of q - "even" at 1.5x). POSITION ONLY; the size
  is preserved exactly. 177 buttons across 29 scripts at 1.5x. In the Landscape
  case the nudge also improves stock alignment, moving the odd button onto the
  same x as its four siblings.

  CAUSE 2 - ScaleDim's CellUnit IS A GUESS, AND THE BUILDER NEED NOT GUESS.
  The gate written for cause 1 immediately found 152 MORE, at EVEN left edges:

      a 136px FOUR-state sheet: CellUnit(136) = LCM(2,4,8) = 8, so 136*1.5 =
      204 snaps to 208 and the cell becomes 52 - but its 34px button scales to
      ScaleRound(34*1.5) = 51. 204 was ALREADY divisible by 4. The 8 came from
      the sheet's width happening to divide by 8, NOT from 8 states.

  Worse on the other axis: a horizontal 4-state strip needs NO vertical cell
  division at all, yet a 50px-tall sheet snapped 75 -> 76 and every button on
  it sat one row short of its art. That is the BOTTOM arm of the reverse L.

  Upscale2x.cs runs over a directory and cannot know which button binds which
  sheet. The builder parses the .UI, so it does not have to guess:

      sheetW = states * ScaleRound(buttonW * FACTOR)
      sheetH =          ScaleRound(buttonH * FACTOR)

  FIX: build_selective_safe.py::fit_state_strips_to_windows regenerates each
  art-sized state strip at exactly that size, FROM THE PRISTINE 1x SOURCE -
  never by resampling the already-upscaled sheet, which would compound the
  error and smear the magenta colour key (the failure that shipped a pink
  Mayor Rating bar in v2.93.2). The mapping is UpscaleNearest's own,
  sx = ox*srcW/dstW; Pillow decodes and encodes but NEVER resizes, so the
  project still has exactly ONE resampling rule. 61 sheets rebuilt, 0
  conflicts. A sheet with two consumers of different sizes is left UNCHANGED
  and reported.

  WHY BOTH ARE INVISIBLE AT 2x AND 3x: ScaleRound(l*2) is exact for every l, so
  w = 2*(r-l) always; and ScaleDim returns early at an integer factor, so its
  snap never fires. Both repairs are no-ops at an integer factor BY
  CONSTRUCTION - and that was proven rather than asserted.

  NEW GATE: tools\uimap\emu\gate_btn_undercover.py reads the STAGED scripts and
  the STAGED art - the artefacts that actually ship, not the intention - and
  asserts scaled window == art cell on both axes for every art-sized
  state-strip button.

      1.5x  177 + 152  ->  0
      2x            0  ->  0
      3x            0  ->  0

  Negative control: against a pre-fix build, 1.5x fails and names Level Terrain
  and the three Day/Night buttons.

  ALSO IN THIS RELEASE
  - REVERTED: the imagerect "under-read extension" from earlier in the day. It
    was real arithmetic (427 rects short at 1.5x, 0 at 2x/3x) and it damaged
    the thumbnail flyouts TWICE - a tolerance widened small-atlas cells across
    two cells, then an exact 1x test widened the last cell of every strip,
    which legitimately ends at the sheet edge. Neither moved the reported
    defect, because the affected buttons carry no imagerect at all. The
    one-directional #95 over-read clamp is all that remains. 1.5x
    over-extensions: 0 (below the integer tiers' long-standing baseline of 5).
  - NEW INSTRUMENT: tools\uimap\emu\render_flyout.py - the project's first
    OFFLINE COMPOSITOR. Every other gate in emu\ is arithmetic; its own README
    said "IT NEVER LOOKS AT A PIXEL", which is why ten theories were checked
    against numbers instead of against an image. It composites a window subtree
    from the SHIPPED art the way GZWinBMP/GZWinBtn do (imagerect crop,
    sheetW/states cell, magenta punched to alpha, 1:1 blit, optional window
    boxes) at any tier and under either candidate geometry rule. It killed two
    theories in three minutes each, with no build and no launch.
  - tools\uimap\emu\gate_btn_cell_vs_window.py kept as a REPORT-ONLY
    instrument recording a FALSIFIED law: a GZWinBtn's cell need not match its
    window in general (420 mismatches at 2x AND 3x on user-confirmed-perfect
    tiers, including a 24x6 sheet drawn into a 996-wide window). Only
    ART-SIZED buttons - those whose 1x cell exactly filled their 1x window -
    are bound by the new rule.
  - UPSTREAM-CAM-REPORT.md section 4: CAM's Power/Water graph legends bind
    label LTEXT 0xFF5D2E9F, which exists in no installed archive (0 hits in
    118,896 records across 107 DBPF files, with named positive controls). It
    should be 0xFF5D2E98 = "Exported". Reported, not yet worked around.

2026-08-06  v2.93.2  1.5x: THE GAME'S OWN CELL DIVIDE STOPS BEING EXACT
                     (and a rejected scaler was re-introduced and reverted)

  DATA-ONLY. No DLL change. 2x and 3x output is BYTE-IDENTICAL - proven,
  2206/2206 files, before anything was rebuilt.

  THE REPORTED DEFECT (#143). White seams across flyout art and the round
  tool buttons, at 1.5x only.

  THE WRONG TURN, RECORDED BECAUSE IT COST A LAUNCH AND SHIPPED A SECOND BUG.
  The first diagnosis blamed the resampler: 1.5 samples floor(o/1.5), which
  duplicates source rows 2,1,2,1, and uneven duplication could read as streaks.
  The "fix" made HighQualityBicubic automatic for fractional factors.

  It was refutable in one sentence without launching anything: NEAREST-NEIGHBOUR
  ONLY COPIES SOURCE PIXELS. It cannot introduce a colour the source lacks, so
  a white line absent from the 1x art can never be an NN artifact. The upscaler
  was never a candidate.

  Worse, README.md had rejected the HQ scaler in writing since the 2x pipeline
  was written - "blurs pixel art, FRINGES THE MAGENTA COLORKEY" - in the row
  describing the very tool being edited. Magenta 0xFF00FF is this game's
  transparency key; interpolation moves an exact key pixel to 0xFE01FE, the key
  test misses it, and the key colour DRAWS. Within one launch the user reported
  a pink Mayor Rating bar and pink outlines on the news reader. Both are
  colour-keyed art.

  A free detector existed and was ignored for an hour: SelectiveArt-15x went
  10.5 MB -> 20.5 MB. A 1.5x package has 2.25x the source pixels where 2x has
  4x, so it must be SMALLER than the 2x package (11.4 MB). A package size that
  moves the wrong way is an instant, launch-free regression signal.

  THE ACTUAL CAUSE. The game cuts art sheets into cells with an INTEGER DIVIDE
  whose divisor is baked into its own code:
      NineSlice        cell = (img->Width()/3, img->Height()/3)   VA 0x00794100
      four-state strip cell = width/4    normal/hover/pressed/disabled
  When the scaled dimension stops being divisible by that count, cell*count no
  longer covers the sheet. A 516px strip at 1.5 is 774; the game computes
  cell = 774/4 = 193 and 4*193 = 772. The true boundary is 193.5, so each cell
  drifts a further half pixel and every state draws a sliver of the NEXT state
  - the bright hover art. That is the seam.

  MEASURED over the 2,206 extracted 1x sources:
      factor 1.5   /3: 475 of 1534 broken (31.0%)   /4: 967 of 2256 (42.9%)
      factor 2.0   /3: 0                            /4: 0
      factor 3.0   /3: 0                            /4: 0
  An integer factor preserves divisibility automatically (N|v implies N|k*v).
  The defect is not merely unobserved at 2x/3x - it is STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE
  there. No amount of testing at the integer tiers could have found it.

  CURE. Upscale2x.cs::ScaleDim snaps a FRACTIONAL factor's output to preserve
  whatever cell divisibility the source had (CellUnit = 12/4/3/1), ties up.
  Integer factors return round(v*f) untouched. UpscaleNearest now maps output
  to source by the ACTUAL ratio ox*w/ow rather than by the requested factor, so
  a snapped target still resamples the whole image; for an integer factor
  ow == w*factor so ox*w/ow == ox/factor exactly.

  This is a RULE, not an inventory - no per-TGI table of which sheet is cut
  how, so it cannot silently miss a sheet (cf. find_tgi.py's archive list,
  which failed exactly that way). Precedent already in-tree:
  rebuild_namicons.py has carried snapped/non-div4 counters since #139.

  GATES, all offline, all run before the user launched:
      2x byte-identical to the pre-change tool ... 2206 / 2206
      mode line at factor 1.5 .................... nearest-neighbor (default)
      snap reached the output FILES .............. 1442 moved, 0 broken
      snap reached the DEPLOYED .dat bytes ....... 673 moved, 1769/1769 pass
      Test-DatIntegrity .......................... ALL PASS (24 dats, 29 hashes)

  REBUILT AT 1.5x: SelectiveArt (655), DialogStatic (262), ItemIcons (356),
  ItemIconsSub (130), NamIcons, CamUI, ThirdPartyUI, SaveWarningUI.

  #144 Set-Tier.ps1 REPORTED EVERY PACKAGE "DEPENDENCY-GATED OFF" WHILE ALL
  NINE WERE LIVE. Deploy writes the active X-<tag>.dat AND refreshes the
  X-<tag>.dat.x1-disabled twin, so both exist for one tier. Get-Families used a
  plain assignment keyed on the tier; ".dat" enumerates before
  ".dat.x1-disabled", so the disabled twin overwrote the active one and every
  family read active=false. -Status printed all nine as "(none -
  dependency-gated off)" and the rename loop did nothing. The active file now
  wins the slot and a duplicated slot is REPORTED. A status instrument that is
  wrong in the safe-looking direction is worse than no instrument.

  THREE LAWS PAID FOR HERE:
   1. When a rule is justified by "it is exact", NAME THE FACTORS IT IS EXACT
      FOR. Fourth defect of this exact shape in one session.
   2. CHECK OUR OWN PRIOR WORK FIRST. The rejected option was documented in the
      README row for the tool being changed. One grep.
   3. A COMMENT IS NOT CODE. The first revert rewrote the comment above
      `if (!hqExplicit) hq = ...` and left the statement. The tool kept printing
      "Mode : high-quality" and rebuilt the whole tier bicubic a second time.
      Only the printed mode line caught it.

  STILL OPEN AT 1.5x: flyout bottom item (container height / scroll extent, not
  art) and a corrupt dashboard city map. Neither is touched by this build.

2026-08-06  v2.93.1  A BUILD PATH WAS HIDING IN THE DLL AS UTF-16

  Found while preparing the first public Release, by a scanner written
  specifically because every existing check reads files as TEXT and is
  therefore blind to a compiled binary.

  THE DEFECT. dist\SC4UIScale-v2.93.0\Plugins\SC4UIScale.dll carried this in
  .rdata, as a UTF-16LE string:

      C:\Users\<user>\...\vendor\gzcom-dll\...\cRZCOMDllDirector.cpp

  Source: a plain assert() in the vendored SDK. Without NDEBUG, MSVC's assert
  expands to _wassert(..., _CRT_WIDE(__FILE__), __LINE__), and _CRT_WIDE puts
  the compiler's ABSOLUTE source path into the binary as wide characters.

  WHY IT SURVIVED EVERY EARLIER CHECK. /PDBALTPATH:%_PDB% (v2.66.0, #112)
  removed the ASCII PDB path from the debug directory, and the #108 plan, this
  changelog and the ship manifest all treated that as the whole problem. It was
  half. A text scanner cannot see a wide string; a scanner that never opens a
  .dll cannot see anything at all. TWO independent text scanners called the
  bundle clean while it sat there.

  CURE: /d1trimfile:"<repo root>\\" in both vcxproj files, so __FILE__ is
  relative at compile time. Chosen over defining NDEBUG because it changes NO
  behaviour - the asserts still fire, which is worth keeping in a DLL that
  hooks a 2003 binary. Verified by rebuild: ZERO occurrences, ASCII or wide.
  WARNING: the trailing backslash must be DOUBLED. A single one makes MSVC read
  the closing quote as escaped and the build dies with `D8003: missing source
  filename`, which looks like a broken project file rather than a quoting bug.

  NEW STANDING CHECK: _tests\Test-BinaryPii.py. Reads BYTES, twice - raw, and
  with NUL bytes stripped (the pass that turns UTF-16 back into something a
  byte search can find). Handles a directory or the .zip itself, and scans a
  zip's CENTRAL DIRECTORY too, because entry names are stored in the clear.
  Negative control: run it against dist\SC4TouchControls-v1.0.5 and it reports
  3 files carrying personal data - the frozen bundles still embed the path and
  must never be attached to a Release.

  TWO OF ITS OWN BUGS, CAUGHT IN THE FIRST FIVE MINUTES, BOTH WORTH KEEPING:
  * its bundle auto-picker sorted "...-v2.93.1.zip" above the FOLDER of the
    same name; os.walk on a file yields nothing, so it scanned ZERO files and
    printed CLEAN. Zero files is now a REFUSAL, not a pass.
  * it crashed while PRINTING a hit (a byte the console codepage could not
    encode), which reads exactly like a crash while FINDING one. Output is
    ASCII-sanitised now.

  ALSO: THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md gained a CONTENT NOTICE covering the .dat
  packages in a Release download - what each is derived from and who owns it
  (Maxis/EA, NAM, CAM, warrior, cyclone-boom, null-45), the gating that keeps
  each inert unless its mod is installed, and a standing offer to pull any
  package on request. The SOURCE repo still contains none of it.

  RELEASE ARTIFACT: dist\SC4UIScale-v2.93.1.zip, 82.8 MB, 38 members,
  sha256 A10FF51AF7CCB215D54008A1CA3328C6E94353B643E7210045CFA561F6D47A70.
  Byte-scanned clean, members and entry names both.

2026-08-05  v2.93.0  #139 NAM ITEM ICONS + #140 STARTUP SPLASH + #141 CITY-OPEN
                     COST MEASURED

  Three defects and one measurement, all from a fresh install of the game with
  NAM, CAM and sc4pac content on top - the first time this mod has been tested
  against a large third-party load-out rather than a mostly-stock one. Every
  finding below is about what OTHER PEOPLE'S FILES do to our overrides.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  #139  NAM ITEM ICONS - 392 strips doubled inside doubled cells
  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  SYMPTOM (user): "Icons are appearing twice in our flyouts. When you hover
  they disappear." That is the #49/#55 signature exactly - a FOUR-STATE strip
  supplied at 1x inside a cell we doubled, so the button's imageWidth/4 cell
  arithmetic lands mid-glyph and shows two half-size states side by side. The
  hover state is off the end of the strip, so hovering blanks it.

  CURE: z_SC4UIScale_NamIcons-{15x,2x,3x}.dat - 392 icons, upscaled from the
  MOD's own copies, shipped from zzz-SC4UIScale\ and gated in ScaleTier on the
  presence of NetworkAddonMod_Controller.dat. Presence-only, exact name, no
  size check: the package hard-codes no rects, so a NAM update makes it look
  stale rather than mis-geometried.

  THREE THINGS WENT WRONG ON THE WAY, AND ALL THREE ARE GENERAL:

  1. TEN ICONS WERE SILENTLY SKIPPED BY MAX_PATH. NAM nests its dats to
     283-298 characters (".../Legacy Road Viaduct Puzzle Piece Menu Button
     Access#/..."), past Windows' 260-char limit. A bare `except OSError`
     around the open() swallowed it, so the census reported "covered" for
     files it had never managed to read, and DbpfExtract.exe errored outright
     on the longest ones. Cure: the \\?\ long-path prefix everywhere a mod
     path is opened, plus copy-to-a-short-path before invoking the extractor.
     ⚠ The gate had the same bug - it walked NAM with the same bare except,
     so it was ALSO blind, and would have passed the incomplete package.

  2. COVERAGE WAS COUNTED FIRST-FOUND INSTEAD OF LAST-LOADED. 0x6A47A005 is
     supplied by three files; we took the first one the walk hit and upscaled
     it, but the file that actually WINS at load time is RealRailway_Icons.dat.
     We shipped a doubled version of art the game never displays.

  3. THE RAIL BUTTON STAYED INVISIBLE AFTER "EVERYTHING ELSE WAS FIXED", AND
     THE COVERAGE COUNT SAID IT WAS COVERED. 0x2A3ED76A is a STOCK icon, so we
     had always doubled it - in the ROOT ItemIcons package. NAM also ships it,
     from a SUBFOLDER. Root FILES load before SUBFOLDERS, so NAM won, and our
     root copy could never beat it no matter how correct it was. Fixed by
     shipping that icon from zzz-SC4UIScale\ as well.

     THE LAW: coverage is not "is this TGI in a package of ours". It is
     "does OUR file load LAST for this TGI". Those two questions gave
     different answers for exactly one icon out of 392, and the one they
     disagreed about is the one the user could see.

  GATE: tools\uimap\emu\gate_namicons.py - 392 icons x 3 tiers, 5 negative
  controls, and a section 3b that re-derives the load-order WINNER per TGI
  across the whole Plugins tree and fails if any third-party file wins an icon
  we claim to cover. That section was written after bug 3 and verified RED on
  the old package before it went GREEN on the new one.

  ALSO CAUGHT BY THE GATE ON ITS FIRST RUN: 1.5x widths off the 4-grid.
  356 * 1.5 = 534, and 534/4 = 133.5 - fractional state cells, i.e. the very
  bug this package exists to fix, reappearing at another tier. Widths are now
  SNAPPED to a multiple of 4 (4*round(w*f/4)), not merely rounded.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  #140  STARTUP SPLASH - the "eighth archive" that was really the eighth AND
        the ninth
  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  SYMPTOM (user): "the SimCity logo appearing 4 times". The splash root is
  blttype=tiled, so a 768x600 background inside a doubled 1536x1200 root tiles
  2x2 instead of stretching - the #72 src-follows-dst family.

  The first fix shipped the wrong bitmap: CAM's background, not the game's.
  99.72% of its pixels differ from stock. The reason is the part worth keeping:

  find_tgi.py - the tool whose entire job is answering "does this TGI exist in
  the game" - carried a HARD-CODED LIST OF SEVEN ARCHIVE NAMES, and its own
  docstring called that "all seven". The install actually ships NINE. The
  splash background {46a006b0,ea7f0eae} lives in Intro.dat, which was not on
  the list, so the tool printed a confident negative, a build-time guard was
  relaxed to let the "dangling" ref through, and CAM_Intro.dat's copy was the
  only art left standing.

  The tool now DISCOVERS the archives by enumerating the install root. Run
  against this machine it reports nine and names them: the list was missing
  Intro.dat AND Sound.dat. A hand-maintained inventory of what exists is a
  claim about the filesystem that fails silently and only in the case you
  needed it - so it is now derived, and an expansion that drops a tenth
  archive is covered for free.

  ⚠ THE OTHER HALF OF THE LESSON: a null from an instrument is not a fact
  until you have shown the instrument COULD have seen the thing. This tool
  even carried a comment warning that its negatives are not "dangling" - and
  the warning was about the wrong axis. It said "Plugins were not scanned",
  which was true; nobody noticed that the GAME side was incomplete too.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  #141  FIRST CITY OPEN COSTS 54 SECONDS - MEASURED, and there is no lever
  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  User question: why does opening the first city of a session take so long
  with NAM installed, and can it be made to happen at a less annoying moment?

  MEASURED with _tests\Trace-CityOpen.ps1, anchored on the DLL's own
  EARLYBAKE/PostCityInit log markers:

    segment                     wall     CPU     read        read ops
    startup -> city #1 init     20.7s   11.3s      78 MB      109,000
    city #1 content load        54.3s   53.1s     934 MB    1,902,959
    region + start city #2      16.8s   17.8s      75 MB      140,000
    city #2 content load         9.2s    8.4s     3.3 MB        4,008

  286x the bytes and 475x the operations on the first open. ~515 bytes per
  read. CPU/wall = 0.92 for the whole heavy window, which is a saturated core
  (SC4CPUOptions pins the game to one), and a 15-second stretch of it did ZERO
  disk while the working set climbed 1,807 -> 2,003 MB.

  CONCLUSION: it is a one-time lazy load of the plugin corpus on first use,
  it is CPU-saturated rather than disk-blocked, and we have no handle to
  trigger it earlier - no ini key, no hook, and nothing our DLL can call.
  Prefetching the files would only help if any of it were disk WAIT, and the
  trace says it is not. The one lever with a real mechanism behind it is
  repacking NAM into fewer, larger, uncompressed archives to cut 1.9M
  syscalls - which means generating modified copies of another mod's files,
  and is not justified until the user/kernel split says the cost is syscalls
  rather than parsing.

  ⛔ A RETRACTED VERDICT, KEPT ON THE RECORD. _tests\Trace-Threads.ps1 printed
  "ONE thread does essentially all the work - unpinning CANNOT speed up the
  load" from a capture totalling TWENTY MILLISECONDS across 20 threads, while
  the process had burned 186,000 ms. Win32_Thread returns ~0 per-thread times
  when the querying shell is not elevated and the target is (SC4 runs under a
  RUNASADMIN shim), and the affinity mask read back as a bogus 0x0. The script
  now RECONCILES the thread sum against the process total and refuses to print
  any verdict when it is under 50%, naming the elevation mismatch as the
  cause. A summary function that cannot decline to speak will eventually
  fabricate. Trace-CityOpen.ps1 now also records dKernMs/dUserMs per sample so
  the deciding split is captured automatically rather than asked for later.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  #138  INTRO VIDEO SCALING - patched, verified applied, NO VISUAL CHANGE
  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  ApplyIntroVideoScale rewrites the four intro-video geometry sites at
  0x79D063 / 0x79D068 (SetArea height/width) and 0x79D089 / 0x79D0A4 (the
  centring subtrahends), each verified against its expected opcode and stock
  operand before the write, and called from the EARLY tier block because
  PostAppInit runs 16.4 seconds after the tier decision. Gate:
  tools\uimap\emu\gate_introvid.py - 4 sites, 3 tiers, 6 negative controls.

  It reports 4/4 applied and the video does not change size. The patch is
  therefore NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT - something downstream of these four
  numbers also decides the presentation rect. ⚠ Do not write this up as "the
  intro video scales". BACKLOG.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  TEST-HARNESS DEFECTS FOUND THIS SESSION - both silent, both invalidating
  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  * THE STOCK BASELINE WAS NEVER STOCK. Set-StockPlugins.ps1 parked the user's
    plugins in a folder INSIDE Plugins\. SC4's plugin scan is RECURSIVE, so
    132 dats (98 MB) and 30 DLLs kept loading through every "stock" capture.
    Only an extension rename or a move OUT of the tree disables anything. The
    stash is now a SIBLING of Plugins, and every stock capture taken before
    this fix is contaminated. Enumerate BOTH plugin trees recursively before
    believing any stock claim - the folder listing at the top level looks
    clean while 98 MB loads underneath it.

  * A "GAME NOT RUNNING" TRACE THAT WROTE TO THE WRONG DISK. $PSScriptRoot is
    empty when a param default is evaluated under some `powershell -File`
    invocations, so the first capture went to C:\captures instead of the
    project. Resolved at runtime now, with a fallback chain. Non-interactive
    runs also gained -DurationSeconds, because Console.KeyAvailable THROWS
    when stdin is redirected and the old loop died on its first iteration.

  * THE 4GB PATCH BLINDED EVERY EXE-PINNED GATE, AND SO DID THE REINSTALL.
    Three gates printed "FAIL: fingerprint mismatch" together and stopped
    adjudicating. TWO causes, and separating them was the work. (1) The LAA
    bit is one bit in the PE COFF Characteristics word - it cannot change an
    instruction, but exe_fingerprint hashed the whole file, so it moved the
    hash; the bit is now MASKED before hashing, which keeps every existing pin
    valid in both directions. (2) The reinstall produced a DIFFERENT binary of
    the same size, so the build the gates were derived from no longer exists.
    Re-pinned to f9b059d29940d1a2 - but only after running each gate with the
    fingerprint bypassed and confirming EVERY byte-level site assertion still
    passed. A fingerprint is a proxy for "the code is what I think"; reading
    the bytes at the VAs is the stronger test, and it is what makes a re-pin
    honest rather than a relaxed guard (which is how #140 shipped CAM art).
    Also: gate_103_twin_ids.py held a THIRD private copy of exe_fingerprint,
    so it stayed red while its siblings went green ON THE SAME BINARY. One
    implementation now, imported.

  * NamIcons WAS NEVER IN THE DEPLOY MANIFEST - the third package to rot this
    way. Build-Dist.ps1 produced a 30-file bundle with no NamIcons while the
    live install had all three tiers, because they were hand-placed the day
    they were built. #58 (ThirdPartyUI) and #116 (ItemIcons) verbatim. Added
    to Deploy-OnGameClose.ps1 AND Test-DatIntegrity.ps1's deployed==built
    hashes; the bundle is 33 files. A package is finished when it is in the
    manifest and the integrity test, not when it builds.

  * THE ACTIVE-TIER REFRESH COPIED A STALE FILE OVER A FRESH ONE. .x1-disabled
    means two different things - "not the active tier" (only -15x/-3x) and
    "the mod this package patches is not installed" (any tier). The v2.92.0
    refresh assumed the first, so with the warrior mod absent it copied an old
    gate-disabled WarriorUI-2x over the one the deploy had just written. A
    refresh that moved BACKWARDS in time, caught only by the deployed==built
    hash. The loop is now scoped to -15x/-3x, and where a gate-disabled 2x twin
    exists the fresh bytes are pushed INTO it and the active file removed.
    ⚠ The first attempt DELETED the twin instead and turned
    Test-ThirdPartyGates red - the deploy owns CONTENT, the DLL owns whether a
    package loads at all, and a step that answers the second question while
    doing the first is a bug. It self-healed at the next launch, which is what
    made it worth fixing properly rather than living with.

  * DialogStatic went 261 -> 262 entries. The added entry is exactly
    {0x856DDBAC, 0x46A006B0, 0xEA7F0EAE} - the genuine stock splash background
    out of Intro.dat. Expectation updated in Test-DatIntegrity after diffing
    the TGI sets, not before.

  PRE-GITHUB (task #108): the 54-item scrub is EXECUTED and the publish route
  is built. Ship set = 630 files / 6.90 MB, zero identity tokens, leak scan
  clean. New: _packaging\EXPORT-PUBLIC.ps1 (allowlist export into a fresh
  directory outside cloud sync, then git init THERE - the working tree is
  ~200:1 excluded:shipped and holds a foreign .git clone), tools\sc4paths.py
  (one path resolver replacing fourteen hard-coded literals), and a
  privacy_audit.py that no longer hard-codes the name it is hunting for - the
  auditor was itself the leak and would have shipped in the first commit.

  NEW TOOLS: _tests\Apply-4GBPatch.ps1 (LAA bit, -Status/-Undo, backs up to
  .pre4gb-backup) - the patch is kept on user instruction, and for the record
  it did NOT fix the DX7 surface crash it was tried against: same EIP, same
  ESP/EBP after applying it. _tests\Toggle-OurDll.ps1 parks our DLL OUTSIDE
  the Plugins tree, never inside it, for the same reason the stash moved.

2026-08-05  v2.92.0  #137 GRAPHS RADIO BAND - BORN CORRECT AT LAST

  USER-CONFIRMED "100% fixed" at 2x and 3x; Data Views unaffected.
  THE FINDING THAT MATTERS: #127's born-correct "dock at show" lived inside
  ScaleOnShow, which only runs at gShowHookMode >= 2. The shipped ini has
  ShowHook=0 and the log says so - "SHOWHOOK installed ... (mode 0: log only)".
  The hook is installed at all only because EarlyDock=2 forces the trampoline
  in. So the dock-at-show HAD NEVER EXECUTED ONCE since #127, and every version
  since relied on the tick to correct the panel after its first paint. That is
  the one-frame jump, and it survived two CORRECT fixes in a row because both
  improved code on a path nobody was reaching (law 41, installed != executed).
  The same function already carried this warning for EARLYDOCK at v2.41.17 -
  "the trampoline now serves TWO consumers ... would silently never run if this
  still keyed off it alone". Dock-at-show was the THIRD consumer.
  CURE: the dock gets its own branch in SetFlagDetour, independent of ShowHook
  (which STAYS 0 - scale-at-show is refuted for the city HUD and must not be
  revived to reach unrelated code). It passes fromShow=true so ApplyPanelDocks
  gates on GEOMETRY (w/h > 0) instead of the visible flag: the detour fires on
  the 0->1 transition, so IsVisible() is still FALSE for the very window being
  shown, and the old visibility test rejected precisely the case it existed to
  serve.

  Added the fromShow parameter + geometry gate. Correct, and still invisible -
