2026-08-05  v2.88.0  #136 ADVICE ROW DISMISS X - WIDEN THE ENCODING

  USER-CONFIRMED at 3x ("news is good"); the log proves the path executed:
  "advice row WIDE re-encode at 0x0079388B - sub imm8 -> lea imm32, S=165" and
  "X 54px (scaled)". The old "exceeds the imm8 ceiling - clamped" line now
  appears ZERO times.
  The row's width budget lived in one sign-extended imm8, so 3x (S=165) could
  not be encoded and the dismiss X shipped at STOCK 18px - recorded for months
  as a deliberate, permanent tier split. It was not permanent: the imm8 is an
  ENCODING, not a law. When S > 127 the patch now rewrites the 19-byte window
  at 0x0079388B into "push 8 / lea esi,[eax-imm32] / two stores / 3 nops",
  paying for the extra bytes with a folded "mov" and a store proven dead BY
  LIVENESS (the gate walks the reads; one of its four positive controls drops
  the LIVE store instead, to prove the check can fail). 1.5x and 2x keep the
  untouched 3-byte path.
  ALL FOUR HALVES OF THE COUPLED PAIR SHIPPED TOGETHER: DLL ceiling removed,
  build_selective_safe.py filter removed, 3x SelectiveArt rebuilt 651 -> 655
  entries (now matching 1.5x/2x), Test-DatIntegrity expectation updated.

2026-08-05  v2.87.0  #135 RING/BAR JUNCTION - SEAT RINGS WITH THE DOCK

  USER-CONFIRMED on both families. The ring, strip and bar are ONE welded shape
  in the buffer (ring spans 0..80f, strip starts at exactly 80f), so ANY ring X
  nudge drives the connector wedge into the panel and leaves its two border
  lines ending mid-panel - the "broken bar at the junction". Present at 2x for
  months and simply tolerated; it was never 3x-specific.
  MAYOR family: SubRingDX now derives to 0 and the DOCK carries the alignment
  (SubDockDX = rhu(21f) - rhu(25f) - SubNativeDX()). This CHANGED two
  long-shipped f=2 constants deliberately (-53 -> -28, 25 -> 0) and moves the
  2x assembly ~25px right. If a gate ever reads -53 or 25 again, someone
  reverted the fix - that is not a baseline.
  DISASTER family: same defect, in the LIVE ini only. [Disaster] RingDX=16
  DockX=-2 vs the DLL's own defaults 0/6, which are ring-position-IDENTICAL at
  every tier (DockX is tier-scaled and 8*f exactly cancels the seat-scaled
  RingDX) - so the dial bought nothing and moved only the STRIP. The shipped
  _packaging ini carries neither key, so the public build was never affected.
  Only two ring-X nudges exist in the codebase; both are now zero.

2026-08-05  v2.86.0  #134 SUB-FLYOUT NATIVE OFFSET IS NOT FACTOR-INDEPENDENT

  USER-CONFIRMED at 3x. kSubNativeDX was "const int = 20", commented
  "factor-independent". It is btnW/2 - 27: 20 at f=2 (the old constant
  reproduced exactly) and 43 at f=3, MEASURED by the new SUBCAND instrument.
  Believing that comment is what kept 3x broken.
  WHAT THE STALE 20 COST: born (SUBBORN2) docks from the game's REAL native
  position while the sweep predicted it from the constant. At f=2 they agree,
  so 2x was always right. At f=3 they differed by 23px, so the container rested
  23px off AND the sweep matched NEITHER atNative nor atTarget - it silently
  declined every 3x sub-flyout and never ran, taking gSubArrowAbs (the
  back-arrow click zone, assigned ONLY inside that sweep) with it. One visible
  symptom, one invisible one.
  Ring nudges are now DERIVED per tier, never hand-entered - one ini file
  cannot hold one correct pair for three tiers. New instruments: SUBCAND (logs
  BEFORE the atNative/atTarget gate, so a declining sweep can no longer be
  silent) and SUBSWEEP (its positive control).
  Gate: tools\flyout-sim\gate_subnative.py predicts the game's own measured
  (280,207) from the button alone; 10 negative controls.

2026-08-05  RELEASE HYGIENE  (no code change)

  WebText SHIPPED (user decision). While wiring it in, the deploy script's own
  comment turned out to be FALSE ON BOTH COUNTS. It claimed
  z_SC4UIScale_WebText.dat and MenuFix.dat were 'hand-placed, no build source
  in the repo' and could never be rebuilt. In fact:
    tools\webtext\z_SC4UIScale_WebText.dat  + build_webtext.py that makes it
    tools\itemicons\_work\z_SC4UIScale_MenuFix.dat
  and the repo WebText is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the live one (sha256 5cd27889...),
  so it had been reproducible the whole time.

  THE REAL DEFECT UNDERNEATH: Test-DatIntegrity.ps1 asserts BOTH deployed
  copies match those repo files (lines 331-332) while Deploy-OnGameClose
  copied NEITHER. Those assertions passed only because the live files were
  hand-placed once and never regenerated - the moment a builder emitted
  different bytes, DatIntegrity would fail with no deploy to blame. That is
  the #58 / #116 failure class sitting inside the very file whose comments
  describe it. WebText is now a normal manifest entry, so it reaches the live
  install AND the release bundle through one path.

  MenuFix stays out, but now for the honest reason: it rewrites CAM's gameplay
  submenu data rather than scaling UI, so shipping it is a decision about a
  THIRD-PARTY mod's content. Recorded as such rather than as 'unbuildable'.

  DLL, and a live trap for anyone browsing the repo. Contents inventoried

  (124 art overrides) long superseded by the real ones (655). Nothing unique.
2026-08-05  PLUG-AND-PLAY RELEASE BUNDLE  (no code change)

  _packaging\Build-Dist.ps1 produces dist\SC4UIScale-v<version>\ - a Plugins\
  tree the user copies straight in, plus README.txt, Install.ps1 (with
  -WhatIf and -Uninstall), LICENSE.txt, THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md and
  SHA256SUMS.txt. v2.85.0: 29 files, 66.4 MB, all three scale tiers.

  IT PARSES Deploy-OnGameClose.ps1 FOR ITS FILE LIST rather than keeping a
  second copy, and that is the whole design. A hand-maintained duplicate of
  'what a working install contains' is precisely what produced #58
  (ThirdPartyUI never in the deploy list, live copy frozen at an old build
  epoch, shipped dangling clone refs) and #116 (ItemIcons + ItemIconsSub
  omitted for the entire life of the script). Both looked green throughout.
  One manifest means a packaging bug is the SAME bug as a deploy bug instead
  of a second one that disagrees. It refuses to build if it parses fewer than
  10 entries, or if any named source file is missing.

  VERIFIED: build\Release, dist bundle and the LIVE Plugins DLL are all the
  same SHA256 - the bundle ships exactly the binary the user tested.

  OMITTED ON PURPOSE: z_SC4UIScale_WebText.dat and MenuFix.dat have no build
  source in the repo and cannot be reproduced. WebText has ZERO references in
  src\, so the DLL does not depend on it (worst case the web button keeps its
  stock label). -IncludeUnbuildable pulls them from a live install and says so
  in the output. MenuFix rewrites CAM's gameplay data and stays dropped.

  ⚠ dist\SC4UIScale-preview\ is a superseded hand-made July bundle with an
  old DLL. Flagged in README; do not publish it.
2026-08-05  SHIP BLOCKER CLOSED + LICENSING SETTLED  (no code change)

  SHIP BLOCKER (#128) - the shipped ini made the mod INERT. Verified three
  ways before touching anything: ScaleAll and ScaleRegion both default FALSE
  in Settings.h; the only writes anywhere in src\ are the ini read
  (Settings.cpp:77-78) and a force-to-false at tier 1; and
  _packaging\SC4UIScale.ini contained NEITHER key. A fresh install off that
  file loaded, scaled nothing, and logged no complaint. The file's own header
  even claimed 'any key you delete simply falls back to the same built-in
  default' - true of every key except the only two that matter.
  Added both, plus the region keys, and rewrote that header.

  THE GATE COULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT IT, which is the real fix.
  Test-ShippingIniKeys.py only checked the FORWARD direction - every key in
  the ini resolves to a real read. An absent key is not a wrong key, so a
  missing load-bearing setting was structurally invisible. Added the reverse
  check: any setting that gates a whole feature AND defaults off must be
  PRESENT. It re-reads the default from Settings.h each run, so flipping a
  default later relaxes the requirement instead of leaving a stale rule.
  NEGATIVE CONTROL RUN: deleting ScaleAll=1 makes the gate exit 1 with the
  reason. A gate that cannot fail proves nothing.

  LICENSING (#120) - relicensed to CC0 1.0, PUBLIC DOMAIN, at user request
  ('as free-use as possible / no copyright claim at all'). Previously LGPL-2.1
  with a copyright line. CC0 text fetched verbatim from the canonical source,
  not reproduced from memory.

  ⚠ THE PACKAGE CANNOT BE FULLY UNENCUMBERED, and the docs now say so rather
  than implying otherwise. We STATICALLY compile two third-party libraries:
    gzcom-dll  LGPL-2.1-or-later  (cRZBaseString/cRZBaseUnknown/cRZCOMDllDirector)
    MinHook    BSD-2-Clause       (buffer/hook/trampoline/hde32/hde64)
  LGPL-2.1 section 6 therefore applies to the combined binary - recipients
  must be able to obtain the library source and relink. Publishing complete
  source satisfies it. Section 6 does NOT relicense our code, which is why
  CC0 is compatible. Upstream's README addresses DYNAMIC linking only; ours
  is static, so the licence itself governs and that is stated plainly.

  DEPENDENCY LIST IS NOW FRONT AND CENTRE (user request): a table near the
  top of README.md, and THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md in full. It also records an
  affirmative NEGATIVE: this project does NOT use
  0xC0000054/sc4-resource-loading-hooks, any other sc4-* plugin, memo33/*, or
  nsgomez/scgl - verified by search across src\, zero matches, with the only
  third-party ClCompile entries in the vcxproj being the two above.
  NOTICE's opening line claimed the project was LGPL; that is now quoted as
  superseded rather than deleted.
v2.85.0  2026-08-05  (current)  #132 CLOSED - USER-CONFIRMED
  ZOOM RANGE +/-2 -> +/-5. The two directions are not symmetric, and each was
  limited by something that turned out not to be a real constraint.

  ZOOM OUT was capped at stock by ONE COMPARISON. RegionBuildThunk early-outed
  at factor <= 1.001, so below stock the basis shrank the LATTICE while the
  tiles stayed baked-size - gaps between the diamonds. But GrowTileBitmap has
  always handled f < 1 (it rejects only a no-op or an out-of-range result);
  that path was simply unreachable. Lifting the guard makes the pair coupled
  below 1.0 and the map just gets smaller, which is what a big region wants.
  Zoom-out is also nearly free: a level below the base costs a QUARTER the
  pixels of one above it.

  ZOOM IN was bounded by our own bookkeeping, not by the machine. Two errors:
    * the 256 MB budget was set without checking, and SimCity 4.exe carries
      IMAGE_FILE_LARGE_ADDRESS_AWARE (characteristics 0x012F) => 4 GB of user
      space on 64-bit Windows, not 2 GB. Raised to 512 MB.
    * the pre-check charged for the alpha mask as though it persisted, but
      ReplayOneItem Deinits and releases it per item exactly as sub_7B13C0
      does - only ONE is ever live. That over-stated the working set by ~50%
      and would have refused levels that fit comfortably.
  MEASURED, 48-city region, pristine 260x160: +2 = 149 MB, +3 = 233 MB,
  +4 = 363 MB. Usable range now tier 1.50: -5..+5, tier 2.00: -5..+4,
  tier 3.00: -5..+2 (its tiles are already large). Levels past the budget are
  refused WHOLE, logged with the megabytes, and the level counter holds - so
  an unreachable level feels like the end of the range, not a fault.

  Gate: 65626 checks over 3 tiers x 11 levels x 2 tile sizes. Sub-stock levels
  are now asserted COHERENT rather than asserted-refused.
v2.84.0  2026-08-05
  #131b REGION TILE SHARPNESS - USER-CONFIRMED with a before/after pair.

  THE CAUSE WAS NOT THE SOURCE RESOLUTION. sub_7AE3D0 runs the game's 2-tap
  tent (sub_7AE160) at scale 1.0 with phase fx = 1.0 - frac(pos), purely to
  align a tile to the pixel grid at 1:1. MEASURED on the logged positions:
    tier 2.000 unzoomed : Y blend 0.0% on 9/9 tiles, X blend 4-20%
                          -> worst smear 0.40 screen px. Effectively sharp.
    factor 3.125 zoomed : X blend up to 75%, Y up to 81%, only 3/9 sharp
                          -> worst smear 2.53 screen px.
  ZOOM IS WHAT SWITCHES THE BLUR ON: the base tier's positions land on
  integers so the tent is a mathematical identity, and scaling them puts the
  fractions anywhere. That roughly DOUBLED the blur of the magnification
  itself, and is exactly why the softness was reported after zooming.

  THE FIX: hand the tent a phase of exactly 1.0, where its kernel is
  (tent(-1), tent(0)) = (0,1), normalised (0,16384) - asserted BIT-EXACT over
  all 65536 pixel pairs, not assumed - and re-apply the alignment as whole
  DEST pixels, which is finer than the source pixel the tent worked in.
  Residual misalignment 0..2.5 px -> <=0.5 px. Also fixed a half-pixel
  sampling bias that displaced every tile up and left.
  Identical treatment for all four buffers of an item is load-bearing:
  sub_7AE510 computes fx/fy ONCE (0x7AE548..0x7AE590) and pushes the same
  pair to all four sub_7AE3D0 calls, and sub_7ABCD0 stamps mask into source
  pixel-for-pixel with NO rect intersection - so colour and silhouette
  drifting apart by one pixel would be a black rim down one side of a city.

  ⚠ WHAT WAS *NOT* DONE, and why: nearest-neighbour is already the sharpest
  reconstruction that exists. Every smooth kernel (bilinear, Catmull-Rom) is
  strictly softer, so a 'better filter' would have made the reported symptom
  WORSE. The blur removed is the game's, upstream of our magnifier. And no
  higher-resolution source exists - 258x158 shown at 813x500 is the ceiling.
  This is sharper, not more detailed.

  [UiSpike] RegionTileSharp=0 restores v2.83.1 byte-for-byte.
  Gate grew to 65584 checks (tent identity + its blend control).
v2.83.1  2026-08-05
  #132 HARDENING - four real defects from the adversarial review, all in the
  ALL-OR-NOTHING contract rather than the mechanism (which was user-confirmed
  working at v2.83.0: 48 items, 0 skipped, 265ms, no crash).

  1. THE ZOOM HOOK COULD ARM WITHOUT ITS COUPLED HALF. ApplyRegionTileScale
     installs nothing at factor <= 1.001, but the gate above it only tests
     RegionMapScale != 0 - so RegionMapScale=1.0 reached the zoom hook with no
     tile-growth hook behind it. A wheel notch would then move the BASIS while
     every rebuilt tile came out stock-sized: gaps between the diamonds, the
     exact half-a-coupled-pair failure #131 exists to prevent. Zoom now arms
     only if the tile hook installed, and RegionZoomOperable() re-checks all
     three halves (verified iso patch + both hooks) at every step and at the
     wheel handler.
  2. THE BASIS WAS COMMITTED BEFORE THE ITEM LOOP AND NEVER ROLLED BACK, so a
     per-item skip produced precisely the mixed-scale state the code claimed
     to refuse - and then logged "Nothing changed" while the lattice HAD
     moved. All validation (snapshot coverage over the LIVE item list, size
     cap over all four bitmaps) now runs before any write; the basis is
     restored if nothing rebuilds; a genuine partial says so loudly.
  3. THE SIZE CAP BOUNDED ONE EDGE, NOT THE WORKING SET. At the ini maxima a
     step could ask for ~1GB inside a 32-bit process. Added a 256MB budget
     across all items. And the OOM path was not clean: Init (0x008269B0) sets
     width, height AND the ready latch even when AllocBits stored a NULL, so a
     failed grow left a correctly-sized bitmap with NO PIXELS wired into the
     live item, which sub_7AE510 then sized the composite from. GrowTileBitmap
     now re-Inits at the original size and restores the saved pixels.
  4. The pristine stash could hold a pointer whose AddRef silently declined.
     Unreachable today (verified: the other IGZBuffer vtable is built at one
     device-surface site) but the cost of being wrong is a use-after-free, so
     the invariant is now structural: no ref, no stash.

  Also: SetRegionIsoScaleLive now refuses unless ApplyRegionIsoScale verified
  and took, so a wheel notch can no longer overwrite a third-party mod's basis
  floats that the tier patch deliberately declined to touch.

  CORRECTED A FALSE COMMENT that was cited as evidence during the #132 design:
  UiSpike.cpp claimed [item+0x1C]/[item+0x2C] are vtable 0x00ADB418 "verified
  on BOTH before any write". They are 0x00AC1400 - measured on all nine items
  in every capture. It survived because nothing in that file ever compared
  against it. Kept as a named correction, not erased.
v2.83.0  2026-08-05
  #132 REGION ZOOM - REBUILT ON THE GAME'S OWN ITEM BUILDER.
  Mouse wheel on the region screen, +/-2 discrete levels at 1.25x each.

  WHY THE SHAPE. v2.82.x resized [item+0x1C]/[item+0x2C] in place and CRASHED
  TWICE, both 0xC0000005 at 0x0082653B inside GetPixel (no bounds check), the
  second on MOUSE MOVE with EBP = 260, the ORIGINAL tile width. An item owns
  four source bitmaps, a composite, and THREE DERIVED run lists - +0x44 is
  the CLICK MASK - and the mask is only ever rebuilt inside sub_7AE510.
  Clearing byte[+0x34] regenerates +0x38 and nothing else, so no in-place
  sequence can leave them consistent. A zoom must TRIGGER A REBUILD.

  THE MECHANISM.
    * ApplyRegionZoomHook - MinHook on sub_7AE510 (__thiscall(screen,item),
      ret 4). On entry it AddRefs the item's four bitmaps and records its
      float position + the live basis factor, keyed by REGION CELL (the item
      vector reallocs during the build, so pointers go stale). That entry is
      the last moment the un-shifted savegame art exists.
    * RegionZoomRebuild - per item: write scaled positions FIRST (sub_7AE510
      reads +0x10/+0x14 for its sub-pixel shift and never reads the basis),
      restore the pristine bitmaps, call the trampoline, call sub_7B5430,
      then Deinit+Release+null +0x20 exactly as sub_7B13C0 does. Then
      sub_7AB7C0 (the pan clamp is otherwise computed ONLY at Init), carry
      the scroll accumulator at screen+0x178/+0x17C, and sub_7B29E0.
  Absolute-from-pristine, not ratio-from-current: no compounding, no alpha
  re-stamping, and zoom-out is exact rather than lossy.

  ALSO FIXED, found while doing it: ApplyRegionIsoScale wrote only the four
  L1 basis floats. The SECOND basis at 0x00B0DBBC..D0 (L1/1024 plus an
  elevation term), which sub_7B5430 uses to project the airport/seaport
  overlay icons into tile space, has been stale at 2x/3x since #131 shipped.
  Both bases now move together, verified against stock before any write.
  Unseen until now only because the overlay pass needs view+0x118 != 0.

  RATE AND RANGE. A rebuild is 8-10 full-image passes PER ITEM and the cost
  is quadratic in the factor, so a wheel spin is debounced into ONE rebuild
  120ms after the last notch. Stock (1.00) is a hard floor: at the 1.5x tier
  level -2 wants 0.96, and below 1.0 the basis would shrink the lattice while
  RegionBuildThunk (which early-outs at <= 1.001) left the tiles stock-sized.
  That level is refused and the log says so. Caught by the offline gate, not
  in game.

  NEW GATE: _tests\Test-RegionZoomSizes.py - 46 checks over 3 tiers x 5
  levels x 2 tile sizes. Asserts source/mask/composite/click-mask/blit-list
  all describe the SAME box at every level (the invariant both crashes
  violated), that the round trip is exact, and that every notch changes the
  size. Constants are parsed out of Settings.h so it cannot drift.

  REMOVED: RescaleRegionTiles, RescaleTileBitmapCapped, RescaleTileBitmap and
  TryClearBuiltLatch - the in-place design. Left in place they would be a
  second, wrong way to zoom.

v2.81.1  2026-08-05
  #131 REGION MAP TOO SMALL AT 2x/3x - CLOSED, USER-CONFIRMED.
  Two coupled halves, both required:
    1. ApplyRegionIsoScale - the four .data isometric-basis floats
       0x00B0DBA4/A8/AC/B0 (+90.51/+18.75/-37.49/+45.25). 90.51+37.49 = 128.0
       EXACTLY: one region cell is 128 screen px at EVERY resolution, which IS
       the defect. 12 refs, all region code, none in the city view.
    2. ApplyRegionTileScale - MinHook on sub_7AE3D0 (__cdecl), the per-buffer
       builder inside sub_7AE510's rebuild. Enlarges each freshly-built tile
       bitmap before it returns, so the growth happens INSIDE the game's own
       rebuild. The composite (sized from [item+0x1C] at 0x007AE6D9) and the
       CLICK MASK ([item+0x44], built from [item+0x20] by sub_7AD400 later in
       the same rebuild) are therefore generated at the new size too.
  Also: retired v2.80.0's per-tick pass and removed 379 lines of dead
  scaffolding; demoted four probe lines to [dbg].

  Resized the tile buffers from our own region tick. It WORKED (9/9,

  climbed 9/18/27/36 unbounded, the tiles never changed on screen, and CITY

  Chased "Init returns 0" through four builds. Causes found only in v2.80/2.81:
  byte[buf+0x08] is a READY LATCH and Init refuses on any initialised buffer
  (Shutdown at vt+0x10 clears it); Init is (w,h,colorType,bpp) - FOUR args,
  ret 0x10 - we had been passing three plus a pointer; and the on-screen rect
  comes from the SOURCE buffer, not the composite. v2.79.1/.2/.3 each existed
  only because the previous build's log could not say WHICH branch failed -
  the instrument was the bug three times running.

SC4TouchControls - Version History
===================================

v2.77.0  2026-08-04  ** BORN-CORRECT DOCKING + the arrow fix extended to 2x **
  USER-CONFIRMED this session: "Amazing job on Graphs" and "Arrows are fixed".
  Two residuals from that same message, both closed here.
  1. #127 THE FIRST-OPEN JUMP. v2.76.0 ran kPanelDock only from the two
     SWEEPS, so a docked panel painted ONCE at its anchor's raw seat and
     snapped a tick later - the user: "when you open the city for the first
     time it jumps, which is something you have fixed dozens of times before".
     They are right, and it is the #50/#76 family verbatim: a window must be
     BORN correct, not corrected afterwards. ApplyPanelDocks is now ALSO
     called from ScaleOnShow - the cGZWin::SetFlag detour that fires the
     instant the game makes a window visible, BEFORE its first paint. New
     predicate IsPanelDockMember() gates it, so every present and future
     kPanelDock row inherits born-correct docking with no extra code. The
     SHOWHOOK may hand us either member of a pair (or a descendant), so the
     call walks up to a scope that can see both.
  2. #130 THE ARROW AT 2x, by user direction ("make sure you apply that fix to
     2x as well"). The gate was `factor >= 2.5f`, a v2.74.0 precaution taken
     while the model was UNPROVEN; the 3x run proved it. Now `factor > 1.01f`
     (still off at stock).
     WHY 2x CANNOT REGRESS - and this is a real argument, not an assurance:
     the detour is SELF-GATING ON THE DEFECT. It compares the arrow's LIVE
     L/T against the game's cached seat and writes only when they DIFFER (the
     `stale` field in the RATEANCHOR line). A correctly-seated 2x arrow has
     live == cached, so nothing is written. It also computes NO coordinates of
     its own - it writes back the game's own cached value - so there is no
     arithmetic that could be wrong at another tier.
  ALSO FIXED: the comment under that gate still called itself "THE f=2.00
  PROOF" after the gate widened - a comment describing behaviour the code no
  longer has, which is the law-48 defect (your own comment is an instrument).
  Corrected to say what the branch now does and where 2x safety really rests.
  GATES: all green; anchor-emu still reproduces the 2x golden 39/39, 0
  MISMATCH. Deployed 21:01:48, DatIntegrity ALL PASS.

v2.76.0  2026-08-04  ** kPanelDock: PANEL-TO-PANEL DOCKING AS A TABLE (user-directed) **
  USER DIRECTION: "ALL OF THE UI ELEMENTS SHOULD BE DOCKED VIA MAP." Correct,
  and it is the shape kMayorFlyoutDock already had. New kPanelDock table:
      { childId, anchorId, offX, offY, what }
      { 0x0A4A8176, 0x8A8B5B71, -2, 640, "graphs checkbox band" }
  driven by ApplyPanelDocks(), called from BOTH the full city sweep and the
  INCREMENTAL pass. That second call site is the actual fix for #127: a panel
  the user OPENS does not exist during the load-time sweep, which is why the
  v2.75.1 pin never fired even once (no GRAPHPIN line anywhere in the log).
  THE OFFSET LAW: offX/offY are measured in the ANCHOR's scaled pixels at the
  USER-CONFIRMED f=2 tier and applied as offset * (f/2) - identity at 2x by
  construction, so every row is bit-identical there. Same discipline as the
  disaster ring's seat-scaling (law 53). Idempotent: once seated the delta is
  0 and nothing is written, so a 16ms tick costs one compare per row.
  WHY A TABLE AND NOT A PIN: our per-panel anchor places each root from ITS
  OWN design gaps - right for a screen-edge dock, WRONG for a panel that must
  sit against a sibling. MEASURED: the game seats the Graphs band 1px left of
  the chart at 2400x1600 but 7px left at 3840x2160, and scaling each panel
  independently multiplies that native drift by f (18px left + 12px up at 3x,
  into the chart's bottom-right corner - the user's screenshot).
  ALSO: #130 ARMED (RatingArrowAnchor default 1 -> 2). The log-only run
  adjudicated the model exactly as intended: arrow at (294,174) against a
  cached seat of (98,58) = EXACTLY cached x3, alternating with correct fires.
  GATES: all green, 2x golden 39/39. Deployed 20:54:13.

v2.75.0  2026-08-04  ** #127 attempt 1 - the code NEVER RAN (wrong place) **
  First graphs pin, written INSIDE ScalePanelRoot's anchor block. It could
  never work: the band 0x0A4A8176 is anchored BEFORE the chart 0x8A8B5B71 and
  only once (state Fresh), so the chart's scaled frame was never known at that
  moment - the guard `gGraphChartSeen` was always false. The band did not move
  one pixel, which is exactly what the user reported ("Graphs did not move at

  captures); only the placement was wrong.

  EXECUTE. Two builds were spent proving the same arithmetic twice because the
  absence of the GRAPHPIN line was not treated as the primary evidence.

  Built from a 4-lane workflow, each lane's patch spec checked by its own

v2.73.0  2026-08-04  ** #126: THE DOCK MINIMAP "GARBAGE" IS OUR OWN ARTWORK **
  The user reported it three times, in caps the third time. Three wrong
  diagnoses preceded the right one; all three are recorded below because each
  was disproved by a MEASUREMENT, not by an opinion.
  WHAT IT ACTUALLY IS: the dock artwork sheet {46a006b0,13d14ca0} has a
  DECORATIVE FAKE MAP BAKED INTO THE MINIMAP RECESS. Measured by sampling the
  staged PNGs: at 1x a saturated terrain palette (water 57679A, greens
  7C9B00/75B564, yellow D8B910, tan 8D8259) occupies exactly x[18,82] y[71,135]
  = 64x64 = precisely the minimap's rect; outside it is bezel grey C7D6D9.
  In our 3x staged sheet (705x666) the same block measures (54,213)-(246,405)
  = 192x192. So at 3x the dock paints a 192 fake map, our real map is 128, and
  the user sees a 32px ring of BLURRY UPSCALED FAKE TERRAIN on all four sides.
  It is not stale pixels, not a stride tear, not a hole - it is live artwork we
  ship, painted correctly, into a recess that is now bigger than the image.
  This also explains why the Data Views map was clean: no baked map under it.
  THREE DEAD ENDS, each killed by measurement:
  - v2.72.2 dirtied the parent (vt+0x170, SetDirty-and-propagate). No fault, no
    effect - repainting the ring repaints the FAKE MAP. Correct call, wrong
    theory.
  - v2.72.3 walked the ancestors looking for a stale private paint buffer. The
    probe was INVALID: four ancestors reported the SAME pointer with cachedW=0.
    Two independent reasons it could never have worked - (a) the minimap's
    parent is the dock ROOT (winflag_pbuff=NO) and the pbuff=yes bitmap is a
    SIBLING, so the walk could not reach it; (b) our own REGRESSION had ALREADY
    measured [win+0x6c] as the DRAW CONTEXT, not a pixel buffer. Deleted, with
    a tombstone.
  - "the recess is a transparent hole" - also wrong. It is opaque fake terrain.
  FIX: stop snapping the dock and U-Drive-It instances (v2.72.1 shrank their
  windows to the image size - correct for #109, pointless here, and they were
  never on the crash path: the faulting chain 0x007A2F60 resolves id 0x4203,
  the Data Views map, EXCLUSIVELY). Let the window keep its full 192 and add a
  per-instance vtable-COPY draw hook on cSC4WinMiniMap slot +0x160 (override
  0x007A79B0, class vt 0x00AB83B8) that presents blitSize = the window edge for
  the duration of the original call. The override's live branch 0x007A7A81
  already builds a STRETCH blit - dest {+0x24,+0x28,+blitSize,+blitSize},
  source = the surface's FULL buffer area - so the game's own compositor scales
  128 -> 192 and the real map covers the fake one completely. Soft, which the
  user accepted in advance.
  GUARDS: (1) gate on raster [+0x114] != 0, because the override early-outs
  into the RECOMPUTE (0x7A79BB/0x7A79C6 -> 0x7A7840) which REALLOCS the raster
  FROM blitSize - a faked value there would resize the real buffer. (2) Restore
  [+0xE4] on EVERY exit path including the fault path: the message handler's
  transfer (0x007A86DC) also reads it as a copy extent, so a leaked 192 would
  over-copy. (3) Enlarge-only and square-only, so at 2x (window already ==
  blitSize) the hook is a NO-OP and that tier cannot regress.
  INSTALLED != EXECUTED (law 47): every ScaleAll pass now logs
  "minimap draw hook: installed=N stretched=N". A hook that installs and never
  fires can no longer be mistaken for a working one.
  ALTERNATIVE NOT TAKEN, recorded: edit the 3x dock sheet to blank the baked
  map out of the recess. That would keep the real map SHARP at 128 inside a
  neutral bezel instead of stretching it. It is an art/package change; revisit
  if the stretch reads too soft.
  GATES: all nine green. Deployed 18:54:27, DatIntegrity ALL PASS.
  AWAITING USER TEST.

v2.72.2  2026-08-04  ** THE STALE RING AROUND THE SNAPPED MAPS - REPAINT, NOT A BORDER **
  User report after v2.72.1: "the corrupted image is still behind it" - a ring
  of old picture around the dock minimap. The user asked for a BORDER to cover
  it. It is not corruption and it does not need covering.
  CAUSE: shrinking the window from curW to snap (192->128 dock, 768->512 Data
  Views) hands a ring of (curW-snap) px BACK TO THE PARENT, and nothing
  repaints it. Our InvalidateSelf() dirties only the map's NEW, smaller rect,
  so the parent's last paint of the LARGER area is still on screen. Stale, not
  corrupt - a region nobody owns any more.
  FIX: dirty the PARENT, because the vacated ring is the parent's pixels now.
  Our C++ InvalidateSelf() lands on slot 91 (0x0099BECC) and sets only THIS
  window's dirty byte. The game's own idiom for "and repaint my ancestors" is
  vt+0x170 (sub_99BED1) = SetDirty AND propagate - the same call the #57
  green-box proof identified as the ONLY thing that moves pixels, because the
  repaint gate is cIGZWin+0x70. Applied to the parent of the Data Views map
  and of the dock/U-Drive-It minimaps, inside __try with a loud Error line.
  WHY NOT THE BORDER THE USER ASKED FOR: a border would have painted OVER the
  stale region and left it there - masking the symptom, and a standing
  invitation to re-derive the same bug later. The repaint removes it. (If the
  user still wants a decorative border afterwards, that is a separate,
  cosmetic choice made against a CLEAN background.)
  KNOWN DUPLICATION, now tracked as #124: the snap logic exists twice -
  UiSpike::SnapMiniMapToBake and the DVMAP inline copy - and this release had
  to edit BOTH in lockstep, which is the drift risk arriving on schedule. They
  cannot merge yet: the DVMAP copy also publishes gDvMapClampBlit, which the
  DVPIN table entry reads (law 43, coupled pair), so that publish must be
  threaded through the helper first. Do it before the next change to either.
  GATES: all nine green. Deployed 17:44:50, DatIntegrity ALL PASS (24 dats +
  3 font sources + 2 DLLs + frozen-bundle hash + 26 deployed==built).
  AWAITING USER TEST: the ring around both maps should repaint to dock/panel
  artwork instead of stale picture.

v2.72.1  2026-08-04  ** #109 THE FAMILY: all three minimaps, not just the one that crashed **
  v2.72.0 stopped the crash and the user CONFIRMED it ("It didn't crash") -
  then immediately caught what the fix had left behind: "Map is shrunk in the
  middle as is the town minimap on the left." They were right, and it was the
  SAME defect one control over.
  MEASURED, same log, before AND after v2.72.0 - identical, so pre-existing at
  3x and NOT a regression:
      dock minimap    win 192x192  blitSize=128     <- still mismatched
      Data Views map  win 512x512  blitSize=512     <- fixed by v2.72.0
  and at 2x the dock reads 128/128, matched, exactly like the DV map. The
  #109 invariant belongs to the CLASS (cSC4WinMiniMap), not to one instance:
  all three windows are sized design*f while their surface is created at
  blitSize = terrainDim<<k, so at EVERY non-power-of-two tier every instance
  carries the mismatch. Only the Data Views one happened to reach the faulting
  fill first.
  FIX: the cure is now ONE shared helper, UiSpike::SnapMiniMapToBake, called
  for all three instances - Data Views map, HUD dock minimap, U-Drive-It
  dashboard twin. Snap the window to the largest exact power-of-two multiple
  of terrainDim within the bake ceiling, write blitSize directly (SetW/SetH
  bypass the SetArea override - the v2.69.9 stride tear), re-run the game's
  recompute, drive the bake, centre in the reserved slot, and store an
  AlreadyScaled record so the sweep cannot re-double it. Fixing one instance
  and leaving its siblings is how a family rots (law 43).
  THE SHRINK IS A HARD CONSTRAINT, NOT A BUG WE CAN FIX: the terrain bake can
  only produce an image at terrainDim << k. For a 64-cell tile that is
  {64, 128, 256, 512, 1024} and NOTHING ELSE. At 3x the UI asks for 192 (dock)
  and 768 (Data Views); neither is in the set, so at 3x the map can never
  exactly fill its frame. 2x has always looked right because 128 and 512 are
  both legal. Two honest options, and the user owns the choice:
    (A) SHIPPED - frame shrinks to match the image (dock 128, DV 512).
        Consistent, no empty margin, no crash, smaller than a 3x frame implies.
    (B) Go up one power of two (dock 256, DV 1024). Bigger than stock 3x, but
        the image then OVERFLOWS its slot into neighbouring furniture and needs
        real layout surgery on both the dock and the Data Views panel.
  DEAD END, recorded so it is not re-proposed: upscaling the baked image into a
  larger surface is the heal family #121 already killed - the game re-clears and
  ALPHA-BLENDS the map every sim-day, so anything we paint over it is wiped
  within a second.
  KNOWN DUPLICATION: the DVMAP block still carries its own inline copy of this
  logic (v2.72.0, verified working on screen). It was deliberately NOT
  refactored in the same build that had just been user-confirmed. Consolidating
  it onto the helper is owed - see the note in the DVMAP block.
  GATES: all nine green. Deployed 17:22:36, DatIntegrity ALL PASS (24 dats +
  3 font sources + 2 DLLs + frozen-bundle hash + 26 deployed==built).
  AWAITING USER TEST: expect a MINIMAP window SNAPPED 192x192 -> 128 line
  beside the Data Views one.

v2.72.0  2026-08-04  ** #109 FIXED: THE FRACTIONAL-TIER DATA-VIEW CRASH **
  THE CAUSE WE HAD WRITTEN DOWN FOR TWO WEEKS WAS WRONG, and the evidence
  that settles it had been on disk the whole time: THE GAME WRITES ITS OWN
  EXCEPTION REPORTS to Documents\SimCity 4\Exception Reports\. Twelve of
  them exist. FIVE fault at the IDENTICAL instruction - 0x00910010,
  ACCESS_VIOLATION, the `rep stosd` inside the game's row fill - at 1.5x
  (12:36:27) and at 3x (13:38:40, 14:44:52, 15:29:10) alike, and never at
  2x. One tier crashes, one does not, same instruction: that is a geometry
  invariant, and it is measurable.
  THE OLD DIAGNOSIS (every doc repeated it): "blitSize is not a power-of-two
  multiple of terrainDim, so the blit overruns the raster in stock code."
  REFUTED BY THE CAPTURES: blitSize measured EXACT at BOTH crashing tiers -
  1.5x 256 = 64<<2, 3x 512 = 64<<3 - and our own clips alarm read 0. The
  384/768 numbers everyone quoted are the WINDOW size, not blitSize.
  THE REAL INVARIANT is one level out. The window and the surface disagree:
      tier   window   blitSize/surface   result
      1.50   384      256                CRASH
      2.00   512      512                fine
      3.00   768      512                CRASH
  The window is ScaleRound(256, f); the surface is created AT blitSize, which
  snaps to a power-of-two multiple of terrainDim. Those agree only when f is
  ITSELF a power of two. Everything downstream that takes its EXTENT from the
  window rect and its STRIDE from the surface then walks off the end - in the
  GAME'S code, which is why no __except of ours ever fired and the log simply
  stops mid-sweep.
  FIX: the DVMAP sizing block is no longer a fallback for a declined bake -
  it is THE SIZING POLICY, and it runs at every tier. The window is snapped
  to the largest EXACT power-of-two multiple of terrainDim that fits both the
  scaled window and the bake ceiling (terrainDim x8 with the #121 patch live,
  x4 without), and blitSize/surface follow it. window == surface at every
  tier, exactly as it already is at 2x.
  BIT-IDENTICAL AT 2x: 512 is already the answer there (largest power-of-two
  multiple of 64 within min(512, 512)), so the tier that works cannot regress
  - the gate for this whole family.
  THE TRADE, STATED PLAINLY: at 3x on a 64-cell tile the map is 512 in a slot
  the layout reserved for 768 - centred, correct, still 2x the stock size. A
  non-power-of-two map size is NOT AVAILABLE from this bake at all; the
  alternative here is the crash, not a bigger map. (Contrast #121, where the
  alternative to a smaller map WAS a bigger one, and the user rightly
  rejected the trade.)
  COUPLED PAIR (law 43), already wired: gDvMapClampBlit publishes the snapped
  size and the DVPIN table entry targets the SAME number, so the sweep cannot
  re-double the map behind the policy.
  PROCESS: the assistant burned ~50 minutes of the user's time on a five-lens
  workflow before reading a crash report the game had already written. The
  workflow DID find it - but the first move should have been "does the game
  leave a crash artefact?", not "let us reason about the bytes". Our own law
  says measure, don't infer; a faulting EIP is a MEASUREMENT and it was free.
  GATES: all nine green (MutationCountInvariant + negative control,
  ShippingIniKeys, MiniMapX8Bake + both controls, PackageGating, crosscheck
  262/262 0 MISSED 0 DEFERRED 10 SKIPPED, SubRingLock 311,
  BornCorrectCoverage 51/51, ScaleTierDecide 14+5000x2, ChartLegendMath 32).
  Deployed 16:31, DatIntegrity ALL PASS (24 dats + 3 font sources + 2 DLLs +
  frozen-bundle hash + 26 deployed==built). AWAITING USER TEST at 3x and 1.5x.

v2.71.8  2026-08-04  ** 3x: disaster ring seat scales with the tier **
  First 3x run (3840x2160, tier 3.00) reported the God-mode disaster
  ring "not 1:1 on the disaster flyout being docked". Root cause: the
  v2.71.6 RingDXEff/RingDYEff correction scaled by (f-1), anchoring it at
  f=1 = the game's own 1x anchor d[]=(0,138). But d[] is the UNDOCKED
  stock seat - the ring sits on the disaster button only once the dock
  runs, and the dock IS a scaled placement (gDisDockL/T = tbLive +
  ScaleRound(Dock, f), both proportional to f). For the ring to stay on
  the button its buffer offset must scale the same way, so the (f-1) law
  drifted at 3x: it parked the ring at (32,444) where the seat is (24,437)
  - 8px right and 7px low. Fix: SEAT-SCALING. Keep the ring's CENTRE at
  its f=2 docked seat scaled by f/2 (centre @f=2 = d[]+RingDX/DY+(sw,sh),
  scale that by f/2, subtract the scaled half-size). Bit-identical at f=2
  (even-int RoundHalfUp, exact /2). Retires the RingDXEff/RingDYEff
  helpers; the law now lives inline at the disaster ring blit (it needs
  the sprite size). NOTE for the 1.5 tier: the same law nudges the 1.5x
  seat from the v2.71.6 (8,77) correction to (13,81) - a +5/+4 move -
  because the (f-1) law's f=1 anchor was never a docked state; re-verify
  1600x1200 if that bench returns. Mayor-mode flyouts measured CLEAN at
  3x this session: every kMayorFlyoutDock entry docked to the same x=33
  and the user re-checked them aligned (the emergency flyout is exempt
  from the shared path - it paints its own 496px dispatch panel).
  GATES: all green (selfcheck, ChartLegendMath 32, byte gate 127,
  crosscheck 262/262 0 MISSED 0 DEFERRED 10 SKIPPED, BornCorrectCoverage

  repo at this root to diff against (and the qwen CLI wrapper is absent).
  USER-CONFIRMED at 3x ("Working great") - the disaster ring docks 1:1.
  FOLLOW-UP 2026-08-04: this release ALSO closed #122 (the v2.71.7 hover-break)
  without anyone writing a fix for it. The user re-tested at 3x: ring seated,
  bar intact through the hover. v2.71.7 added only a diagnostic, so the sole
  functional change between the report and the cure was this seat-scaling -
  #122 and the 3x dock misplacement were ONE bug with two triggers (the open,

v2.71.6  2026-08-04  ** 1.5x: disaster ring docked again **
  First user run at the 1.5 tier (1920x1080, then 1600x1200; both boot
  tier 1.50 because the 2x package needs 1760x1116) reported two defects
  in the God-mode Create Disaster flyout: the ring sat BELOW its seat on
  the toolbar's disaster button, and the orange bar looked "broken into
  2 pieces". Both are ONE bug. The game blits the ring at its 1x anchor
  into the tier-scaled container buffer, and [Disaster] RingDX/RingDY are
  the correction from that anchor to the scaled seat - tuned at f=2, where
  the correction equals the anchor itself. The draw added them RAW, so at
  f=1.5 the ring carried a full 2x correction: one correction too low
  (RingDY 153 applied where ~77 belongs), which also dropped the ring
  across the bar's middle and split it visually. Fix: RingDXEff/RingDYEff
  scale the ini value by (gTierF - 1) - the correction-over-anchor form.
  Bit-identical at f=2 (RoundHalfUp of an int), zero at stock. DockX/DockY
  were already tier-scaled (ScaleRound) and the bar widen/shift already
  derive from the tier factor - the ring offsets were the one 2x-only
  constant left in the disaster draw. Captures: the two tier-15 session
  logs under _tests\captures (2026-08-04-1920x1080/1600x1200-tier15).
  GATES: all green (selfcheck, ChartLegendMath 32, byte gate 127,

v2.71.4  2026-08-04  ** RETIRED THE DEAD DATA-VIEWS FALLBACKS: dock-seed + per-sweep heal DELETED **
  Handoff task 1 (HANDOFF-TO-QWEN.md). The x8 bake patch (#121, v2.71.1)
  made the GAME bake a real terrain base at zoom -3; the two workarounds
  that existed to paper over that gap were gated off in v2.71.2 but left
  in the tree. They are now deleted, not gated - v2.71.2 measured the seed
  actively HURTING once the bake was live (it overwrote a correct hidden
  bake with a blurry 128->512 dock upscale), and a fallback that can only
  fight the primary path is a bug, not a safety net. UiSpike.cpp 13,924 ->
  13,636 lines.
  DELETED:
  - The ZOOM-CLIFF dock-seed (v2.69.5): gDvMapSeedPend / gDvMapSeedTries,
    the arming in the first-visible kick, the whole seed execution block
    (dock CaptureSurface + RestoreSurfaceBilinear into the DVMAP surface,
    SEEDED / GAVE UP / FAULTED / heal-cache-too-small log paths).
  - The per-sweep heal (v2.69.6/v2.70.0): gDvSeedMaint / gDvSeedMaintTick /
    gDvBaseCache / gDvBaseCacheN / kDvBaseCacheMax, the maint-probe
    diagnostic (gDvProbeDiag), the HEALED / DISARMED / FAULTED log paths.
    gDvSeedStash + kDvSeedStashMax went with it - the stash had been
    unwired since v2.70.0 (nothing wrote it), so it was already dead.
  - The six Disarm() resets for the above (#92-law clears kept for what
    survives).
  KEPT, deliberately: the bake-ceiling CLAMP (kDvClampEnabled /
  gDvMapClampBlit + the DVPIN coupling at law 43) - it is the LIVE
  fallback for an exe build the x8 patch declines on. DriveMiniMapBake,
  the first-visible kick, and gCarryPix (shared scratch of all three
  surface blocks) untouched. CaptureSurface / RestoreSurfaceBilinear are

v2.71.3  2026-08-04  ** #119 + #118: TWO PACKAGE-GATING DEFECTS IN SHIPPING CODE **
  Both were live in what we would have handed a stranger. Neither is visible
  on our own machine, which is exactly why they survived.
  - #119 WARRIORUI WAS NEVER GATED. z_SC4UIScale_WarriorUI carried a
    kThirdPartyDeps row (ScaleTier.cpp:148, EXACT NAME+SIZE on UI_Compact.dat
    8702 / Mayor_Sign_Menu.dat 5766), so its depOk was computed on every boot -
    and then DISCARDED, because no SyncDat call ever asked for it. It was
    therefore never TIER-gated (it stayed active at the stock tier, where every
    other package is stashed) and never MOD-gated (it stayed active with
    warrior's mod REMOVED - the one state its own comment says must disable us,
    since our copy hard-codes that mod's rects). FIX: the missing SyncDat call.
    VERIFIED ON DISK after deploy - it now carries -15x/-3x .x1-disabled twins
    exactly like its three siblings, where before it had none at all.
  - #118 THE FONT BLOCK WAS DESTROYING THE FILE IT EXISTED TO PROTECT. The
    v2.68.0 preservation snapshotted the live FontStyle.ini as .user-original
    whenever no snapshot existed - which is TRUE on the first launch after
    upgrading from ANY earlier build of this mod, because by then the live file
    is already OUR scaled font. We preserved our own 2x font as "the user's
    original", then faithfully restored a 2x font over their file at stock
    tier, destroying the evidence on the way.
    FIX: MatchesAnyTierFontSource() - we SHIP the tier fonts, so a live file
    byte-identical to one of them is ours BY CONSTRUCTION; the snapshot is
    taken only when it matches NONE. Byte compare (new FilesIdentical), not
    size: all three tier fonts are 23,016 bytes. Not snapshotting is the SAFE
    outcome - with no .user-original the stock tier moves our file aside
    instead of restoring a wrong one, leaving the user where they were.
    RESIDUAL, STATED: anyone who already ran an affected build may have a
    .user-original holding our font. This prevents new occurrences; it cannot
    recover a file already overwritten. Release-notes material.
  - NEW GATE _tests\Test-PackageGating.py: every kThirdPartyDeps row must have
    a SyncDat call; every gate must name ITS OWN package (the v2.38.3
    index-vs-name bug in name form); the #118 font guard must exist, be ON the
    snapshot path, and compare CONTENT. Positive control + BOTH negative
    controls RUN (deleting the WarriorUI call reproduces #119; bypassing the
    guard reproduces #118); source restored byte-identical afterwards (cmp).
  - PROCESS: the first negative-control attempt silently did nothing - /tmp
    does not exist in this Windows git-bash, so three "ALL PASS" lines were the
    UNDAMAGED file passing three times. A negative control that does not run is
    indistinguishable from one that passes. Redone in the scratchpad.
  - GATES: all nine green incl. crosscheck 262/262 0 MISSED, DatIntegrity
    ALL PASS. Deployed + hash-verified.

v2.71.2  2026-08-04  ** #121 CLOSED: THE GAME'S OWN BAKE, EXTENDED TO x8 (.0/.1 folded in) **
  USER-CONFIRMED "Perfect!" - the Data Views map now fills its full 512
  slot on a 64-cell tile, correct colours, no ~3s flash, no jump on open.
  This closes the family that v2.69.4 through v2.70.0 kept failing to fix
  from the outside; the cure was to make the GAME's bake work, not to
  repair its output.
  THE CAUSE, read off the shipped exe (SimCity 4.exe 1.1.641 Steam,
  7,876,608 bytes; every VA below re-verified by
  _tests\Test-MiniMapX8Bake.py and again at runtime before any write):
  - 0x7A852C mov edx,[ebx+0x104] (zoom). The bake's DEST MATH is fully
    general in zoom - destY = cellY*16 >> (zoom+4) at 0x7A853D/0x7A8540,
    tile side = 256 >> (zoom+4) at 0x7A855E. Nothing there stops at -3.
  - The DISPATCH does: 0x7A8560 lea ecx,[edx+2] (index = zoom+2),
    0x7A8563 cmp ecx,4 (5-entry table), 0x7A8566 ja 0x7A85B0 - UNSIGNED,
    so zoom -3 computes 0xFFFFFFFF and takes the skip; 0x7A8568
    jmp [ecx*4+0x7A8628]. Table 0x7A8628 = { 0x7A858B, 0x7A8584,
    0x7A857D, 0x7A8576, 0x7A856F } = x4up / x2up / 1:1 / /2 / /4. There
    is no x8 entry. After skipping, the bake clears the dirty mask and
    reports done - that is the silence.
  - Stock can never reach -3 (max stock blit 256 / min terrain 64 = -2);
    only OUR resized 512 surface gets there. The data CELLS paint fine at
    -3 because their loop (0x7A882A, shl/shr by zoom+4) has NO table and
    NO bound - which is exactly why the symptom read as "data on black".
  - And why every post-hoc pixel repair had to fail: the surface is
    re-cleared to 0xFF000000 and repainted EVERY SIM-DAY tick, and the
    game ALPHA-BLENDS the cells onto whatever base exists at paint time.
    With a black base the cells are BORN dark; no later pass can
    un-blend them. The base must exist BEFORE the cell paint.
  THE FIX, three parts:
  - v2.71.0 CodePatches::ApplyMiniMapX8Bake - 15 bytes, IN-MEMORY ONLY
    (the exe on disk is never written): index becomes (zoom+3) against a
    6-entry table held in our DLL. Entries 1..5 are the game's own five
    stub VAs in their original relative order, so zoom -2..+2 is
    BIT-IDENTICAL; entry 0 is ours -> an x8 tile blitter. zoom <= -4 and
    >= +3 keep the stock skip. The only behavioural delta in the whole
    reachable space is zoom -3: skip -> draw.
    GUARDS: 15 (dispatch) + 33 (stub block) + 20 (table) bytes verified
    before ANY write, declining loudly on mismatch (wrong build, or
    another mod took the site first); never writes at factor <= 1.01;
    raster clip inside the blitter with a gX8Clips alarm; gX8Blits is an
    EXECUTED counter, not an installed one (law 47).
    BLAST RADIUS, enumerated by full-image scan. NOTE THE SCOPE: the gate
    asserts ONLY the table-VA item (check [5]); the other three below were
    verified by hand and re-verified by an independent audit scan, and are
    NOT covered by any automated check. The bake 0x7A7FF0
    has exactly ONE caller (0x7A8721); the table 0x7A8628 is referenced
    exactly ONCE in .text (0x7A856B - inside the very instruction being
    replaced); no branch target lands in the 15-byte window; stubs and
    blitters are not modified.
  - v2.71.1 UiSpike::DriveMiniMapBake - the recompute at 0x7A7840 does
    not paint: it reallocates, recomputes zoom, memsets the dirty-tile
    mask to all-0xFF (0x7A78E2) and sets fd=1. The paint is
    MESSAGE-DRIVEN (handler 0x7A8640 -> bake 0x7A7FF0), so ours landed
    AFTER the panel was already on screen and the user watched it fill
    in. We now call the game's own bake synchronously right after the
    recompute, while the panel is still hidden. Idempotent (the bake
    clears the mask and fd, so the later message finds nothing to do),
    SEH-guarded, falls back to the message path on fault.
  - v2.71.2 - the LAST jump was OURS. The v2.69.5 dock-seed still fired
    on open and overwrote the correctly-baked terrain with a blurry
    128->512 bilinear upscale of the dock minimap: good map, worse map,
    re-bake. CodePatches.h already SAID these fallbacks "must stand down"
    when the bake is live; the condition had never been wired. Now gated
    on !CodePatches::MiniMapX8Active() - seed and clamp both. (Standing
    law: your own comment is an instrument, and that one described code
    that did not exist.)
  MEASURED, final run: x8bake=live blits=16 clips=0 fd=0 - 16 tiles is
  exactly 4x4 for a 64-cell city, painted while hidden - with SEEDED 0 /
  maint probes 0 / HEALED 0 / CLAMPED 0 / faults 0. Every workaround from
  the v2.69.x chain is dormant, which is the proof that the patch, not a
  leftover, is carrying the picture.
  SUPERSEDES v2.69.10's "ACCEPTED RESIDUE" (256 map in the 512 slot on
  small tiles): that residue existed only because the bake stopped at x4.
  The clamp is retained unchanged as the FALLBACK when the patch declines.
  CLOSED APPROACHES - the expensive part, recorded so they are not
  re-proposed:
  - v2.69.4 first-visible kick: fired on every city -> ruled OUT
    visibility as the cause. Kept (it fixes a lesser, real disease).
  - v2.69.5 one-shot dock-surface seed: correct on first open, black
    after - the game re-clears every sim-day.
  - v2.69.6 30-sweep black-hole heal: silent no-op.
  - v2.69.7 diagnostic: proved (a) the re-clear is ~1 Hz, (b) the game's
    black is 0xFF000000, not numeric 0 - so the heal's test had NEVER
    fired.
  - v2.70.0 per-sweep heal from cache: WRONG CELL COLOURS + flash. The
    alpha-blend order makes this whole family unfixable in principle.
  - v2.69.8/.9/.10 bake-ceiling clamp (map at 256, centred): correct and
    stable, but the user rejected the size trade; kept as the fallback.
    Its own three failures are already recorded below.
  - REFUSED (unchanged): a global blt-stretch hook - blast radius (every
    blt in the game pays) and the hit box would stop matching the sprite.
  PROCESS FACTS:
  - The STOCK CONTROL decided it TWICE, ~2 minutes each, with no build:
    first that the black map was ours, then that the open-jump was ours -
    the latter AFTER the assistant had suggested it was probably stock.
  - Test-MiniMapX8Bake.py FAILED ITS OWN POSITIVE CONTROL on the first
    run: it searched for the bake VA as an imm32, but the bake is reached
    by call rel32, so its absolute address never appears in the image.
    The GATE was wrong, not the exe. Control changed to a blitter VA that
    check [2] independently proves IS an imm32.
  - crosscheck.py immediately flagged the 4 new sites as unknown to the
    offline model. Correct, and correctly resolved: they are CONTROL
    FLOW, not geometry. Classified PERMANENT out-of-scope with reason +
    falsifier (as kPopupStyleRetargets already is), adjudicated by their
    own dedicated gate. Result: 262/262, 0 MISSED, 10 skipped.
  - The chain cost ~13 builds because the assistant iterated against the
    user's eyes instead of disassembling first. The disassembly answered
    it in one pass. Our own law had already said so: measure, don't infer.
  INHERITED, STILL OPEN (#109): the bake's addressing assumes
  blit == terrain << -zoom EXACTLY. Inexact sizes (1.5x's 384, 3x's 768)
  overrun the raster in STOCK code - including the unbounded data-cells
  loop - which IS the 1.5x data-view crash, and it exists independent of
  this patch. Any sizing policy must select ONLY exact power-of-two
  multiples of terrainDim; gX8Clips is the alarm that says it leaked.

v2.69.3  2026-08-04  ** THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEW EARNS ITS COST: THREE v2.69.0 REGRESSIONS FIXED **
  A 57-agent adversarial review of the v2.69.x hardening (every finding
  independently refuted before acceptance; 46 raised, 8 survived) found THREE
  regressions in the changes this same session shipped. All fixed here.
  - R1 (BLOCKER, use-after-free): the #117 O(n^2) gate RE-BASELINED its
    mutation signal whenever a verify ran. Unsound: a verify proves liveness
    of ONE pointer (the current index), never of the remainder, so crediting
    later indices with it reintroduced the crash the verify exists to kill.
    Kill sequence: i=0 mutates, the game tears down c1 AND c2; i=1 verifies
    (c1 dead, continue) and consumed the signal; i=2 saw the count unchanged,
    SKIPPED its check, dereferenced freed c2. The old code was safe because
    every i>0 re-verified. FIX: the five mid-loop consumption lines deleted -
    the baseline is taken once, before the loop, and never advanced. The skip
    window is only the provably-safe prefix before the first mutation; the
    steady state ("everything already scaled") still costs zero enumerations,
    which is where the whole O(n^2) win lived. The refuting agent itself
    proposed this minimal form.
  - R2 (BLOCKER, reached ON THIS MACHINE): v2.69.0's `if (gProbeOn)` gate
    around the "DPROBE block" was justified by "verified zero mutations
    inside; pure instrument". FALSE - and the verifying scan had NO POSITIVE
    CONTROL: its regex matched Set*/GZWinMoveTo CALLS while the block's real
    mutations are raw vtable swaps (the DISASTER DRAW HOOK installs,
    gVtCopy/gVtCopy2), gDisasterDrawTuning=1, and InvalidateSelfAndParents().
    That walk IS the per-tick re-find the runtime-image lever requires for
    TRANSIENT disaster-flyout windows. With the dev ini's [Probe] Enabled=0,
    v2.69.0-v2.69.2 killed the disaster ring/bar 2x draw fix on this very
    machine. FIX: gate reverted (bare scope restored; the log lines inside
    were always per-line gated). Also gProbeOn's COMPILED DEFAULT flipped
    1 -> 0: a shipped install (clean ini, no [Probe] section) no longer runs
    the MPROBE walk at 60 Hz; the dev ini opts back in explicitly.
    Lesson recorded: my own "zero mutations" scan violated the null-is-not-
    evidence law - it never proved it COULD see a vtable store.
  - R3 (diagnostic): the BMPX 40-line budget was burned at every LogLevel
    while the rows (Debug since v2.69.0) were dropped, so a LogLevel=1 log
    carried "saturated at 40 lines" with ZERO rows above it - an instrument
    announcing data it never produced. Budget + saturation sentence now sit
    behind IsEnabled(Debug).
  - The same review's sweeps produced a ranked worklist (B3-B7, S1-S10) held
    in tasks - notably: SyncFont can snapshot OUR OWN scaled font as the
    "user original" on upgrade installs; WarriorUI has a kThirdPartyDeps row
    but NO SyncDat call (its depOk is computed and discarded); licensing
    still absent; WebRedirect=0 leaves the WebText tooltip dat live (split
    coupled pair).
  - GATES: MutationCountInvariant PASS (negative control tripped),
    DatIntegrity ALL PASS, deployed + hash-verified.
  - EYES-ON OWED (unchanged from v2.69.0, now doubly so): rapid menu
    switching must not crash, scaled-window counts unchanged vs v2.68.0, AND
    the disaster flyout ring/bar must draw 2x again (the R2 regression's
    visible symptom).

v2.69.2  2026-08-04  ** MINHOOK OWNERSHIP + THE EMULATOR AT FULL SCOPE **
  DLL: two ScaleRemap fixes, invisible in the shipped default config but
  load-bearing the moment [Scaling] UseScaleRemap=1 is set.
  - MINHOOK IS A SHARED SINGLETON AND SCALEREMAP TREATED IT AS PRIVATE.
    Five modules in this DLL initialize MinHook (UiSpike x4, WebRedirect,
    ScaleRemap); the touch DLL compiles NO MinHook at all (verified from its
    vcxproj), so the "two DLLs" worry dissolves into a sharper one INSIDE
    this DLL:
    (a) ScaleRemap:386 was the ONE init site that treated
        MH_ERROR_ALREADY_INITIALIZED as failure. WebRedirect installs
        unconditionally at every tier, so if it ran first, ScaleRemap
        silently never hooked. Now tolerated, same as every other site.
    (b) ScaleRemap::RemoveHooks ran MH_DisableHook(MH_ALL_HOOKS) +
        MH_Uninitialize() at SHUTDOWN 2/3 - ripping out UiSpike's four
        game-code hooks and WebRedirect's ShellExecute pair mid-teardown
        and freeing the SHARED trampoline pool under them (the #104 spin
        thread can be live exactly then; the shutdown probe's own legend
        records "SHUTDOWN 2/3 ... (MinHook) hung" as an observed outcome).
        The shipped default only survived because UseScaleRemap=0 keeps the
        path disarmed. RemoveHooks now tracks the targets ScaleRemap itself
        hooked and disables/removes exactly those; MinHook stays initialized
        for the process lifetime.
  OFFLINE MODEL (tools only, no DLL behavior): Phase 1 CLOSED.
  - crosscheck.py: 251 adjudicated + 8 DEFERRED + 9 SKIPPED
    -> 262 adjudicated (262 PASS, 0 MISS) + 0 DEFERRED + 6 PERMANENT.
  - The 8 #57 deferrals: closed by the PRESCRIBED procedure, which exposed a
    real census bug on the way - promoting a builder did not invalidate the
    resume unit, so the first regeneration served STALE rows and honestly
    derived 0 constants (the same failure class as #58's stale dat). Census
    units now fold an owner-set digest into their resume keys.
  - Two new TIGHT recorders in geomextra.py, allowlist-scoped like the
    existing ones: D (imm arithmetic reaching an object-member store - the
    five legend immediates) and E (imm applied to a stack-pair difference,
    a width by construction - the three rebuilt blocks). 0x76E2C8 derived by
    BOTH from different instruction shapes; collapse_by_va confirms the
    bytes agree. 9 double-derived sites total, 9 agree.
  - kRatingImulSites: resolved by promotion + slot 0xCC (SetW, per
    SC4-UI-ENGINE.md:249) allowlisted for 0x7E8510 ONLY - 0xCC on the legend
    iface is a DIFFERENT method, so the slot went into the per-owner
    foreign-slot table, not global VT_NAME (the fabrication trap), and
    FOREIGN_SLOT_OWNERS now grants (owner, slot) PAIRS, not owners.
  - kTipWrapSites + kPopupStyleRetargets: PERMANENT out-of-scope with
    MEASURED reasons and stated falsifiers (the tip wrap feeds a text-
    measure call on the HTML engine, slot +0x1C on a non-window object; the
    retargets swap font-style GUIDs, not geometry).
  - crosscheck --selftest: guards now exercised against a SYNTHETIC deferral
    so the machinery stays tested while the real table is empty.
  - #108 SWEPT (18-agent workflow): plan in _packaging\PUBLISH-PLAN-108.md,
    final .gitignore-PROPOSED replaced. 451 real PII hits (old 584 stale);
    76%% die with three directory exclusions; 54-item scrub worklist; 6
    NEEDS-HUMAN questions pending.
  - GATES: DatIntegrity ALL PASS (deployed==built), crosscheck 262/262 +
    selftest 7/7, all 5 emu gates exit 0, coverage_rederive PASS,
    MutationCountInvariant + ShippingIniKeys PASS.

v2.69.1  2026-08-04  ** #111 REFUTED: THE PACKAGE SELECTOR WAS NEVER WRONG **
  NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGE. Two log lines and two new offline gates.
  - #111 CLOSED AS REFUTED. The open ticket said package selection read the ini
    ScaleFactor instead of the computed tier, shipping 2x art on 1.5x geometry.
    It does not, and the ticket said so honestly ("CAUSE (hypothesis) ... Not yet
    confirmed in source"). Confirming it first was right; it is wrong:
      * SC4UIScaleDllDirector.cpp:196  settings.spikeScaleFactor = tier;
      * SC4UIScaleDllDirector.cpp:224  ScaleTier::SyncStaticLayers(settings.spikeScaleFactor);
        Same `if (spikeAutoScale)` condition, 196 BEFORE 224.
      * ScaleTier.cpp reads the ini NOWHERE - grep for GetPrivateProfile or
        ScaleFactor in that file returns ZERO hits.
      * SyncStaticLayers matches packages only on its own parameter (:571-572).
      * Every other consumer - CodePatches x9 (:304-342), the tier line (:500),
        ~15 UiSpike sites - runs after :196. The ONLY read before it is the
        settings-dump log line itself.
      * Corroborated independently by the deployed state: at tier 2.0 the -2x
        dats are active and the -15x/-3x copies carry .x1-disabled.
  - SO WHAT WENT WRONG? THE INSTRUMENT. The log showed "Settings: factor=2.00"
    next to "AutoScale: tier 1.50" and that was read as one contradictory state.
    They are not simultaneous: the settings dump is at :103, the AutoScale block
    at :192, ~90 lines apart in the SAME constructor. One prints the ini REQUEST;
    the other prints what replaces it. FIFTH time in this project a confident
    diagnosis rested on an instrument whose provenance nobody checked (cf. #103,
    #98 twice, the #54 denominator). Standing law: your own log line is an
    instrument.
    FIX: :103 now reads "Settings (as READ FROM INI, before the tier decision)"
    and, when AutoScale=1, prints an explicit follow-up saying that value is
    about to be REPLACED and naming the tier line as the effective one. :224 now
    logs "Static layers: selecting the x%.2f package" immediately before the
    call, so "which art is live" is answerable directly instead of by inference.
  - CONSEQUENCE: #109's "investigate #111 first" instruction is DISCHARGED. The
    1.5x data-view crash is NOT "1.5x geometry meeting 2x art" - the 1.5x tier
    really does select the -15x art. #109 must be investigated on its own.
  - NEW GATE _tests\Test-MutationCountInvariant.py. v2.69.0's O(n^2) fix rests
    on "every window mutation in ScalePanelRoot/ScaleSubtree increments the
    scale count" - which until now was enforced by ONE human read of the code.
    Add a SetW without a count++ and the sweep skips a liveness verify it needs;
    the rapid-menu-switch crash returns later, elsewhere, with nothing in the
    log. The gate asserts all 8 mutation sites sit within 12 lines of an
    increment ON EITHER SIDE (ScaleSubtree's third cluster increments 5 lines
    BEFORE the writes - a forward-only search nearly mis-cleared it), and it
    runs a REAL NEGATIVE CONTROL: it deletes an increment from an in-memory copy
    and fails if the check does not reject it.
  - NEW GATE _tests\Test-ShippingIniKeys.py. The shipped user ini may not
    document a key the DLL does not read, and may not carry a BOM. It caught its
    own false failure on ScaleFactor (read via a GetPrivateProfileFloat helper
    with no trailing W) - the gate was wrong about the code, not the ini.
  - GATES: BornCorrectCoverage 51/51, ScaleTierDecide, SubRingLock 311,
    DatIntegrity ALL PASS, ShippingIniKeys 6/6, MutationCountInvariant 8/8 with
    its negative control tripping. Deployed + hash-verified.

v2.69.0  2026-08-04  ** RELEASE HARDENING: PER-TICK COST + SILENT TRUNCATION **
  NO VISIBLE CHANGE INTENDED. Every item here is cost, noise or honesty; the
  on-screen result must be byte-identical to v2.68.0. Adjudicator for the whole
  build: the log's scaled-window counts UNCHANGED, and rapid menu switching
  (the #117 repro) does not crash.
  - #117 O(n^2) LIVENESS RE-VERIFICATION, GATED (5 sites: ScalePanelsUnder,
    ScalePanelRoot, DialogDockTick, ScaleMenuFlyouts, ScaleSubtree).
    Each loop re-enumerated the WHOLE child list once PER CHILD, at every level
    of an 8-deep recursion, on a 16 ms tick.
    * The obvious fix - hoist the enumeration out of the loop - is WRONG, and
      the site's own comment says why: the list is re-read because OUR OWN
      WRITES can make the game destroy a later sibling. A pre-loop snapshot is
      stale by construction and the crash returns under rapid menu switching.
      A "do not hoist" note is now written at the ScaleSubtree site.
    * The sound gate: re-verify only when the PREVIOUS iteration actually
      mutated something. Nothing else runs inside the stack frame (single UI
      thread, our code never pumps), so if we touched nothing the child list
      cannot have changed.
    * The mutation signal is the EXISTING count, not a new flag: every
      SetW/SetH/GZWinMoveTo in ScalePanelRoot (:11240-11242) and ScaleSubtree
      (:13104/:13129/:13159-13163) is paired with a count++ - verified site by
      site, not assumed. A new hand-maintained flag could silently miss a
      mutation; this counter cannot, because it is the same number the
      "%d windows scaled" log lines are read from.
    * Steady state ("everything already scaled") now costs ~0 enumerations.
  - DPROBE WALKED EVERY TICK WITH THE PROBE OFF. Only its LOG LINE was gated on
    gProbeOn; the 312-line subtree walk (512-frame stack) ran regardless, for
    #80, which is closed. Verified zero mutations in the block before gating it,
    so switching it off cannot change what the user sees.
  - THREE POINTER-KEYED STATICS NEVER CLEARED (mPrev, prevGeom, probedOnce).
    Same defect #92 fixed for the gauge latches: at city teardown every key
    dangles, and a second city landing on a recycled address reads as "not new",
    so the probe silently reports nothing. All three now clear on gGaugeEpoch.
  - LIVE-TUNE INI POLL: 48 GetPrivateProfileStringA calls every 20 sweeps
    (~144 ini reads/second) for a lever only WE use. Now reads ONCE at startup -
    so a user's overrides still apply exactly as before - and keeps polling only
    when [UiSpike] LiveTune=1.
  - LOG VOLUME: 14 per-window TREE DUMP rows moved Info -> Debug (RGKID, VWKID,
    MWKID, POPKID, BHDR child rows, DBVT, BMPX draw rows). MEASURED, not guessed:
    a tag census of a live session showed RGKID+VWKID alone were 575 of ~800
    lines. Event lines (surface created/failed, N instances hooked) stay at Info,
    so LogLevel=1 is now a readable log and LogLevel=2 restores every instrument.
  - SILENT TRUNCATION NAMED. ScaleSubtree's kMaxDepth(8)/kMaxWindows(1500) and
    PurgeSubtreeRecords' depth cap all stopped without a word - the #53 failure
    class, where the log reads clean because the instrument never saw the
    dropped windows. Each now logs once per city, per cap.
  - WEBREDIRECT IS DECLINABLE. The dead-EA-link fix installs a PROCESS-WIDE
    ShellExecuteA/W hook, is the only non-scaling behaviour in the DLL, and runs
    at EVERY tier including stock - with no off switch. Now [UiSpike]
    WebRedirect=0. Default stays on: the EA URL really is dead.
  - THREE INERT SETTINGS NAMED (scalingAutoConfig, presentWidth, presentHeight):
    parsed from the ini, read by nothing. Kept as fields (shared header with the
    FROZEN touch DLL) but marked, and they must not appear in the shipped user
    ini - a documented key that does nothing is a lie to the user.
  - GATES: BornCorrectCoverage 51/51, ScaleTierDecide, SubRingLock 311,
    coverage_rederive 27/0, crosscheck 251/251, DatIntegrity ALL PASS
    (24 dats + 3 font sources + 2 DLLs + frozen bundle + 26 deployed==built).
    Binary PII scan clean (the only .pdb string is the bare filename).

v2.64.0  2026-08-03  ** #102 RESIDUE: THE CONFIRM FAMILY'S MISSING 4th BASE **
  HARDENING, NOT A VISIBLE FIX. Nothing on screen changes on any path we have
  ever observed; what changes is what happens IF a latent path is raised.
  - THE DEFECT: kCityDialogIds carried THREE base sizes per id, but the confirm
    family has FOUR. Stock declares 0xAA921F4F as a root in three scripts, and
    I-4a551b4c (330x109, the region-screen two-button "Quit SimCity 4"/"Cancel")
    was absent from the table. We SHIP that script staged at every tier.
    The exact-product guard looped `b < 3`, so a data-born 660x218 matched no
    product, dataBorn stayed false, and the block fell through to SetW/SetH ->
    1320x436 = the v2.39.14 4x shape - inside the guard whose own note says
    "the data must be complete".
  - THE FIX: bw[]/bh[] widened 3 -> 4; 330x109 added; loop bound now derived
    from sizeof(dlg.bw) instead of a literal 3. That last part is the anti-rot
    half - a literal bound is EXACTLY how the 4th base went unread, and it can
    no longer happen when the table is extended.
  - SCOPED BY MEASUREMENT, not symmetry: the 4th base goes to 0xAA921F4F ONLY.
    The staged file declares `id=0xaa921f4f area=(664,340,1324,558)` = 660x218
    = RoundHalfUp(330x109*2), and `6aaeec4a` appears ZERO times in it. Giving
    the twin the slot would have invented a base we have never seen.
  - ARITHMETICALLY SAFE: 330x109's products (495x164 / 660x218 / 990x327)
    collide with no 1x base, so no existing member can start matching the new
    slot.
  - REACHABILITY, STATED HONESTLY: LATENT. Disarm() on kSC4MessagePreCityShutdown
    sets continuous=false and IncrementalPass() only runs while continuous, so
    the region-screen variant is not reached with no city loaded. The null is
    MEASURED, not structural - this same block logs 0xAA921F4F whenever the
    in-city variant opens, and across every capture it has only ever seen
    270x162. So there is NOTHING to eyes-on here; do not ask for one.
  - Gates: BornCorrectCoverage 51/51, ScaleTierDecide, SubRingLock 311,
    DatIntegrity ALL PASS (26 deployed==built).

  SAME SESSION, NO CODE: #98 CLOSED NOT-A-BUG. The standing instruction to
  "re-apply" the Trip Types legend cure was STALE and would have shipped a 4x
  legend - the cure had already been shipped once, rendered at 4x on screen
  because the runtime was already scaling those windows, and was reverted. The
  generator grepping clean is the CORRECT state. De-mined in HANDOFF.md (2 rows)
  and in the #98 entry below.

v2.63.0  2026-08-03  ** #110 BUDGET POPUP CLOSE-X - THE DEAD X WAS OURS **
  - SYMPTOM: on every empty budget department (City Beautification, Neighbor
    Deals, Government, Take Out A Loan - anything at SS0) the "no entries in the
    budget ledger" box's close-X did nothing. Several empty departments = several
    dead Xs, which is what "the broken Xs" meant.
  - #103 CLOSED THIS NOT-A-BUG AND WAS WRONG. Its gate decoded the command
    dispatch correctly (id 0xCC -> branch 0x78B406, which removes nothing) but
    nothing ever established that a CLICK on the X arrives there as command 0xCC.
    It does not. STOCK CONTROL, user-confirmed: with the whole scaling layer
    parked (Set-StockCompare -Mode Stock, 1024x768) the X CLOSES THE BOX.
  - THE REAL CAUSE, ours since v2.28.2: window 0x0423278D is built by TWO
    functions with DIFFERENT stock heights, and the POPBOX pin applied the
    ORDINANCE twin's 125 to both.
      * sub_78B120 ordinance description popup - stock H 125, backdrop
        0x384/0x484. Host = the Ordinances dialog (~754 tall at 2x). Correct.
      * sub_77BEC0 empty-ledger box - stock H 100, backdrop 0x385/0x485,
        close-X 0xCC/0x1CC. Its HOST IS THE BOX: a top-level 600x127 window,
        127 because CodePatches clamps its five SetSize sites to the push-imm8
        ceiling (round(100*2)=200 will not encode).
    Pinning that popup to round(125*2)=250 inside a 127-tall host made the pin's
    own y clamp resolve to 127-250 = -123, so the popup - and the close-X at
    popup-local y=22 - sat at host-local y=-101, ABOVE the host rect. The sprite
    still drew (the engine does not clip it); the router's hit walk only descends
    into children whose rect contains the point, so the click never reached the X.
    LOGGED 19 TIMES as `POPBOX 600x127 -> 600x250 at y=-123` and read past.
  - ISOLATED, not argued: PopupWrap=0 at 2x restored the X immediately AND
    reintroduced the ordinance twin's text clip - which is exactly why the cure
    is per-twin and a kill switch is not a fix.
  - THE FIX (UiSpike.cpp POPBOX block): twin gate on 0x484 vs 0x485 (the split is
    total; gate_103_twin_ids.py). Ordinance path is BIT-IDENTICAL to v2.62.0 -
    the v2.28.4 math is kept, not re-derived. The ledger twin now uses ITS OWN
    stock height 100, and host + popup + content + 0x485 + 0x385 move together to
    round(100*f) - a COUPLED SET, all five or none. The popup then fits its host,
    the clamp is a no-op BY CONSTRUCTION, the X lands inside the host rect, and
    the body fill has round(100*f)-2y = 100px to wrap in instead of 27.
  - NEW INSTRUMENT: POPSEEN, one unconditional line per popup instance carrying
    host size, popup rect, twin and the chosen stockH/wantH. The pin never had a
    reachability line, which is why a 19-hit defect read as "never executes".
  - gate_103_closepath.py now prints a SCOPE CORRECTION naming the refutation.
    Its byte decoding was never wrong; its verdict was outside its scope.
  - LESSON (third time today): a gate that answers its own question correctly
    still says NOTHING about a question nobody checked it could answer. The
    positive control proved the classifier could spot a close idiom - never that
    the command handler is the path a mouse click takes.
  - Also recorded, stock and unrelated to the fix: the WinProc closes this popup
    on ESC / ENTER / F4 (0x78BCFE, ChildRemove + Release + post), so the box was
    never a soft-lock.

v2.62.0  2026-08-03  ** #104 SHUTDOWN HANG - SYMPTOM FIX **
  - THE HANG: after the window closes the process spun at 85-150% of one core
    forever. Root cause is the GAME'S, confirmed by disassembly + in-process
    measurement over 20+ instrumented runs:
      * service priorities force ~cGZWinMgr (1,250,000) to destruct BEFORE
        ~cGZMessageServer (5,000,000); RemoveAllSystemServices erases ascending
      * ~cGZWinMgr frees its valid-window bucket array and leaves [set+4]/[set+8]
        dangling, so IsWindowValid answers FALSE for EVERY window afterwards
      * cGZWinGen::Init self-subscribes and AddRefs; only Shutdown unsubscribes
      * ChildRemove orphans a child WITHOUT calling Shutdown
      * cGZWin::ChildDeleteAll ORs the removal failure into bl and NEVER TESTS IT
    Net: an orphaned window outlives the manager, every removal is a no-op, and
    ChildDeleteAll retries the same child forever.
  - THE FIX ([UiSpike] SpinFix, default ON, needs SpinProbe > 0): when the spin is
    MEASURED, point the stuck child list's sentinel at itself so the loop's own
    empty test passes. Two 4-byte writes. NO game code is called - the obvious
    repair routes through IsWindowValid, which reads the freed bucket array, and
    the vtable slot it was specified at (+0x3C) does not hold that function.
    Guards: thread suspended, refuses if the iteration guard is held, every
    pointer range-checked, repeats per DISTINCT list (max 64), nothing logs
    inside the suspension.
  - VERIFIED ONCE, NOT ESTABLISHED: process exited within ~1s of the fix and the
    120s sampler died with it (zero further per-second reports). #104 is
    intermittent; SC4UIScale-104.csv + _tests\Show-104Rates.ps1 accumulate the rate.
  - The CAUSE remains open and is not ours to fix without patching game code.

  - In-process EIP sampler (20Hz) + append-only outcome recorder + per-child

    enumerates the valid set and feeds one of its own keys back through the
    lookup. That self-test is what cracked #104: it proved the table was not
    healthy-but-empty but DESTROYED.
  - Fixed three of our own instrument bugs: a stack scan that could only ever
    print game frames while being asked whether a frame was foreign; a field dump
    gated on a coincidence so it never fired; a valid-set lookup with no positive
