v2.7.0  2026-07-22  ** AUTOSCALE: dynamic resolution tiers **
  - The scale factor is now a FIT FUNCTION, not a constant: on every boot
    the DLL reads the effective resolution and picks the largest installed
    package factor whose widest UI piece (880 design px) and tallest
    dialog (558 design px) fit the screen, capped at 800x600-density.
    Any resolution - including ones never directly tested - gets a
    provably fitting factor (2000-random-resolution sweep: 0 violations).
  - The DLL manages the static data layers to match: at stock tiers the
    art dat, dialog dat, and FontStyle.ini are renamed aside (suffix
    .x1-disabled) BEFORE the game loads data; at scaled tiers they are
    re-activated. Idempotent, self-healing, logged.
  - Stock tiers are TRULY inert (isolation-proven): no subclass, no
    timer, no hooks, no window attach - indistinguishable from a no-DLL
    install except the WebRedirect dead-link fix.
  - PLUGINS-ONLY footprint (user requirement): everything lives in
    Documents\SimCity 4\Plugins - proven the game reads FontStyle.ini
    from there; the redundant install-dir copy is retired. No Program
    Files writes; portable for users on any Steam path.
  - Live-verified: 2400x1600 -> 2.00 (9/9 panels), 800x600 -> 1.00
    (stashed + inert), 1920x1080 -> 1.00 today (becomes 1.5x
    automatically once the 1.5x package ships). AutoScale=0 keeps the
    manual ScaleFactor for development.
  - Known separate issue: sub-native fullscreen modes garble under
    dgVoodoo on this panel (proven with ZERO plugin DLLs loaded - not
    our code); needs a dgVoodoo config pass before 1x tiers are
    VISUALLY judged on this machine.

v2.28.0-.4 2026-07-30  ** ORDINANCE DESCRIPTION POPUP - SOLVED **
  - THE DEFECT: an ordinance's "Open Details" description rendered as one
    line, cut off. Four earlier attempts (v2.27.1/.2/.3 plus v2.28.0/.1)
    all failed because they assumed the TEXT was wrong. It was the BOX,
    plus one flag bit. Solved with the offline emulator (tools\uimap\emu)
    running the game's own layout code under Unicorn.
  - CAUSE 1 - the box was never scaled. Its own constants: height 125
    (push 0x7d, FOUR twin sites), right margin 60 (sub ebx,0x3c), x 30
    (push 0x1e), y clamp -125 (add eax,-0x7d). At 2x that gave 840x125
    where round(stock x f) is 780x250 - and the body, sized by the
    align-0x63 FILL branch as parentH-2y, landed 25px tall, less than ONE
    line of Arta 28. Margin + x are byte patches; 250/-250 exceed the imm8
    ceiling so height + clamp are the POPBOX sweep pin, which also
    re-applies the fill formula to the body (690x150 at 2x).
  - CAUSE 2 - the text was never word-wrapping. A GZWinText picks one of
    three regimes at 0x009BF486 and the constructor default (flags=0)
    selects "break at explicit \n only, then clip". One call fixes it:
    SetWinTextFlag(0x0002,true) then a resize (the resize is the trigger).
    The engine then wraps at GetW()-10 at EVERY tier by itself - 335 at
    1x, 680 at 2x, 1025 at 3x - with no constant of ours. Confirmed live:
    "wrap flag was 0".
  - This NARROWS law 21: text does not re-wrap from a re-applied caption,
    but it DOES re-wrap from a SetArea (the class overrides it at
    0x009BFCA5 and re-breaks every line).
  - Also fixed, from the offline census + predictive sweep: the Neighbor
    Deals title-y patch site was recorded as 0x77F5B9, which lands inside
    the NEXT instruction. The real stanza is 0x77F5B2. Every launch had
    been logging "site 0x0077F5B9 bytes unexpected - skipped" and that
    title never doubled its y. Zero skipped sites now.
  - Measurement worth keeping: the descriptions are 4,225-6,166 px as one
    unwrapped line at 2x. The standing screenshot-inferred guess was
    ~920 px - wrong by 6x, and it made "just widen the box" look viable
    for hours. The screen is 2,400 px.
  - Dead end recorded: do NOT measure text via cIGZFontSys. Three
    FontAcquire overloads precede EnumerateFontInfo and MSVC reverses
    overloaded vtable groups, so header-order calls hit the wrong slots.

v2.38.5 2026-07-31 ** THE 0b APPLY-TAIL: OUR TWO BUILDERS DISAGREED AND
                      WHICHEVER RAN LAST WON **
  - We run TWO builders over the same art with DIFFERENT exclusivity rules, and
    neither could see the other's verdict. build_selective_safe.py knows which
    TGIs are CODE-BOUND and refuses to double one whose .UI referrers are all
    unscaled (its cb_conflict case) - a code consumer is invisible to a .UI-only
    judgement, so "no scaled referrer" is NOT permission to double.
    build_dialog_static.py has no code-bound term at all and reads refmap only
    for clone targets, so it doubled 2x-in-place exactly what the other builder
    had deliberately refused. That is the origin of the open #4 News defect.
  - NEW cross-builder guard in build_dialog_static.py: staging a TGI that
    selective-safe refused is FATAL unless it carries a reasoned
    KNOWN_BUILDER_DISAGREEMENTS entry. Negative-controlled.
  - ⚠ THE NARROW SET WAS THE WHOLE POINT, and the broad reading was nearly
    shipped: 114 TGIs are "untouched" in refmap, but almost all of that is
    correct DIVISION OF LABOUR - art referenced only by the 163 static dialogs
    is legitimately unscaled to selective-safe and legitimately exclusive to
    dialog-static. Honouring `untouched` wholesale would have gutted the static
    package. The real disagreement is only over CODE-BOUND art: exactly TWO,
    {46a006b0,46a006a4} and {46a006b0,46a006a6}.
  - ⚠ REGEX-PARSING A SOURCE FILE UNDER-COUNTED FOR THE SECOND TIME TODAY:
    CODE_BOUND_TGIS is built up programmatically - 283 entries by import, 39 by
    regex. The conflict set happened to be the same either way, but only
    because those two entries were literals. Both builders now IMPORT each
    other's lists; no list of record is ever regexed out of source.
  - ⚠ A LOGIC BUG IN MY OWN CHECK, caught by its negative control: the winner
    assert and its staleness check keyed on `third_party_WINNERS`, which is
    SELF-ERASING - the moment our override ships WE become the winner, so after
    SaveWarningUI shipped its two scripts dropped out and the check advised
    RETIRING the entries that were doing the fixing. winning-corpus.json now
    emits `third_party_HOLDERS` (a mod we do not control SUPPLIES this script -
    the durable fact) alongside `winners` (informational), and the builders key
    on holders. The assert now correctly reports 8 plugin-owned targets
    (6 CAM + 2 save-warning), all built from the winning script.
  - NO SHIPPED DATA CHANGED: stage/, stage-15x/, stage-3x/ byte-identical to
    the pre-0b baseline; DialogStatic 261, CamUI 10, SaveWarningUI 2.
    The two known disagreements are HELD, not silently accepted - a6 is the
    open #4 suspect and is not reverted because the News -> a6 link is still
    INFERRED and the blast radius is every runtime scrollbar in the game.
    One DPROBE reading settles it.

(data)  2026-07-31 ** SELECTIVEART 655: THE ROW FURNITURE WAS HTML <IMG> ALL
                       ALONG (task #87 SOLVED, third mechanism) **
  - The user's "keep going" push cracked it in one disassembly pass: the
    news/advice row bullet, expander arrows and close X are HTML <IMG>
    elements - sixteen .rdata templates at VA 0xA83560-0xA83820 read
    <IMG SRC="sc4://HTML/46a006b0/1441625X"> (X=0..F) with NO
    width/height, so the renderer draws each at the art's INTRINSIC size:
    18x18 stock = the 1x glyphs in both screenshots. Rendered the sixteen
    into a proof sheet: bullet/right/down/X in four severity colours,
    colour-matched to the headlines. HTML-only refs are invisible to the
    .UI reference pass - no builder could ever have staged them.
  - FIX: TWELVE of the sixteen staged at every tier. SelectiveArt
    651 = 639 - 1 + 12 + 1: the e2b66db8 entry REVERTED per the revert law
    (its clone theory was refuted - there is no cloned button at all; the
    "delete me" template has ZERO exe references and is vestigial).
  - ✅ ARROWS USER-CONFIRMED 2x. ⚠ THE FOUR X GLYPHS ARE HELD AT 1x
    (...53/57/5B/5F, mask i&3==3): the all-sixteen build put the row X OFF
    THE RIGHT EDGE. So the left-hand glyphs sit in normal text flow and are
    size-agnostic, while the X's slot is computed against something that
    assumes 18px. Twelve is strictly better than either earlier build -
    arrows fixed, X back at its known-good baseline - and the A/B itself is
    the measurement that split the two behaviours. The X placement is task
    #88; those four must never be re-added without the placement fix in the
    SAME build. (This is the day's method working: when the third theory
    finally held, the FOURTH question got an A/B instead of a guess.)
  - THE DAY'S ARC ON THIS ONE WINDOW, for the record: theory 1 scrollbar
    (refuted - factory self-sizes, measured correct), theory 2 cloned
    button born at art size (refuted by eyes-on), theory 3 code-sized
    clone (refuted - no clone exists), TRUTH: HTML <IMG> at intrinsic
    art size, found by scanning .rdata for template strings instead of
    guessing again. The lesson is law 21's cousin: WHEN A GLYPH IGNORES
    EVERY WINDOW-SIDE LEVER, IT IS NOT A WINDOW.
  - VERIFY: News rows - bullet/arrow/X at 2x, colours intact; expanded
    state's down-arrow too; advisor briefing + My Sims story lists (same
    furniture); dialogs' OK/X unchanged (e2b66db8 back to stock).

  ############################################################################

  ## The load-bearing claim below - "NO sweep root reaches a main-window     ##

  ## ON SCREEN, and the change was reverted for that reason.                 ##

  ## So the generator grepping clean is the CORRECT state, not a loss. The   ##

  ## the standing law STATIC DEFECT = HYPOTHESIS - which was WRITTEN from    ##
  ## this incident.                                                          ##
  ############################################################################
  DATA item, no runtime half: SelectiveArt (+ DialogStatic rebuild) only. The
  shipping DLL is unchanged at SC4UIScale.dll v2.55.0 - do NOT read a version
  number into this entry. Trip Types legend, CITY instance, reached by Route
  Query on any road or rail in a founded city. Script I-abb0120f, root
  0x6BB92BCB. 37 windows = 1 root + 36 descendants (13 GZWinBtn, 12 GZWinText,
  9 GZWinBMP, 1 GZWinGen, 2 clsid=0x89e1567c).
  - THE ARTEFACT, measured in the shipping staged script at every tier
    (tools\selective-safe\stage{,-15x,-3x}\T-0x00000000_G-0x96a006b0_I-
    0xabb0120f.ui): nine GZWinBMP icons at area 18x14 (48..66) carrying
    imagerect=(0,0,36,28) - 2x art drawn out of 1x windows.
      row pitch 21 vs art height 28        -> 7px VERTICAL OVERLAP every row
      drawn right edge 48+36=84 vs label column x=71 -> 13px OVERRUN into text
  - THE CENSUS'S OWN DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG AND WAS CORRECTED FROM THE EXE. The
    VAs below are this session's disassembly result, recorded as reported -
    they were NOT re-derived when this entry was written:
    * 0x6BB92BCB is a CONSTRUCTION-ONLY CONTAINER. Its id occurs exactly ONCE
      image-wide (VA 0x004C594F; created 0x004C595C from TGI
      {0,0x96a006b0,0xabb0120f}) and 0x218 bytes later the SAME function calls
      mainWindow->ChildDelete(container) at 0x004C5B64 (cIGZWin vt+0x40). It
      never lives in the window tree, so its area= is DEAD DATA - the "1x root
      box" the census reported was a PHANTOM. Scale it only to keep the file
      internally consistent.
    * The two REAL windows are its children, PROMOTED to direct children of
      the MAIN WINDOW: GetChildAs(0x0BB0F5E7) -> ChildRemove -> ChildAdd
      (0x004C5A04..0x004C5A16), same for 0x6BB92BCA (0x004C5AB5..0x004C5AC8).
    * NO sweep root reaches a main-window child (city = SC4View3DWin, region =
      0xEA659793, and neither id is in kCityDialogIds), so nothing at runtime
      can grow the boxes our 2x art is already drawing into.
    * 14 distinct art refs, not the census's 12 - VERIFIED here in refmap.csv:
      13 EXCLUSIVE/2x-in-place + 1 SHARED (0x14416245 -> clone 0x47026244).
  - THE INTENDED FIX (data only): in build_selective_safe.py's per-file loop,
    I-abb0120f gets double_one_window_area + double_subtree_areas on 6bb92bcb
    (1 root + 36 descendants), followed by an in-generator ADJUDICATOR that
    sys.exit()s unless all 9 GZWinBMP have area==imagerect AND row pitch >=

v2.53.0 - v2.55.0  2026-08-03  ** #57 GRAPHS LEGEND CLOSED - USER-CONFIRMED
                    "looks fantastic". THE CHART NEVER LAID OUT ITS LEGEND. **
  Shipping DLL = SC4UIScale.dll v2.55.0 (UISCALE_VERSION_STR "2.55.0",
  src\SC4UIScaleDllDirector.cpp:43). This entry covers the whole 2026-08-03
  arc; the three bands are listed separately because two of them shipped and
  were superseded inside the same day.

  ---- WHAT SHIPPED (v2.55.0, the surviving code) ----
  - CodePatches::ApplyGraphLegendBudgetScale(factor): FIVE in-place imm8
    patches plus THREE equal-length instruction-block re-encodings inside the
    Graphs PANEL builder sub_76D3D0, verify-ALL-before-write-ANY, so the whole
    legend column is BORN at f. Sites, all in src\CodePatches.cpp:616-651:
      imm8   0x0076E233 swatch dy   lea ecx,[eax+3]
             0x0076E239 swatch bot  add eax,9
             0x0076E23C swatch w    add ebx,0xA
             0x0076E2AF swatch->txt add ecx,4

             0x0076E145 (41 B) B2 checkbox rect

  - EARLYCHART (v2.54.0, kept): the chart's plot margins are scaled inside the
    game's own layout store, iface vt+0x30 = sub_9B1F1D, so the chart is born
    correct and no stock frame reaches the screen on a graph switch.
  - COUPLED PAIR (law 43): the budget strip and EARLYCHART's plot right margin
    arm together behind the one ChartScale flag, and
    CodePatches::GraphLegendPlotRightMargin() returns 0 unless all EIGHT sites
    took - so the two halves can never split.

  ---- THE MECHANISM, IN TWO SENTENCES ----
  The Graphs chart does NOT lay out its legend: the PANEL builder sub_76D3D0
  (0x0076D3D0..0x0076E420) does, once per chart build, from hard-coded literals
  plus the chart window's WIDTH, and the list at chart+0x228 is walked only to
  DRAW (sub_9B5ADE, main vt+0x278) and to destroy (sub_9B5990) - never to lay
  out. The whole column is therefore a SIX-CONSTANT right-margin budget
  measured off winW that NONE of our scaling ever touched (plot right reserve
  110 | checkbox left 108 | swatch left 90 cbox / 106 plain | swatch 10x6 |
  swatch->text gap 4 | text right 4), so at 2x the 32px checkbox window and the
  wider 26pt text box crowded 52px of content into the same 110px gutter,
  checkboxRight met textLeft and the 17px slot the swatch lives in collapsed to
  zero - the swatch never moved, its BUDGET was eaten.
  - Stated as an inequality, which is why f=1 never showed it:
      swatch buried  <=>  cbox.L + 16f > swatch.L  <=>  16f > 18  <=>  f > 1.125
    False at f=1, TRUE at every shipped tier (1.5, 2, 3).
  - THE PROOF that the chart is not the layer: a whole-.text scan found exactly
    ONE call site for each legend-entry allocator - iface+0xC4 = sub_9B963D
    (text block, vt 0x00ADE540) at 0x0076E20A, iface+0xCC = sub_9B5A84 (swatch,
    vt 0x00ADE0DC) at 0x0076E220. Both inside sub_76D3D0.
  - THE STRIP IS TABLED FROM THE ORACLE, NEVER COMPUTED: f=1.5 -> 178,
    f=2 -> 240, f=3 -> 371 (f=1 = 108 = stock). A factor with no certified
    strip DECLINES rather than guessing.

  ---- LIVE ACCEPTANCE (v2.55.0, user-confirmed) ----
    graph legend budget x2.00 (8 of 8 sites) - strip 240, cboxL winW-240,
      swatch winW-204 (cbox) / winW-236 (plain), gap 8, textR 8
    EARLYCHART store (45,20,866,492) -> (90,40,732,472) budgetRM=244
  matching the oracle's certified 2x targets to the pixel: cbox 736..768,
  swatch 772..792, text 800..968 (box 168), plot.R 732.

  ---- RESIDUALS, NAMED (none of these blocked the close) ----
  1. 1.5x AND 3x ARE VERTICALLY UNVERIFIED. lineHeight has never been measured
     at those tiers, so the oracle reports 480 U1 + 160 U6 checks as UNKNOWN
     rather than assuming them. The horizontal budget is certified at all four
     factors; the ROW PITCH and the fit-in-panel-height question are not.
     ONE capture at each tier clears both (see
     tools\research\_incoming\lineh-tier-capture-procedure.md).
  2. THE 32x32 CHECKBOX WRITER IS STILL UNIDENTIFIED. sub_76D3D0's own
     SetArea at 0x0076E151/59/62/68 writes a 16x16 checkbox AT EVERY TIER;
     something downstream turns it into 32x32 at 2x and we have not found it.
     The fix deliberately leaves the game's SetArea 16x16, which makes the
     column correct under BOTH surviving hypotheses (H_NONE: nothing rewrites
     it, the box stays 16 and the swatch slot is wider than needed;
     H_SCALE: our sweep scales it to 16f and the slot is exactly right).
     The gate proves both. UNPROVEN either way - do not write it down as
     settled.
  3. Two of the nine Garbage rows are MODELLED, not measured, as taking an
     extra text line at 2x; the glyph table has no B H K N O Q S U X Y Z,
     h j k q z, no digit and no # ( ) - $ % : , so those labels are counted
     UNKNOWN by the oracle rather than assumed to fit.
  4. The offline model does not know the new sites yet. crosscheck.py counts
     them as DEFERRED (8 entries, guarded G1-G4), NOT as passes. Clearing
     needs census.EXTRA_BUILDERS + a regeneration.

  ---- v2.53.1 / v2.53.2: THE MISSING HALF, AND THE PIN COMES OFF ----
  - v2.53.1 THE GREEN-BOX PROOF. All three earlier "inert" levers shared ONE
    cause: the value writes landed but nothing ever DIRTIED the window. The
    chart's repaint gate is cIGZWin+0x70 and only vt+0x170 (sub_99BED1, the
    game's own SetDirty-and-propagate) reaches it - InvalidateSelfAndParents
    does not. With that call appended, field write = pixels moved.
    ⚠ THIS CORRECTS THE v2.49.0-v2.52.1 ENTRY BELOW: its "LEADING SURVIVOR,
    UNTESTED - the chart is code-painted into its own CACHED BUFFER" theory is
    FALSIFIED. The chart is destroyed and rebuilt on every graph switch
    (0x0076D3DA-0x0076D409), so the repaint was never the blocker; the dirty
    byte was, and then the builder budget was. The old entry is left standing
    as the record of what we believed; read this line with it.
    Also gated on the FIELD (bandH==32) instead of a pointer latch, since the
    chart object is REPLACED per switch and a reused address would silently
    skip (the #92 trap). Coupled-set write: plot rect at chart+0xE0..0xEC PLUS
    the band fields at +0x108/+0x10C/+0x110/+0x114 and ticks at +0x180/+0x184,
    whole or not at all (law 43).
  - v2.53.2 ChartTickText comes OFF the KEEP_STOCK pin (make_fontstyle.py:96)
    now that the plot gutter is a lever rather than a frozen 45px rect;
    KEEP_STOCK is empty today.
  - ⚠ OPEN, AND SAY SO: the chart's own band rect at chart+0x108 (measured
    live by CHARTGEO as (4,4,972,36), bandH 32) and the per-row legend COLUMN
    built by sub_76D3D0 are two MEASURED objects from two independent
    instruments, and NOTHING in the doc set proves they are the same thing.
    Both writes ship. UNKNOWN which pixels the band rect owns. THE
    MEASUREMENT THAT WOULD SETTLE IT: dump chart+0x108 and the legend child
    rects in ONE CHARTGEO line at the same instant, or disassemble what reads
    chart+0x108 inside the draw path sub_9B5ADE.

  ---- TWO MEASUREMENTS THAT CHANGE OTHER FAMILIES ----
  - THE CHART USES **ChartLabel**, NOT **Legend**. Byte-verified: 0x0076DD91
    pushes GUID 0xE9C86B5E = ChartLabel. Legend (0xE9C86B5F) is the DATA VIEWS
    legend (0x007A0747). So make_fontstyle.py's SIZE_SQUEEZE = {"Legend":0.92}
    HAS NEVER APPLIED TO THE GRAPHS CHART AT ALL - it renders at ChartLabel's
    RAW 26pt at 2x, and every calculation that assumed the squeezed 24pt was
    using the wrong style. (This retires the v2.53.2 note above that credited
    the squeeze with clearing the "Expense / s" shortfall.)
  - TEXT INK GROWS x2.13, NOT x2.00. 17 label strings measured at both 13pt
    and 26pt out of the game's own rendered pixels: mean 2.130, sd 0.026,
    n=17; pooled 2080/975 = 2.133; spread 2.085 ("Air Pollution") .. 2.188
    ("Commute Time"). ⚠ The single figure 2.121 that appears in
    src\CodePatches.cpp:589 and in several docs is ONE string's ratio -
    "Income" 33->70 - not the population mean. A box of round(stockBox * f)
    therefore WRAPS MORE THAN STOCK; boxes must be sized from the FONT, not
    from f (law 48). The shipped fonts are <install>\Fonts\*.mxf (Monotype
    MicroType Express, magic MXFN) - there is no .ttf/.otf anywhere, so
    FreeType cannot be pointed at Arta and every metric is measured out of
    rendered pixels with a stated residual of +/-3.8 px.

(fonts/tooling) 2026-08-02  ** #57 phase 4: THE FONT PACKAGES HAD ROTTED, AND
                    THE 1.5x/3x TIERS WERE SHIPPING A SILENT REGRESSION. **
  No DLL change. Found while planning the Graphs chart work.
  - THE REGRESSION: the deployed FontStyle-15x.ini and FontStyle-3x.ini were
    the raw `.gen.ini` side-outputs - 62 styles, and ZERO
    MessageHeaderHtml/MessageBodyHtml. CodePatches' popup retarget
    (0x52CCEE, src/CodePatches.cpp:56-62) therefore pointed at styles that do
    not exist at those tiers. It "degrades softly", i.e. FAILS INVISIBLY, and
    has done since v2.25.2. 2x was fine (64 styles); only 1.5x/3x were hit.
  - ROOT CAUSE: the two HTML clone styles were HAND-ADDED to candidate.ini
    AFTER generation, so make_fontstyle.py's output was never the shippable
    file - and PACKAGES.md documents the bare generate command. Anyone
    following the docs produced a broken file. **The generator now emits the
    clone block itself** (HTML_CLONE_BLOCK, anchored after
    MessageBodyUnderline, never scaled at any tier - the game derives an HTML
    size index from them and the DLL scales the size TABLE instead), and
    raises FATAL if they are absent from its own output.
  - STRONGER SELF-CHECK: `--selfcheck` now asserts factor 2 reproduces
    candidate.ini **BYTE FOR BYTE** (ignoring its hand-written ";;" banner),
    not merely the 88 sizes. The old size-only check passed happily while the
    clones were missing entirely - which is exactly how this shipped.
  - ALSO FIXED: tools\packages\{3x,15x}\FontStyle-*.ini still carried
    ChartTickText 30 / 15 (KEEP_STOCK landed v2.25.2 but these were never
    regenerated - missed, not deferred). All four repo copies plus both
    `.gen.ini` files are now regenerated and byte-identical per tier.
  - THE DURABLE FIX (this is the real deliverable): fonts were the ONE asset
    family with no deploy automation and no content assertion.
    Deploy-OnGameClose.ps1 had ZERO font lines - the three tier sources were
    placed BY HAND - and Test-DatIntegrity checked font EXISTENCE only. Both
    fixed: 3 Copy-Item lines + 3 $BUILT_PAIRS rows (23 -> 26 hash pairs).
    ⚠ The new rows FAILED on their first run, naming exactly the two drifted
    files and not the good one - the gate proving itself before the fix.
    Same lesson as #58, one asset family later.
  - CORRECTED IN PLACE: make_fontstyle.py's claim "(ChartLabel/Legend keep
    scaling: their regions have room - verified in the same screenshots.)" is

    "Income / Expense s". Legend is NOT pinned yet; that is the #57 decision
    still gated on the stock capture.

    2 DLLs + frozen-bundle hash + 26 deployed==built).

  stayed 1x = the clipped-buffer defect the 43 siblings had before v2.25.14.
  - FIX, both halves (law 43): child born 2x in DATA (its own branch in
    build_selective_safe.py, since the doubling is gated on the literal
    `id=0x4bcb938a` and this script declares its own root id) AND 0xEC1A5CBF
    in kDataScaledSubtreeIds so the sweep scales the root and stops.
    BornCoverage 50 -> 51 ids, which is the automatic proof that both halves
    are wired: the id is in SCALED_WINDOW_IDS *and* has a born-correct route.
    All three tiers rebuilt: 655/655/651, counts unchanged.
  - ⚠ I OVERSTATED THE MECHANISM AND THE SIBLING REFUTED IT. The task called
    this an "uninsured v2.21.0 HEAP-OVERRUN shape" and I repeated it in the

    also 463x132 and also pbuff, and it has shipped for many versions under

v2.47.1  2026-08-02  ** #92: the second-city gauge silent-skip (hardening,

  Three dashboard-gauge latches live as FUNCTION-LOCAL STATICS inside
  HookDashboardGauges - lastDashRoot, scanRoot, scanCount - declared far

  three are keyed on the dashboard's POINTER. A second city whose dashboard
  lands on a REUSED heap address therefore reads `pDash != lastDashRoot` as
  FALSE for a genuinely new object: the re-hook and the GAUGESCAN survey are
  both skipped, the gauges are never hooked, and NOTHING LOGS, because the
  code believes it already did the work. Same address-reuse shape scaleMap
  already defends against with its ID check.

    Disarm() bumps it; the hook drops all three latches when its own epoch is
    stale. Monotonic rather than a dirty flag so a double Disarm - or one
    racing a sweep - cannot leave it stuck: no state to get wrong, only a
    number to compare. scanRoot/scanCount moved up beside lastDashRoot so ONE
    check covers all three.

v2.46.0  2026-08-02  ** #95: BOTH HALVES. The container goes where the game's
                    own math says; the ring is PINNED where it already is. **
  The inference from v2.45.2 is now a MEASUREMENT. tools\flyout-sim\
  emu_plot.py, four runs with the object's [0x100] set to 138 / 200 / 0 /
  417, moved the ring blit's dst to exactly (0,138)(0,200)(0,0)(0,417) while
  the three BAR rects stayed BYTE-IDENTICAL; [0xF4] 6 -> 12 moved nothing.
  So the stem's y is a free variable that CANNOT disturb the strip - exactly
  what the user said twice: "the flyout never moves, just where it attaches
  in the list should".
  - THE FIX, both halves in one action:
      1. container -> SubPlaceTop() (the game's own expression + its four
         clamps, evaluated at f; already 32/32 vs sub_79AD00).
      2. ring sprite -> offset by MINUS that move (gSubRingAutoY), which
         pins it to the legacy dock the user already signed off.
    Net: the column stops hanging into the bottom HUD and the ring does not
    move by even one pixel. v2.45.0 shipped half 1 alone, which is precisely
    why the ring slid off and it was reverted the same day.
  - gSubMath default 0 -> 1. [Flyout] SubMath=0 still reverts instantly with
    no rebuild, and with it OFF this build is bit-identical to v2.45.2
    (both Auto terms are hard 0 on that path).
  - ⚠ X IS DELIBERATELY NOT MODELLED. The model's X is not wrong, it is a
    different convention: the 80f-wide ring sprite has the stem built into
    its right half, so the game's left = cx - 27f draws the ring 13f RIGHT
    of the button centre while our measured dock CENTRES it - a 26px
    difference at f=2. Nothing is broken horizontally, and moving X would
    also have desynced birth from the sweep (the sweep only re-docks a
    container found at native OR target; a 26px disagreement fails both,
    which silently ends the dock and freezes the back-arrow click zone).
    That desync was latent in v2.45.0/.1 and could only fire with SubMath=1.
  - The back-arrow click zone carries the Auto term too, so it lands exactly
    where v2.45.2 put it. The ring is a hit-test-sensitive control; a moved
    sprite with a stationary hit box would be a silent regression.
  - Birth and the sweep provably feed the model the SAME anchor: substituting
    the game's own ringY = (ch>>1) - 26 into the measured natT law gives
    natT = bcy - (ch>>1) - 3, which IS the game's nativeT = cy - (ch>>1) - 3.
    So the birth path's recovered cy equals the sweep's button centre
    identically (asserted, 48 cases).
  - NEW GATE _tests\Test-SubRingLock.ps1 - 311 assertions, and its scope is
    the RING (law 42). emu_subflyout's 32/32 was true and insufficient: it
    models the container and not the ring, which is how v2.45.0 shipped
    green and still broke. Asserts the pin, ring-centred-on-button, the f=1
    stock reduction (skipping the 2 cases the GAME itself clamps - asserting
    equality there would be asserting stock never clamps), the birth/sweep
    anchor identity, and the payoff: 8 items on a low button overflows under
    the legacy constant (top 1155 + 874 = 2029 vs margin 1580) and fits
    under the model (706 + 874 = 1580, autoY = 449). A gate that cannot fail
    on the OLD code proves nothing.
  - Offline: SubRingLock 311, emu_subflyout 71, BornCoverage 50/50,
    ScaleTierDecide 14 + 5000x2, DatIntegrity 24 dats + 23 deployed==built.

v2.45.2  2026-08-02  ** #95: SUBGEO probe (log-only), and the measurement
                    that finally named the lever. **
  Placement UNCHANGED from the v2.45.1 revert - this build only prints.
  SUBGEO dumps the whole assembly in ONE line and ONE coordinate space,
  because reasoning about these rects separately produced two wrong models:
    SUBGEO BTN(180,642 94x74 ctr 227,679) CONT(147,434 258x874)
           RINGr(0,192) RINGa(147,626) STRIP(307,484 88x774) f=2.00
  READ IT: the ring sprite is 160x106 at 2x, so its centre is (227,679) =
  THE BUTTON CENTRE, exactly, on both axes. **The ring half was already
  correct and must not be touched.** The strip is what reaches to 1258, into
  a bottom HUD whose top is ~1236.

  U-Drive-It pickers with buttons ~60px apart): **the strip occupies the SAME
  vertical band in all three; only the stem moves.** So the strip does NOT
  track the selected button - which refutes the input Phase 2 was built on
  (`cy` is the MENU anchor, not the selected button's centre). A 32/32
  emulator pass on a mis-identified input is still wrong: law 42.
  THE LEVER, now measured (disasm + 4/4 live menus): the stem is PART of the
  80x53 ring atlas cell, and its Y is obj[0x104]/win[0x100] - the value we
  ALREADY record as gSubRingBltY. ringY = (contentH>>1)-([0xF4]>>1)
  unclamped, = cy - containerTop - [0x100] in general. It is independent of
  the strip rect by construction (stripTop carries no cy term), and it is
  LATCHED per open from the 1x contentH. When a screen-margin clamp pins
  `top`, ringY absorbs the difference - which is precisely the stock
  behaviour above.
  ⇒ The fix is BOTH HALVES: SubPlaceTop (already written + validated) to
  place the container, plus a per-open ringY correction through gSubRingDY
  (ring-sprite only) to keep the stem on its button. Design + the free
  offline confirmation test are in HANDOFF.md's email and REGRESSION #95.
  NOT BUILT THIS SESSION - deliberately, with a compact imminent.

  hash-verified. 2x config restored after the stock capture.

v2.45.1  2026-08-02  ** #95 PHASE 2 REVERTED THE SAME SESSION IT SHIPPED.

  USER, eyes-on: "The items are no longer docked to their flyout which is

  "ORIGIN STAYS PUT, only the SIZE doubles. v2.15.0 scaled the origin too
  ((0,94) -> (0,188)) and that pushed the circle 94px down, UNDOCKING it."
  The ring's origin is the GAME's stored blit origin INSIDE the container;
  it does not follow a container we relocate. Container placement and ring
  attachment are ONE COUPLED SYSTEM.
  THE LESSON, and it is the expensive one: **the emulator models the
  CONTAINER half only, so 32/32 exact proved the arithmetic and said nothing
  about the outcome.** A perfect offline gate over an incomplete model is
  still an incomplete gate - it can only ever be as honest as its scope. The
  scope was never stated; it is now.

  container per button at all. The user's law - "the flyout never moves,
  just where it attaches" - says the ATTACHMENT POINT inside the list is the
  lever for the bottom-HUD overlap, not the container's position. That is
  where #95's remaining work goes.

  pre-v2.45.0 behaviour exactly. The model, its emulator harness

v2.45.0  2026-08-02  ** #95 PHASE 2: THE SUB-FLYOUT IS PLACED BY THE GAME'S
                    OWN FORMULA AT SCALE. The 8-item picker was 197px low. **
  USER: "bar is still broken and it's still overlapping the bottom UI" - the
  screenshot was the AIRCRAFT picker, 8 items, i.e. the worst case.
  MEASURED with the game's own sub_79AD00 under Unicorn (stock metrics vs
  every-metric-scaled), the dock delta our DLL must apply at f=2:
      n:      1     2     3     4     5     6     7     8
      true  -54   -74   -98  -123  -147  -172  -196  -221
      ours  -24   -24   -24   -24   -24   -24   -24   -24
      err   -30   -50   -74   -99  -123  -148  -172  -197
  A CONSTANT CANNOT BE RIGHT FOR MORE THAN ONE ITEM COUNT. The container is
  CENTRED on its button, so the correction grows with content height. 197px
  is exactly the overlap into the bottom HUD. Horizontally we applied -53
  where the truth is -27, so it also sat 26px too far left - at every n.
  ⚠ THE PLAN'S CLOSED FORM WAS WRONG BY EXACTLY -2 AT EVERY n, and the
  offline gate is the only reason that did not ship. The game computes
  (F4>>1) - (contentH>>1), and (53*f)>>1 != (53>>1)*f - the truncation
  differs per factor. So v2.45.0 reproduces the game's INTEGER expression
  (SubPlaceTop/SubPlaceLeft) rather than re-deriving it, including its FOUR
  clamps in ITS order (top margin, bottom margin, button-not-above,
  button-not-below).
  VALIDATION, before a single line of C++ was written: a python model of the
  expression vs the REAL machine code, 32/32 EXACT at n=1..8 x f=1/1.5/2/3,
  including the clamps (at f=3 the top margin fires for n>=7) and with f=1
  reproducing stock EXACTLY - the regression guard. The harness is now
  permanent: tools\uimap\emu\emu_subplace_model.py. Run it after ANY change
  to those two functions; REJECTED means the DLL is placing the flyout by an
  expression the game does not use.
  contentH is read from the container's LIVE height, never a recomputed item
  count - the container IS contentH, so the model cannot disagree with what
  is on screen.
  BOTH DOCK PATHS MOVED TOGETHER, and they had to: the sweep only re-docks a
  container it finds at the native OR the target position, so leaving birth
  on the old constant would have left it at neither and it would never be
  docked at all. The born path has no button in scope, so it RECOVERS the
  button centre by inverting the game's 1x expression
  (cy = nativeTop + (ch>>1) + 3) and SELF-CHECKS it: if recomputing the 1x
  top from that cy does not reproduce nativeTop, a 1x clamp fired, the
  inversion is invalid, and it falls back to the constant and lets the sweep
  correct it.
  [Flyout] SubMath, DEFAULT 1. The model is proven against the game's own
  code at four factors and eight item counts and the constant it replaces is
  measurably wrong by up to 197px - so 1 is the PROVEN mode and 0 is the
  revert switch (law 38 the right way round, same reading as MayorDock).
  Suites: emu_subflyout 71 PASS (unchanged), emu_subplace_model 32/32,
  DatIntegrity 24 dats + 23 hash pairs. Deployed + hash-verified.
  EYES-ON: open pickers with DIFFERENT item counts (1, 4 and the 8-item
  aircraft one) - each centred on its picker button and fully clear of the
  bottom HUD; no jump between the born position and the first sweep tick.

(data)  2026-08-02 ** #95 PHASE 3: THE IMAGERECT INVARIANT - the green-strip
                   break, fixed as a build-time LAW. **
  USER: "the stock resolution doesn't have a BREAK in the green strip."
  MECHANISM (measured): the green rail behind a flyout column is a plain
  GZWinBMP = **dst-follows-src** - its drawn length comes from the ART plus
  `imagerect` ONLY; the window rect is never read. So a rect that asks for
  more pixels than the bitmap HAS cannot draw them: the strip stops short.
  Measured break ratios 45-50% of column height on three columns.
  ⚠ WE SHIPPED THAT BREAK OURSELVES. warrior's script over-reads its own
  art (imagerect (0,0,178,269) over a 170x248 bitmap) and our builder
  FAITHFULLY DOUBLED the over-read to (0,0,356,538) over 340x496 - 42px of
  rail that can never exist. Doubling an upstream bug is still our bug.
  THE INVARIANT (now enforced at build time, every tier, every script):
      imagerect.right <= artW  AND  imagerect.bottom <= artH
  We CLAMP, never propagate, and every clamp PRINTS (no silent caps):
      {46a006b0,14215e27}: asked 356x538 -> art is 340x496
      {46a006b0,eb7c4d3b}: asked 448x164 -> art is 446x162
  Same two at 1.5x and 3x, scaled. Stock scripts produce ZERO clamps -
  the positive control that the rule is inert where the data is right.
  ⚠ THE INVARIANT CAUGHT OUR OWN BUILDER BUG BEFORE IT SHIPPED. First run
  reported THREE clamps incl. `{46a006b0,cbc3c2b9}: asked 1032x1308 -> art
  is 1032x792` - CoriBoom's Building Styles background, which is NOT an
  over-read. Cause: the third-party art was upscaled AFTER the scripts were
  edited, so the invariant resolved the MOD's rect against the STOCK
  upscale. Fixed by moving the art upscale BEFORE the script loop; the
  false clamp disappeared and the two real ones kept their (correct, larger)
  mod-art bounds. A clamp against the wrong reference would have been worse
  than the bug it was added to prevent.
  Suites: DatIntegrity 24 dats + 23 hash pairs, ThirdPartyGates 4,
  BornCoverage 50/50 - ALL PASS. Deployed + hash-verified. No DLL change.
  EYES-ON PENDING: the green rail continuous head-to-tail on the warrior
  Landscape column and on Signs & Labels.

                                        overrides table (22,344)

  No stock flyout triggered it - the change is inert exactly where it should

v2.43.1  2026-08-02  ** #94 PART 2: DOCK FROM THE **LIVE** MARKER, NOT THE
                    CACHED CONSTANT. Fixes the misdocked mayor flyouts. **
  v2.43.0 gave warrior's mod its 2x data but the flyouts still docked wrong
  (user: ring not against the right circle; Signs & Labels docking against
  the TOP circle instead of the bottom). MEASURED CAUSE - the mod moves the
  hidden 0x0000AAAA alignment marker, and our dock table caches
  R = -marker(1x) read off the STOCK script:
      LANDSCAPE 09923283  marker (3,27)  -> mod (3,59)
      SIGNS&LAB cb95403e  marker (3,183) -> mod (4,5)   <- 178px of misdock
  The S&L jump is the big one: stock's marker sits near the bottom of a tall
  125x249 column; the mod's sits at the TOP of a wide 224x95 row. A cached
  constant cannot survive that, and no amount of art fixing would.
  FIX: use the LIVE marker. The rule was already written above the table -
      target = spawnButtonAbs - markerOffset(live)
  and the table's R is just that rule evaluated against the stock script.
  The marker is scaled with the subtree we scale a few lines earlier, so its
  live L/T are already screen units. The constant remains the FALLBACK when
  a script has no marker. Self-correcting for ANY replacement script -
  nothing to hand-tune per mod, now or in future.
  INSTRUMENT: when the live value disagrees with the table we log
  "MDOCK 0x... live marker (x,y) -> (X,Y) overrides table (X,Y)". For a
  STOCK script the two MUST agree (the constant was derived from that very
  marker), so this line appearing on a stock flyout is a real alarm - and
  its absence on stock is the positive control that the change is inert
  where it should be.
  Suites: DatIntegrity 24 dats + 23 hash pairs, ThirdPartyGates 4,
  BornCoverage 50/50 - ALL PASS. Deployed + hash-verified.

  ACCEPTANCE MET, from the log (not from optimism): EIGHT consecutive picker

    open #1 census: scaled=22 clamped=2   open #5 census: scaled=22 clamped=2
    open #2 census: scaled=22 clamped=2   open #6 census: scaled=22 clamped=2
    open #3 census: scaled=22 clamped=2   open #7 census: scaled=22 clamped=2

  roots 0x698894D3 / 0xCA1F1D9C. That is correct and not a failure - there

    BMPX open #1 of 0x6A243D9E ... census: scaled=22 clamped=2   <- looked right
    BMPX root 0x6A243D9E resolved 338B4E14 -> 338B2814
    BMPX open #2 of 0x6A243D9E ... 25 instance(s) hooked
    BMPX open #2 of 0x6A243D9E ... census: scaled=0 clamped=0     <- looked WRONG
  Read it exactly: on the failing open the dialog was a NEW object, our hook
  WAS installed on all 25 windows, the dialog sat on screen for 13 seconds -
  and our per-window Draw was **never called once**. The engine put those
  pixels there through a path that does not go through Draw, so the cells
  show whatever their private buffer holds (every portrait cell is
  winflag_pbuff=yes). That is the pre-committed "scaled=0" outcome.
  ⚠ BUT THE PRE-COMMITTED CURE FOR IT IS WRONG HERE, AND SAYING SO IS THE
  POINT OF PRE-COMMITTING: v2.42.3 promised "cure = born-2x DATA (law 8, the
  v2.25.14 gauge precedent)". The picker's cells are ALREADY born 2x -
  DialogStatic doubles their area= (36x41 -> 72x82, verified in the staged
  script). The gauge cure worked because those windows were born 1x and
  painted once at 1x; that precondition is absent here, so the precedent
  does not transfer. Do not ship a data change on this evidence.
  WHAT SHIPPED INSTEAD (smallest step the measurement actually supports):
  kick ONE `InvalidateSelfAndParents()` through EACH freshly hooked leaf,
  not just the root (v2.42.2 kicked the root only, which demonstrably does
  not reach the leaves' draw path). Bounded: once per instance per open,
  list capped at 64 with a NO-SILENT-CAPS saturation line.
  ACCEPTANCE, decided in advance: the next failing-style open must read
  **scaled>0**. If a census still reads scaled=0 after this, the engine is
  not calling Draw even when the window is marked dirty - STOP, do not add
  sweeps (that is ghost-heal, already dead), and disassemble the blit path
  that paints these cells.
  Suites: DatIntegrity 21 dats + 20 deployed==built hashes, BornCorrect
  50/50 - PASS. No data change. Deployed + hash-verified.

v2.42.3  2026-08-02  ** #47 THE DECISIVE INSTRUMENT: PER-OPEN CENSUS.
                    LOG-ONLY - NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGE (deliberately). **
  v2.42.2's invalidate helped but did not cure, and our own rule says STOP
  and re-measure rather than iterate repaints - so this build deliberately
  adds NO new behaviour, only the measurement that decides the mechanism.
  - An OPEN is now detected as (root pointer CHANGED) **or** (root went
    hidden -> visible). The second half is the one that matters: the My Sims
    STRIP is resident and hooks ONCE at city load, so a reopen changes
    neither the pointer nor the hook count - v2.42.2 was therefore blind AND
    inert on exactly the repro the user reports ("close, reopen, small").
  - Per-open draw census, flushed as ONE line at the next open of any root
    and at Disarm: "BMPX open #N of 0x... census: scaled=X clamped=Y".
    COUNTS, not per-draw lines, so it CANNOT saturate - a failing open is
    now guaranteed to leave a line. Opens are numbered per root so a single
    user sentence ("the 3rd open was small") lands on one census row.
  - Budget re-arms per OPEN (was: per new-hook pass).
  THE DISCRIMINATOR, decided in advance (written at the flush site):
    scaled=0 clamped=0 -> that open's paint NEVER reached our hooked Draw;
      the engine composited the cell from its private buffer (every portrait
      cell is winflag_pbuff=yes) => more repaints cannot cure it; the cure
      is born-2x DATA (law 8, the v2.25.14 gauge precedent).
    scaled=N (N>0) on an open the user saw as small -> our draw WAS applied
      and the composite ignored it => different mechanism, re-measure; do
      NOT ship a data change on that evidence.
  Suites: DatIntegrity 21 dats + 20 deployed==built hashes, BornCorrect
  50/50, ThirdPartyGates 3, ScaleTierDecide 14+5000x2 - all PASS, no data
  change. Deployed + hash-verified.

v2.42.2  2026-08-02  ** #47 PARTIAL: one invalidate per new-hook pass.
                    HELPS, DOES NOT CURE (user: "happens less frequently but
                    is still happening"). #47 STAYS OPEN. **
  - `root->InvalidateSelfAndParents()` when a pass hooks >=1 new instance
    (UiSpike.cpp ~:6360). Bounded: once per city for the city roots, once
    per open for the picker dialog. NOT a blind sweep, so it is not the
    banned ghost-heal - but it is also NOT a cure, and the reason is
    structural: it can only fire when NEW instances hook. The My Sims STRIP
    windows hook ONCE at city load, so the strip never gets a second
    invalidate no matter how often the panel is reopened.
  WHAT THE INSTRUMENTED RUN PROVED (log 10:45-10:47, 139 BMPX lines):
  - ⛔ REFUTED - the single-find/hidden-template theory (the prime suspect
    in the #47 plan and HANDOFF-47-QWEN.md §3.1): the picker root
    0x6A243D9E resolved to a DIFFERENT pointer on every open
    (2DD00014 -> 2DD01214 -> 2DD02414 -> 2DD01C14), i.e. the single-find
    DOES follow the freshly created dialog, and 25 instances hooked on each
    open. Do not spend another build on IdCollectCtx for this family.
  - MEASURED CORRECT: whenever our hook runs, the math is right - all 22
    picker cells (0x12340000-15) and both strip portraits (0x22220000/01)
    logged "img 36x41 win 72x82 -> dst 72x82 (x2.00)" on every logged open.
  - ⛔ REFUTED (offline, same session): "our DialogStatic doubling enlarged
    the cells' source rect". The staged 2x picker script I-0a243d80 doubles
    area= (36x41 -> 72x82) and leaves imagerect=(0,0,36,41) UNTOUCHED, which
    is correct for a runtime-supplied bitmap.
  - ⚠ REMAINING BLIND SPOT: the 40-line budget re-arms only on a new-hook
    pass, so a failing paint that happens when nothing new hooks prints
    NOTHING. This log therefore CANNOT be said to contain a failing open -
    treat it as "no failure captured", never as "no failure occurred"
    (null-is-not-evidence).
  LEADING HYPOTHESIS (not measured - do not cite as fact): the failing
  paints never reach our hooked Draw at all; the engine composites each
  cell from its private buffer (every portrait cell is winflag_pbuff=yes)
  without calling Draw, so 1x pixels already in that buffer win. That fits
  all three observations: correct whenever Draw runs, "clicking a Sim fixes
  that one" (a click forces a real Draw), and why one invalidate helps but
  cannot cure.
  NEXT (decisive, log-only): re-arm the budget on the panel's OPEN
  (visibility transition), not on new-hook, and emit ONE summary line per
  open - "cells=22 drawn=N skipped=M". Then a single user report ("the 3rd
  open was small") identifies the mechanism: drawn=0 = the paint bypassed
  our hook (pbuff/composite); drawn=22 = the draw was right and the
  composite ignored it.

v2.41.19 2026-08-01  ** #89 CLOSED - "It loaded perfectly and I think it's
                    fixed." THE DOCK IS NEVER SEEN AT 1x, AND THE MAP IS
                    NEVER SEEN CORRUPTED. **
  The complete fix is TWO mechanisms, both required:
   1. CARRY-OVER (v2.41.12-.14): the corruption itself was OUR OWN repair
      erasing a good map (destroy surface -> new surface -> pre-clear to
      black). Cure: capture the old picture before the destroy, black floor,
      repaint bilinear. Applied to all three cSC4WinMiniMap blocks.
   2. EARLYDOCK (v2.41.17-.19): the 1x beat was the sweep arriving
      +0.8-2.3s after reveal. Cure: scale the dock from inside the
      cGZWin::SetFlag detour - the game's own stack, which keeps firing
      after city init returns - the moment the dock reports its full 20
      design children (CITY-DOCK-OVERLAP 1.4). MEASURED: fires at +328ms
      (city 1) and +109ms (city 2), before reveal, and the sweep then finds
      the dock AlreadyScaled and skips it (ScaleAll count 456 -> 431).
      CRITICALLY, the scale and the minimap surface recreate are ONE action:
      TryRecreateMinimapSurface is the sweep's own block, extracted verbatim
      (all gates, retry budget, carry-over inside), called by both.
  PROOF BY THE INSTRUMENT THAT OPENED THE TASK: FLASHSET no longer emits a
  line for 0x0987B48F at all - there is no on-screen scale left to report.
  And the user's session happened to open TWO cities: both clean, +109ms on
  the second, so the second-city law held live.
  THE GATE THAT MADE IT FIRE IN TIME: the two-consecutive-checks stability
  test cost 625ms because SetFlag barely fires during load (24 calls in the
  whole window). The design child count IS the "fully built" signal - fire on
  kids==20 directly, keep stability as the fallback for a modded dock.
  WHY THE v2.41.15 CRASH DID NOT RECUR: that build scaled the dock WITHOUT
  the surface recreate. Scaling self-updates the minimap's blitSize to 128
  while the one-shot surface stays 64; the next bake is a 128 render into a
  64 surface = the v2.21.0 heap overrun, dying late and silently. The
  explanation predicted mode 2 would be safe WITH the recreate in the same
  action - and it was, twice. (Still a strong hypothesis, now with a
  successful prediction to its name rather than a proof.)
  Ini: EarlyDock=2 live on the user's machine; compiled default stays 1
  (law 38). EarlyBake stays 1. Escape: EarlyDock=1/0, no rebuild.

v2.41.16 2026-08-01  ** MODE 2 CRASHED AT CITY OPEN - REVERTED, AND IT SHIPPED
                    WITH THE WRONG DEFAULT. My safety inference was wrong. **
  v2.41.15 added EarlyBake=2: scale the dock SUBTREE inside PostCityInit so it
  is never seen at 1x (the user's request - "load our map directly without
  first showing the unscaled map"). It CRASHED on the first city open.

  ⚠ AND IT SHIPPED DEFAULTED TO 2. The ini key protected THIS machine; a fresh
  install without the key would have crashed. Default is now 1. A live escape
  hatch is not a substitute for a safe default - if the only thing standing
  between a user and a crash is a line in an ini they did not write, the
  default is wrong.

  WHAT THE CRASH DISPROVED, and it was MY REASONING, not the game's. I argued
  mode 2 was safe because the PostCityInit ban is about the 456-window FULL
  WALK while this was one subtree of ~25 windows. Wrong, and the log says so:
    ScaleAll done, 431 windows scaled.        <- 25 FEWER than a normal run,
    RCI column ... post-pass x3                  i.e. scaleMap's idempotence
                                                 worked exactly as designed and
                                                 the sweep correctly skipped the
                                                 dock the early pass had claimed
  The early scale ran, the whole later sweep ran, and the process died after.
  So THE THRESHOLD IS NOT WINDOW COUNT. Two byte writes at PostCityInit are
  safe (mode 1, proven over several runs); 25 geometry mutations are not.
  MUTATING WINDOW GEOMETRY during city init is categorically different from
  writing flags, at ANY size. The likely shape - not isolated, so recorded as a
  hypothesis - is that the game's own init sequence continues after us and
  re-lays against a dock whose geometry it did not expect.

  WHAT SURVIVES: mode 1 (bake flags + InvalidateSelf) is untouched and remains
  the shipping behaviour, as does the whole v2.41.12/.13/.14 carry-over, which
  is what actually fixed #89. The user's goal - dock never seen at 1x - is NOT
  abandoned; it needs a lever that is neither the message queue (refuted) nor
  geometry-mutation-inside-PostCityInit (this crash).

v2.41.13 2026-08-01  ** #89 FOUND: OUR OWN REPAIR WAS THROWING THE MAP AWAY.
                    USER-CONFIRMED "soft image then full, it's getting better" **
  Six mechanisms were refuted before this. The cause was inside our own
  surface-recreate block the entire time, and every one of those six died on
  the same unexamined assumption: that the corruption came from OUTSIDE our
  code. Nothing was writing wrong pixels - WE WERE ERASING THE RIGHT ONES.

  THE MEASUREMENT. Sampling the raster [+0x114] on a CENTRE DIAGONAL around
  our pass:
    before our pass   64x64   px FFCFCFC5,003D66B4,007979FF,FFCFCFC5,0073B000
                              distinct=4   <- real terrain: blue, green
    after our recreate 128x128 px 00000000 x5, distinct=1
  The map is present and correct at 64x64 BEFORE we touch it, and all zeros
  after: we destroyed the surface, built a new one and PRE-CLEARED IT TO BLACK,
  so the user watched an empty box until the engine's message-driven bake
  landed. The pre-clear exists to hide uninitialised VRAM and it does - but
  black is not the only non-garbage option.

  THE FIX. Carry the picture across the recreate:
   1. capture the old surface's pixels (GetPixel) BEFORE the destroy;
   2. recreate at the new size - DESTROY/CREATE ORDER UNCHANGED, because that
      is the v2.21.1 crash site and lifetime changes there are how it crashed;
   3. black-fill as the FLOOR, so a partial restore still cannot show garbage;
   4. repaint the captured picture over it, BILINEAR scaled to the new size.
  The map is now continuously visible - soft for one beat, then sharp when the
  bake lands. v2.41.12 shipped this nearest-neighbour and the user confirmed it
  working; v2.41.13 made the transitional frame smooth (fixed-point 8.8, all
  four channels blended - the raster carries meaningful alpha, measured 00 on
  the map body and FF on the border, so it is interpolated too, not forced).

  ⚠ THE INSTRUMENT THAT NEARLY HID IT. The first raster probe sampled p[0],
  p[n/4], p[n/2], p[n-1]. For a 64-wide raster n/4=1024 and n/2=2048 are EXACT
  MULTIPLES OF THE WIDTH, so both landed on COLUMN 0 - three of four samples
  were the border. It returned four identical greys and I read that as "the
  raster is blank", which is not what it showed, and built a conclusion on it.
  Sample a DIAGONAL through the centre and report a DISTINCT COUNT instead of
  eyeballing hex; the corrected probe returned distinct=4 immediately.

  STANDING LESSON, and it is the expensive one: WHEN A REPAIR IS IN THE FRAME,
  CHECK WHAT THE REPAIR DESTROYS BEFORE HUNTING FOR WHAT CORRUPTS.
  Suites: all green. No data change.

v2.41.8  2026-08-01  ** A WILD CALL I SHIPPED, REMOVED; AND I RETRACT THE
                    CONSTRAINT THAT KILLED SIX OF SEVEN #89 CANDIDATES. **
  From the minimap paint-path analysis (13 agents; ZERO candidate mechanisms
  survived refutation - and the empty field turned out to be the finding).

  FIXED - SAFETY. v2.41.6/.7's probe passed `raw + 0x114` to SafeBufProbe,
    which does a VIRTUAL call (QueryInterface). [+0x114] is NOT a COM object:
    it is a plain 3-dword struct {pixel ptr, w, h} - our own fallback uses it
    that way (0x007A7570(raw+0x114, w, h)), and the bake reads it as a raw
    base (0x7A8550). So the probe loaded THE FIRST PIXEL OF THE MAP RASTER AS
    A VTABLE POINTER AND CALLED THROUGH IT. SEH caught the fault, which is
    precisely why it survived review and shipped. Replaced with plain dword
    reads + direct pixel sampling. ⚠ Every `rbuf` field in the v2.41.6/.7 logs
    is meaningless; do not read them as raster data.
  RETRACTED - AND THIS IS THE BIG ONE. "The corruption is present BEFORE our
    sweep runs" was written into four documents as MEASURED. It never was. Its
    only evidence is the probe line `dock 235x223 vis=1 onscreen=1`, and that
    `onscreen` is IsOnScreen() - a pure IsVisible() walk up the parent chain,
    with no rect test, no composition and no pixel read. It proves the
    visibility FLAGS were set. The corruption conclusion had been attached to
    it by the "2x art in 1x windows" theory, WHICH WE THEN REFUTED for this
    window - the argument died and the premise it produced stayed in the file,
    where it became the primary kill in SIX OF SEVEN later candidates. Our own
    record also contradicts it: both user screenshots show the dock at the same
    size, and the correct one is necessarily post-sweep, so the corrupt one is
    too. EVERY CANDIDATE KILLED ON THAT TEST IS RE-OPENED.
  NEW HARD CONSTRAINT (measured, and nobody had stated it): after our pass the
    display surface [+0xF0] is PROVABLY ALL BLACK - we Fill the entire 128x128
    and the log confirms it every run. So if the corrupt pixels are on screen
    after our pass, SOMETHING WROTE NON-BLACK AFTER OUR FILL. The only writers
    (transfers 0x7A66F0/0x7A67F0, bake 0x7A7FF0) are reachable only from
    0x7A8640, which is MESSAGE-DRIVEN, not paint-driven: 0x7A7140 subscribes
    &[this+0xDC] to ids 0x99EF1142/0x99EF1143 gated on the init latch [+0xFC];
    the handler consumes [+0xFE] then [+0xFD]. So the fd=1/fe=1 our pass sets
    is consumed on a MESSAGE TICK - the first structural reason "it corrects
    when the modal is dismissed" could be CAUSAL. Not yet a mechanism: alone it
    predicts a black square and the user reports colour.
  FIXED - THE VTABLE SLOT TABLE IN UiSpike.cpp WAS OFF BY ONE and omitted slot
    89 (Draw) entirely, which is what shifted everything after it. That comment
    cost a wasted probe build. Replaced with the measured table (base cGZWin vt
    0x00A8D000): 93 GetDrawContext = [ecx+0x6c], 94 GetBufferToDrawTo =
    [ecx+0x68], 100 PrivateBuffer(bool) is NOT zero-arg. Also recorded: slot 89
    Draw reaches PlotPresent only if PlotComposite returned true, so a
    [win+0x64] private buffer cannot reach the screen without a paint on the
    same object in the same call. Our InvalidateSelf() calls are unaffected.
  Suites: all green. No data change.

    the MinHook detour on the flyout opener, that hook is never uninstalled,
    and between city 2's PostCityInit and city 2's first sweep (~1-2s,

  FIXED - TWO CONTRADICTING GLYPH-TIER COMMENTS, both retired v2.40.0 wording

v2.41.6  2026-08-01  ** #89 STILL OPEN. FOUR MECHANISMS TRIED, ALL REFUTED.
                    THE HARDENING SHIPPED; THE BUG DID NOT GET FIXED. **
  READ THIS BEFORE TOUCHING THE DOCK MINIMAP AGAIN. Four theories died today
  and each one looked correct until it was measured. Deployed behaviour is
  IDENTICAL to v2.41.3 (the revert); .4/.5/.6 add log-only probes.

  WHAT THE USER ACTUALLY REPORTS (corrected by him, and it invalidated my
  first model): on city open the dock minimap shows CORRUPTION - not the
  region map, not a stale image - and it should show the city map directly.
  It corrects itself later (he dismisses the load-warning modal).

  REFUTED 1 - "run the first pass earlier" (v2.41.0, reverted same session).
    Posted WM_APP message arrived +2016ms after arm; first timer tick +2031ms.
    FIFTEEN ms. The game does not pump messages AT ALL during the load tail,
    so there is no queue to jump. This kills the whole family: WM_TIMER
    cadence, ShowHook, WM_APP. Positive control passed (probe AND first-tick
    control both found the dock at 235x223).
  REFUTED 2 - "data pre-scale the dock subtree" (v2.41.1). FIXED THE MINIMAP,
    BROKE EVERY FLYOUT. kDataScaledSubtreeIds makes ScalePanelRoot RETURN
    EARLY, and the god/mayor flyout DOCKING runs inside that child recursion.
  REFUTED 3 - "data pre-scale the minimap alone" (v2.41.2). Flyouts fine,
    map hung OUTSIDE the dock: the dock's rect is the UNION OF ITS CHILDREN
    WITH NO CLAMP, so one overhanging pre-doubled child grows the union and
    drags the bottom-anchored dock. => NEW LAW: in a union-rect container you
    cannot data-pre-scale SOME children; all or none - and "all" is barred
    here by the docking recursion. The dock is runtime-scaled ONLY.
  REFUTED 4 - "[win+0x6c] is the pixel buffer" (v2.41.4/.5 probes). It is the
    DRAW CONTEXT (our own note at ~:4988). And cIGZWin vtable slots "92/93"
    are NOT GetDrawContext/GetBufferToDrawTo - slot 93 returned [+0x6c]
    verbatim, slot 92 returned NULL, so this file's own slot list is OFF BY
    ONE. That comment also calls 87..97 "exactly the zero-arg draw group"
    while listing SetBufferToDrawTo/SetAreaToDrawTo, which take arguments.
    Do not trust it; do not call slots by guess (wrong arity = stack
    corruption, per the file's own SAFETY note).

  WHAT IS NOW KNOWN AND SHOULD NOT BE RE-DERIVED:
   - STOCK IS CLEAN at the SAME 2400x1600 with our files renamed aside
     (Set-StockCompare, resolution held constant). The defect is OURS.
   - The corruption is present BEFORE our sweep runs (+766..+2250ms measured
     across four runs), while the dock is at DESIGN size and ON SCREEN. So it
     is not caused by our resize.
   - The minimap window has NO art TGI (clsid 0xca318388, winflag_pbuff=yes),
     so it cannot be "2x art in a 1x window".
   - Our full repair sequence SUCCEEDS per the log (surface destroyed,
     recreated at 128, pre-cleared, 0x7A7840 recompute ok zoom=0 fd=1 fe=1).
     Every size in that log looks healthy - which is exactly why the bug
     survived four readings of it.

  SHIPPED AND KEPT (real, independent of #89):
   - MINIMAP TWIN GATE: the block now proves the instance descends from the
     dock before touching its surface, and PRINTS the parent id. The comment
     had claimed "the parent id is now printed" since v2.22.3 - it never was,
     so the one fact identifying the wrong twin was missing. Comment fixed.
   - BOUNDED RETRY on all three surface blocks. They latched the pointer
     after the attempt REGARDLESS of outcome, so a faulted/failed recreate
     was never retried and the window kept a stale 1x surface for the rest of
     the city - the v2.21.0 crash shape made permanent by the guard meant to
     prevent it. Now: latch on success; count failures; give up after 2 so a
     repeating fault cannot loop at sweep cadence. Budgets keyed on the
     pointer, reset in Disarm (second-city law).
   - UDMAP gained the "surface creation FAILED" branch it never had - the one
     case the retry needs to see, previously silent.
   - Warnings written INTO kDataScaledSubtreeIds, build_selective_safe.py and
     the live ini so refuted 1/2/3 cannot be re-proposed.

  PROCESS LESSONS (the expensive ones):
   - The symptom matched a solved family (advisor faces #43: load-time damage
     is cured in DATA, never by a faster sweep) and I applied that cure
     without checking whether THIS container could take it. Matching the
     family is step one; step two is the new host's own constraints.
     CITY-DOCK-OVERLAP.md already documented the union rect. It was read and
     not applied.
   - kDataScaledSubtreeIds conflates TWO powers - "do not scale this" and
     "do not walk here". The dock only needed the first. Check both meanings
     before adding a member.
   - Two log-only probe builds cost nothing and killed two theories. The two
     builds that changed behaviour both shipped regressions. Probe first.

  Suites at ship: Test-DatIntegrity ALL PASS (21 dats, SelectiveArt
  655/655/651), ThirdPartyGates ALL PASS (3), ScaleTierDecide ALL PASS
  (14 + 5000x2), emu_subflyout 71 + 62, placement 4. Art dats byte-reverted
  (2x dat back to 11713020 bytes; minimap area back to design (18,72,82,136)).

v2.41.0  2026-08-01  ** MINIMAP TWIN GATE + BOUNDED SURFACE RETRY; THE WHOLE
                    "SCALE IT EARLIER" FAMILY REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT **
                    Task #89. NO fix for the corrupted city-open minimap
                    yet - this ships hardening and a large negative result.
  - ⛔ REFUTED, then REVERTED IN THE SAME SESSION: a posted-WM_APP channel
    to run the first city pass before WM_TIMER. Theory: WM_TIMER is
    SYNTHESISED only when the queue is empty, so a busy load-tail queue
    starves it and a POSTED message would jump the line. MEASURED: the
    posted message arrived +2016ms after arm; the first tick +2031ms.
    FIFTEEN MILLISECONDS - one timer period. There is no line to jump;
    the game does not pump messages AT ALL during the load tail. This
    kills the entire family: WM_TIMER cadence, ShowHook, WM_APP. The ban
    is written into Settings.h, UiSpike.h, the director and the live ini.
  - POSITIVE CONTROL PASSED (what makes the negative trustworthy): probe
    and first-tick control both reported "dock 235x223 vis=1 onscreen=1,
    minimap found 64x64 blitSize=64". Not blind - and that same line
    proves the mechanism: the dock is ON SCREEN at DESIGN size for ~2s.
  - MECHANISM CORRECTED. It is NOT a stale display surface: scale ->
    recreate is 2ms, sub-frame, invisible. It is 2x ART DRAWN INTO
    1x-SIZED WINDOWS. The dock's art ships doubled IN PLACE at package
    load (refmap: 0x13D14CA0 action=2x-in-place; 0x0987B48F is in
    SCALED_WINDOW_IDS) while the windows stay at design size until the
    sweep. That interval is the corruption.
  - PHASE 0 STOCK PARITY RUN at a HELD resolution (2400x1600, so the mod
    was the only variable). User: "loads correctly at the stock UI
    instantly shows the city map without showing the corrupted one
    first." Predicted clean, was clean - so it is ours.
  - STRENGTHEN (shipped): the MINIMAP block now GATES on the instance
    actually descending from the dock before touching its surface, and
    PRINTS the parent id. The comment had claimed since v2.22.3 that
    "the parent id is now printed" - it never was, so the one fact that
    identifies the wrong twin was missing. Comment corrected.
  - STRENGTHEN (shipped): BOUNDED RETRY on all three surface blocks. They
    used to latch the window pointer AFTER the attempt regardless of
    outcome, so a FAULTED or FAILED recreate was never retried and the
    window kept a stale 1x surface under a 2x rect for the rest of the
    city - the v2.21.0 crash shape made permanent by the guard meant to
    prevent it. Now: latch on success; count failures; give up after 2 so
    a repeating fault cannot loop at sweep cadence. Budgets are keyed on
    the pointer and reset in Disarm (second-city law). UDMAP also gained
    the "surface creation FAILED" branch it never had - it was the one
    case the retry needs to see, and it was silent.
  - THE LESSON, and it was already written down: our own
    kDataScaledSubtreeIds comment says of the advisor faces (#43) that
    the game binds them during CITY LOAD, before our first sweep, so
    "runtime doubling was therefore always too late" and the cure was
    pre-scaled DATA. Same family, same answer. Load-time damage is cured
    in data, never by making the sweep faster. Match the symptom to the
    solved family BEFORE reaching for a new mechanism.
  - NEXT (designed, not built): add 0x0987B48F to the data-pre-scale
    family - double_subtree_areas on I-c973b411 in both group variants,
    plus 0x0987B48F in kDataScaledSubtreeIds so the sweep scales the ROOT
    ONLY. Precedent is the U-Drive-It dashboard 0x4BCB938A (43 scripts,
    same split, shipping). Both halves must land together or the result
    is 4x. Markers 0x0000AAAA stay 1x. Model offline first: if the
    minimap is born 128x128 in data, does the game's own init already
    create a 128 surface?

    ALL PASS (3), ScaleTierDecide ALL PASS (14 + 5000x2), emu_subflyout

v2.40.2  2026-07-31  ** THE DISMISS X IS NOW A REAL TAP TARGET (36px at 2x) **
                    ✅ USER-CONFIRMED ("perfect") - collapsed AND expanded,
                    scrollbar present, X large and clear of the edge.
                    #87 and #88 both CLOSED. Three builds, and each was
                    caught by a test the previous one would have passed:
                    v2.40.0 model right / expanded clipped; v2.40.1 reserve
                    right / X still stock; v2.40.2 confirmed.
                    User, twice: "I don't think it's larger?" / "the x still
                    seems small?" - correct, it was held at stock by v2.40.0's
                    encoding argument, and that argument was too conservative.
  - WHAT UNBLOCKED IT: with the reserve correctly decomposed (v2.40.1), BOTH
    forms declare the SAME row total, W - 9 - round(16f):
        X at stock:  S = round(18f) + 18 + 9 + round(16f)
        X scaled:    S = 2*round(18f)     + 9 + round(16f)
    Scaling the X therefore moves width BETWEEN the X and the headline and
    CANNOT change whether the row fits its pane. The only constraint left is
    the sign-extended imm8 on S itself: 61 / 87 / 113 / 165 at 1x / 1.5x /
    2x / 3x. 113 fits comfortably; only 165 does not.
  - SO: sixteen glyphs at <=2x, twelve at 3x. SelectiveArt 655 / 655 / 651.
    This is the project's ONLY per-tier data difference and it is forced by
    an encoding ceiling, not by taste. Documented in four places.
  - THE CONDITION IS DUPLICATED ON PURPOSE and both sites say so:
    build_selective_safe.py stages the four X ids under `FACTOR <= 2.0`, and
    CodePatches::ApplyAdviceRowScale scales its X column under
    `factor <= kAdviceXScaleMaxFactor` (2.0f). If they ever disagree the
    budget describes art that did not ship and the X clips again - which is
    exactly the failure this task has already produced twice.
  - Log now names all three parts and which branch it took, so a mismatch is
    readable without a rebuild:
      "advice row x2.00 - arrow 36px, X 36px (scaled), scrollbar 32px,
       middle W-61 -> W-113."
    At 3x it reads "X 18px (stock - tier ceiling)".
  - Verified in the DEPLOYED packages, not just the builder: 2x/1.5x carry
    all sixteen with the X glyph at 36x36 / 27x27, 3x carries twelve.
    Test-DatIntegrity ALL PASS at the new per-tier counts.

v2.40.1  2026-07-31  ** THE 25px RESERVE IS THE GUTTER PLUS THE SCROLLBAR -
                        AND OUR SCROLLBAR IS 2x, SO HALF OF IT SCALES **
                    Eyes-on caught this; v2.40.0 was half the answer.
  - v2.40.0 RESULT: collapsed rows PASSED - the X came back and the arrows
    are 2x, so the eviction model is CONFIRMED. But EXPANDING a row clipped
    the X to a sliver. That is the acceptance test I wrote precisely
    because the collapsed-only check cannot fail; it earned its place on
    the first run.
  - CAUSE, measured: expanding a row raises the pane's SCROLLBAR, and the
    pane's usable width is not GetW() - it is GetW() - 2*gutter -
    scrollbarW (sub_9BCBC5 @0x009BCBC5, gutter 5 @0x009BFFCC), with
    scrollbarW fetched LIVE from the scrollbar's own GetW(). Our shipping
    a6 strip is 384x32 at 2x (stock 192x16) and cGZWinScrollbar::SetImage
    sizes the bar as art width / 12, so the bar really is 32px - task #82
    working exactly as designed. Usable width therefore drops to GetW()-42
    while v2.40.0's flat reserve still declared GetW()-25: 17px short,
    which clips 17 of the X's 18px.
  - THE CORRECTION: the reserve is not one number, it is two.
        25 ~= 2*gutter (10) + stock scrollbar cell (16)
        S(f) = round(18f) + 18 + 9 + round(16f)
               arrow        X   fixed  scrollbar
        f=1 -> 61 EXACTLY (the game's own constant, reproduced from its
        parts - a free self-check; if it ever stops reducing to 61 the
        split is wrong), 1.5 -> 78, 2 -> 95, 3 -> 129 clamped to 127.
  - 3x now CLAMPS instead of skipping (ordinance-inset precedent): a
    reserve 2px short clips the edge of the X, whereas skipping leaves the
    arrow column unbudgeted and loses the X entirely. Logged when it fires.
  - We budget for the scrollbar UNCONDITIONALLY rather than detouring
    Refresh to ask whether one exists. A static worst-case reserve is
    correct in BOTH states - no single flat value can be - and costs only
    a slightly wider right margin on scrollbar-less lists.
  - WHAT THIS CORRECTS IN OUR OWN NOTES: v2.40.0 called the 25 a "flat
    unscaled reserve" in CodePatches.cpp, REGRESSION.md, VERSION-HISTORY
    and the builder comment. It is not flat. The v2.40.0 entry below is
    left standing as written (it was true of that build) and this entry is
    the correction - the "still open" item it records is now CLOSED, and
    it closed by costing exactly one eyes-on, which is why it was recorded
    as a risk rather than dismissed.
  - No data change: SelectiveArt stays 651, packages untouched.
    Test-DatIntegrity ALL PASS. Deploy hash-verified.

v2.40.0  2026-07-31  ** THE NEWS ROW X: NOT THE X'S FAULT AT ALL - THE ARROW
                        COLUMN ATE THE ROW'S 25px RESERVE (#87 + #88) **
                    Awaiting eyes-on. Data + code shipped as ONE change.
  - THE BUG: staging 2x art for the news/advice row glyphs fixed the
    expander arrows and DELETED the per-row dismiss X - a functional
    control. FOUR theories were refuted by eyes-on before this one: a
    scrollbar, a clone born at art size, a code-sized clone, and "the X
    glyph's own size moved it" (that last was tested by reverting only the
    four X glyphs - the X stayed gone, which is precisely what pointed at
    the ARROW column).
  - MEASURED (9-agent workflow wf_c722a528-099; the load-bearing bytes
    re-verified by hand against the exe). cSC4WinAdviceList::Refresh
    (0x00793810) is the SINGLE emitter for every advice list in the game -
    news reader, advisor briefings, both My Sims lists, the briefing
    panels and the never-touch ticker marquee - one dword xref image-wide.
    It writes each row as a THREE-COLUMN HTML table:
        <TR><TD WIDTH="18">arrow</TD>
            <TD WIDTH="%d">headline</TD>          %d = pane->GetW() - 61
            <TD WIDTH="18"><A ...close...>X</A></TD></TR>
    61 = 18 + 18 + a flat 25px reserve, from `83 EE 3D` at 0x0079388F. The
    X cell is emitted UNCONDITIONALLY - no dismissible flag, no fit test.
    Column width is the MEASURED cell rect, not the declared one (declared
    WIDTH= lands in col+0x08/+0x0C, which the distribution loop never
    reads), and a container's rect is the UNION of its children with NO
    clamp - so a 36px <IMG> really does grow its cell, eat the reserve,
    and push the X past the pane's content edge.
  - THE FIX: CodePatches::ApplyAdviceRowScale rewrites the subtrahend to
    round(18*f) + 43 (70/79/97 at 1.5x/2x/3x; 61 at f=1, so it is a no-op
    at stock). This RESTORES THE DECLARED TOTAL to the stock GetW() - 25
    by taking the arrow's extra width out of the HEADLINE column. Framing
    matters: "restore the confirmed-good total" is true regardless of
    exactly where the content edge falls; "budget the overflow" would rest
    on the one quantity still not measured live. The code comments say the
    former deliberately.
  - TWELVE GLYPHS, NOT SIXTEEN (SelectiveArt 639 -> 651 at all three
    tiers). `83 EE ib` SIGN-EXTENDS: sixteen would need 2*round(18f)+25 =
    133 at 3x, which encodes as 0x85 = `add esi,123`, so the patch would
    skip while the builder still staged 54px art - this exact bug at the
    one tier nobody eyes-on tests. The four X ids (...53/57/5B/5F) keep
    stock 18px at every tier BY DESIGN.
  - HARD COUPLING, recorded in five places: the twelve glyphs and this
    patch SHIP TOGETHER AND REVERT TOGETHER. Art without the patch IS the
    bug, so [Spike] AdviceRowPatch=0 is NOT a safe revert - it reproduces
    it. The true revert is a data rebuild.
  - WHAT THIS BUILD REFUTED, beyond the four above: (a) editing the two
    ASCII "18" literals at 0x00AB57A3 / 0x00AB56C0 - the declared width
    never reaches the width-distribution loop, and with the container rect
    measured as an unclamped union it cannot bound a single-IMG cell
    either; (b) a runtime detour on Refresh - the constant depends only on
    the ART width, one global fact per boot, so one static byte reaches
    the whole family; (c) routing the site through kBudgetSubImm8Sites,
    which writes round(stock*f) = 122/183, both wrong (61 is 2*glyph plus
    a flat unscaled 25, not a pixel span).
  - CORRECTED IN THIS BUILD (stale notes that were already misleading a
    reader): REGRESSION.md and HANDOFF.md both claimed twelve glyphs were
    SHIPPING at 651 while the builder staged ZERO and the suite asserted
    639, and REGRESSION.md still carried the refuted sentence "the X's
    slot is computed against something that assumes the 18px glyph".
  - STILL OPEN, and free to close at the next eyes-on: the exact
    decomposition of the 25px reserve. sub_9BCBC5 (0x009BCBC5, verified by
    disassembly) computes a text pane's content width as
    GetW() - 2*gutter - scrollbarW, gutter default 5 - and scrollbarW is
    fetched LIVE from the scrollbar's own GetW(), so the reserve a row must
    clear CAN scale with the tier. A flat 25 is bounded-correct at 2x by
    the user-confirmed-good baseline; 1.5x and 3x are asserted by
    construction only.
  - Suites: Test-DatIntegrity ALL PASS (21 dats, SelectiveArt 651 at all
    three tiers) - Test-ThirdPartyGates ALL PASS (3) - Test-ScaleTierDecide
    ALL PASS (14 + 5000x2 sweep) - emu_subflyout 71 + 62 - placement 4.
    Deploy hash-verified.

v2.39.12 2026-07-31 ** TASK #85: THE "CONTRADICTION" WAS A NAMING FAILURE -
                        MADE SELF-EXPLAINING INSTEAD OF EDITED AWAY **
  - Three ids (0xAA8DEF97, 0xC9264BE2, 0x8926EEBE) sit in BOTH
    kCityDialogIds and kNeverScaleIds. Yesterday I read that as a defect.
    It is not one, and the reflex edit would have REMOVED a safety net.
  - THE LISTS GOVERN DIFFERENT MECHANISMS, and the never-scale list says so
    itself: IsNeverScaleId is consulted ONLY by ScaleSubtree and the city
    SWEEP, and the list's own Establish-City note reads "it is served by
    z_SC4UIScale_DialogStatic ... so THE SWEEP must leave it alone" (the
    868x468 -> 1736x936 precedent). kCityDialogIds is the separate
    mechanism for main-window transients the sweep cannot reach. "The
    sweep must not touch it" and "this block may size it if it ever
    arrives unscaled" are compatible statements about one window.
  - MEASURED before deciding (who_owns_tgi.py + staged corpora + stock
    corpus) - all three are DATA-BORN AT EVERY SCALED TIER:
      0xAA8DEF97 I-ca8cbf0f 300x166 -> 450 / 600 / 900   winner today:
        zzz-SC4UIScale\z_SC4UIScale_CamUI-2x.dat at 1000x350 (CAM ships a
        LARGER replacement script; 4 files carry the TGI)
      0xC9264BE2 I-e9263d4c 319x113 -> 479 / 638 / 957   root DialogStatic
      0x8926EEBE I-e9263de5 249x92  -> 374 / 498 / 747   root DialogStatic
    and at stock tier the DLL renames the dats aside and is inert. So this
    block's scaling of them is UNREACHABLE in any shipping configuration -
    they are belt-and-braces for a package-load failure. Deleting the
    entries would leave a failed data load rendering these dialogs at 1x
    with no mechanism left to catch it.
    ⚠ Independent corroboration of the v2.39.9 diagnosis from a different
    instrument: the CamUI winner's 1000x350 is EXACTLY the live arrival
    that the dialog block doubled to the reported 2000x700.
  - SHIPPED INSTEAD OF AN EDIT: (a) the full reasoning + the measured
    ownership table beside kCityDialogIds, with an explicit "⛔ do not fix
    this by consulting IsNeverScaleId here" (that would make the skip
    absolute and throw away the belt-and-braces to remove a double-scale
    the Fresh+width guard already removes by measured state - law 23);
    (b) a reciprocal note on kNeverScaleIds telling the reader to read its
    name as "never scaled BY THE SWEEP"; (c) a self-maintaining assertion -
    a one-shot `DLGLISTS` line naming the overlap each session, so the next
    reader finds an explanation instead of a landmine, and a NEW id added
    to both lists changes the line and announces itself.
  - ZERO BEHAVIOUR CHANGE (comments + one log line). Suites: DatIntegrity
    ALL PASS, ThirdPartyGates ALL PASS.
  - VERIFY: one `DLGLISTS 3 id(s) in BOTH ...` line per session; the
    dialog family (quit/exit confirms, Save City, Text Entry, Set Lot Size,
    save-during-disaster) unchanged.
