v2.39.10 2026-07-31 ** gBarCache: OWNER-KEYED, AND CLEARED ON CITY SWITCH
                        (task #84) **
  - Found in the v2.39.5 arrow audit. The bar-tile cache is filled by a
    family-AGNOSTIC gate (`gDisasterDrawTuning ? destIsContainer :
    destIsSubContainer`), so BOTH the disaster container and the mayor
    sub-flyout container fill it - but the ONLY drain is inside the
    DISASTER ring block. After any sub-flyout paint its tiles sat there
    holding RAW ATLAS POINTERS across opens and across a city switch, and
    the next disaster ring draw would have replayed FOREIGN tiles onto the
    disaster container.
  - FIX, three parts: (a) owner-key the cache - a different container means
    a different paint, so discard first; (b) replay only when the owner
    matches, and clear either way; (c) clear in Disarm with the other
    per-city state (second-city lifecycle law - a cache of dead pointers is
    the same defect as a function-local static holding one).
  - NO SILENT CAPS: the 64-tile cap now logs `BARCACHE saturated` once per
    paint instead of quietly truncating the LayerFix replay.
  - VERIFY: open a mayor sub-flyout, then Create Disaster - bar/ring
    correct, no stray tiles; then switch city and repeat. `BARCACHE
    saturated` should never appear in normal use.

v2.39.9 2026-07-31 ** THE "SAVING DISABLED" BOX AT 4x: A GUARD I SCOPED TO
                       THE CASE I HAD TESTED (task #38 regression) **
                    ✅ USER-CONFIRMED: box renders correctly. See v2.39.11
                    for the follow-up defect the eyes-on could not see.
  - SYMPTOM (user screenshot): the save-during-disaster warning opened
    ~2000px wide with its frame art TILING across the over-wide window.
    Not a mod-state test - the normal shipping config.
  - MEASURED IN ONE LINE, no theorising: MWKID logged
    `id=0xAA8DEF97 vt=00ADC678 (200,241 2000x700)` = EXACTLY 4x its
    500x175 design. So: scaled twice.
  - CAUSE, and it is mine: v2.38.0 made the 6a553aa4/0a55161d confirm
    family DATA-BORN 2x, then added the "this dialog ARRIVED already
    scaled, leave it alone" guard - but SCOPED it to the two confirm ids
    with the comment "law 29 - blast radius ... the Save box 0xAA8DEF97 is
    not covered by this session's testing, so the general guard stays
    unimplemented". The save flow RE-USES THAT SAME DATA-BORN WINDOW under
    id 0xAA8DEF97 (the v2.25.7 note in the same block says so), so it
    arrived 1000x350 and the block doubled it again. The `newW > scrW`
    bail cannot catch it: 2000 < 2400.
  - FIX: the guard is now GENERAL - `w >= dlg.designW * 5 / 4` for every
    id in kCityDialogIds. That is what the per-id `designW` column has
    been declared for since v2.25.6 and what three comment blocks already
    described. It keys on the ARRIVED SIZE, so it is correct in every
    package configuration (root DialogStatic, mod-gated zzz-, or neither)
    with no state test - a 1x birth still scales exactly as before.
  - LAW: SCOPING A GUARD TO THE CASE YOU TESTED LEAVES THE UNTESTED CASES
    UNGUARDED. When the guard's test is on MEASURED STATE rather than on
    identity, narrowing it by id ADDS risk instead of removing it. (Law 29
    blast-radius reasoning is for changes that ACT; it does not apply to a
    guard that only ever declines to act.)
  - STRUCTURAL DEFECT FOUND ALONGSIDE (documented, not yet changed):
    0xAA8DEF97, 0xC9264BE2 and 0x8926EEBE are in kNeverScaleIds - the list
    whose entire purpose is preventing this 4x double-scale - AND in
    kCityDialogIds. IsNeverScaleId is honoured by ScaleSubtree (:4398) and
    the city sweep (:5645) but NOT by the city-dialog block. Two lists
    with opposite intent; the width guard now covers the symptom for all
    three, but the contradiction should be resolved deliberately.
  - VERIFY: trigger a save during a disaster - box at 1000x350 (not
    2000x700), frame art continuous (no tiling), OK button correct.
    Regression: quit + exit-to-region confirms unchanged (DLGBORN prints),
    Save City box normal, budget dialogs untouched.

v2.39.8 2026-07-31 ** THE READ-GUARD DID ITS JOB: BORN METRICS WERE IN THE
                       WRONG OFFSET FRAME (task #80 reopened) **
                    ✅ USER-CONFIRMED same evening: "arrow works now in both
                    modes" (pre-founding + founded god). Task #80 CLOSED.
                    v2.39.6 Signs & Labels also user-confirmed - task #81
                    CLOSED: every flyout in the game is born-modern.
  - Founded-city god-mode eyes-on (the axis the pre-founding "works!"
    confirm did not cover - SCENARIOS law 4): Signs & Labels FLYOPEN2 ✅
    user-confirmed; born-DOCK ✅ (sweep sees (6,502)->(6,502), screenshots
    show position correct from frame 1); but the ARROW still waited for a
    mouse-wheel scroll.
  - The log said why in one phrase, on every open: "metrics left to Plot" -
    the v2.39.5 READ-GUARD had REFUSED the born-metrics write every time.
    It caught a frame-shift error: dstrip is the OUTER SetItemMetrics
    object (vptr at +0, cIGZWin embedded at +4), so its metrics live at
    OBJECT-relative 0xF8/0xFC/0x100 - which is exactly what the proven
    sub-flyout branch writes - while v2.39.5 used the WINDOW-relative
    0xF4/F8/FC trio read out of the strip Plot's disassembly (its `this`
    is the embedded window). Same three fields, +4 embed apart. Without
    the guard this would have been a v2.39.1-class field corruption;
    with it, it was a log line.
  - LAW (added to 21): a disassembly-derived field offset must state its
    FRAME (object vs embedded-window, ±4 here); when two code sites
    disagree by 4, they are probably both right in different frames.
  - Also: DISBORN now prints "born (x,y) docked (x,y)" - the old
    single-position print showed the PRE-dock position and made a working
    born-dock read like a failure.
  - VERIFY (both god modes, pre-founding AND founded): DISBORN prints
    "docked (6,502)" and "metrics BORN"; arrow present on first open with
    no scroll; pickers game-wide single; SUBCLAIM 0.

v2.39.7 2026-07-31 ** DISHEAL BECOMES A REAL REDRAW (arrow lens follow-up) **
  - The workflow's arrow-lens agent landed after v2.39.5 shipped and closed
    the last gap in the mechanism: the container's draw (0x79B0E8) is gated
    on dirty bit [0x114] bit0, which InvalidateSelfAndParents NEVER sets -
    so DISHEAL's forced "repaint" was re-BLITTING the stale cached buffer,
    not re-DRAWING it. The game's own hover/press handlers always do BOTH
    (0x79AF02 `or [0x114],1` + invalidate) and our two SUBHEAL sites already
    follow that protocol. One line: set the dirty bit before the invalidate.
    (v2.39.5's born metrics made the first draw correct anyway; this makes
    the belt-and-braces heal actually able to heal.)
  - Same report refined the atlas map (ring 0..94 | plain bar 94..147 |
    arrow-normal 147..200 | pressed 200..253 | hover 253..306, cap = top 25
    rows / bottom rows 37..62 of the 53-wide sprite) and confirmed the arrow
    flags live at cIGZWin+0x118 (top) / +0x119 (bottom), set via class
    setter 0x79B080 which also ORs the dirty bit - the game's own protocol.
  - LATENT (new task): gBarCache is filled by BOTH twins' bar draws but
    cleared only in the disaster ring block; after a mayor sub-flyout use it
    stays pinned (cap 64) holding raw atlas pointers across opens.

v2.39.5 2026-07-31 ** THE FIRST-OPEN DOCK + THE ARROW WAS A DECISION, NOT A
                       FRAME (task #80) **
  - The v2.39.4 eyes-on found two first-open-of-session residuals: wrong
    position until a mouse HOVER, and the down arrow missing even AFTER the
    hover. The live log + disassembler closed both; a 12-agent read-only
    workflow (6 lenses -> 3 designs -> 3 refuters) audited the design.
  - DOCK: gDisDockValid was a latch that could only warm while a flyout was
    OPEN - cold on open 1 by construction. The sweep docked the rect 36ms
    later + invalidated, and the rect never moved again; what the user saw
    snap on hover was PIXELS - the thumbnail strip is PARENTLESS, a container
    move does not carry it, only the game's own re-layout does. CURE: the
    sweep warms the dock cache from the scaled toolbar EVERY tick (before any
    flyout exists), so birth docks on open 1 and the game lays its own strip
    right. Byte-verified: the builder HIDES the container right after Place,
    so the born position can never paint.
  - ARROW: DISHEAL (v2.39.4) fired once, correctly, and cured NOTHING - the
    diagnosis was wrong. The container Plot only READS byte flags
    [0x118]/[0x119] (born 0) to choose plain-cap vs ARROW-cap atlas cells;
    the open flow computes "scroll needed?" as
    visibleRows = (stripWinH+spacing)/(itemH+spacing)  (0x79AA70, read from
    the instructions) - and v2.39.4's birth state MIXED UNITS: 2x window, 1x
    pitch -> (578+5)/49 = 11 >= 9 disasters -> "fits" -> no arrow, forever.
    A scroll recomputed with 2x metrics -> 6 < 9 -> arrow "appeared". CURE:
    BORN ITEM METRICS - write the strip's 0xF4/F8/FC to base*f at birth, so
    (578+10)/98 = 6 < 9 and the arrow is BORN. Safe now (unlike v2.39.1):
    both latches are already primed from the builder's STOCK 44/44/5 before
    Place, and a READ-GUARD refuses unless the fields still hold exactly the
    stock bases (wrong offset/layout -> logged no-op, never a second scale).
    Blast-radius audit: ZERO structural paths to shared gStripBase*.
  - Also: EnsureBufferClassBltHook() now installs at birth (the chrome
    corrector used to need one hooked Plot first - first frames of a
    session's first open painted uncorrected); the stale FlashGuard comment
    that MISLED v2.39.4 is corrected in-source; the settled dock log line is
    one-shot per open (was 867 lines in a 23s open); TRIAGE gains the rule
    "a stale frame that survives a REPAINT is a stale DECISION".
  - OFFLINE PROOF (emu_subflyout --builder=disaster, 53 -> 62 checks): stock
    6 rows -> arrow; v2.39.4 state 11 rows -> NO arrow (the suite REPRODUCES
    the shipped bug); v2.39.5 state 6 rows = stock -> arrow. First measured:
    the bug was 2x-SPECIFIC (1.5x lands 8 rows < 9), and 1.5x born diverges
    one row from stock (5 vs 6) - task #75 family, printed loudly.
  - Levers: [Disaster] BornDock / BornMetrics (NEW, exact-match) / BornScale.
  - AWAITING EYES-ON: DISBORN must print "at (6,502)" and "metrics BORN";
    arrow on first open with no scroll/hover; then pickers game-wide single.

    flyouts. This was the first flyout we did so maybe it needs to be
    fixed/upgraded as well." Exactly right. Disaster was the FIRST flyout we
    ever scaled (v2.11.x) and was still on that era's mechanism - ScaleGodFlyouts
    sizes it only once IsVisible() is true - while every other flyout had been
    upgraded twice since (pre-scale-while-hidden for the id-bearing ones,
    born-at-Place for the nested sub-flyout).
  - ROW 1 WAS NOT AVAILABLE, and that was MEASURED not assumed: archived DPROBE
    shows the container at FOUR different pointers in ~60s of one session
    (29B6C618, 29B6C418, 29B6B818, 29B6C618 AGAIN - the heap address recycled
    within 11s). It is created fresh per open, so you cannot pre-scale it while
    hidden. Row 4 (born-at-Place) was the only lever. That recycling also makes
    Classify's id==0 address-reuse hazard real and OBSERVED.
  - PROVEN OFFLINE FIRST. sub_79AD00 (Place) is SHARED by both builders, so the
    emulator needed new INPUTS, not repointing: emu_subflyout.py --builder=disaster
    = 53 checks, born 282x678 at n=6, strip rel X 184, born == sweep at every
    tier, matching three independently recorded live numbers. The default run
    still gives the identical 71-check sub-flyout pass.
  - ANCHORS re-verified from the exe (they were an unchecked decode, absent from
    the repo): 0x7E74D3 `ff 52 14` = call [edx+0x14] = Place; accept return
    0x7E74D6 `8b 57 04`; delta(SetLayout->Place) = 0x25 in BOTH twins;
    sub_7E7270 has ONE caller (0x7F4D2C, via cmp esi,0x69B9324A) and ZERO
    raw-address occurrences. The container is ANONYMOUS - no SetID anywhere in
    it - so REGRESSION.md:2132's "same class, DIFFERENT id" is wrong.
  - THE BLOCKER, fixed: DrainBornScaleRecords() ran inside ScaleGodFlyouts,
    AFTER the generic walk. The sub-flyout survives that because the walk skips
    it by id; the ANONYMOUS disaster container is skipped by nothing, so a born
    282x678 would have come back Fresh and been scaled AGAIN to 564x1356. The
    drain now runs at the top of ScalePanelsUnder; it is a plain queue drain, so
    the later call is a harmless no-op.

  ⛔ TWO BREAKAGES I SHIPPED, BOTH THE SAME ROOT MISTAKE - changing something
     that FEEDS an existing mechanism without first reading what that mechanism
     does with it:

  - v2.39.0 THUMBNAILS WENT TINY. Marking the container born made Classify
    return AlreadyScaled, so the sweep SKIPPED THE WHOLE SUBTREE - including the
    strip item metrics it had been scaling all along. An 88x578 strip window
    full of 44px cells. This is the v2.36.2 law ("a born-correct window needs
    its STATE born too") which the v2.39.0 comment QUOTED and then did not
    apply. WHEN BORN-SCALING TAKES A WINDOW OFF THE SWEEP, IT INHERITS
    EVERYTHING THE SWEEP WAS QUIETLY DOING FOR IT.
  - v2.39.1 DUPLICATED ICONS GAME-WIDE - a regression of yesterday's #55/#56
    fix. SlotThunk2<88> (:1924) latches its 1x base from a strip's own fields on
    the FIRST Plot, then writes base*f forever - and gStripBase* is SHARED BY
    EVERY STRIP IN THE GAME. Writing 0xF8 = 88 at birth, before that latch ran,
    made it latch 88 and start writing 176; every picker cell went double-width
    and showed both art states side by side. Law 30, warned about VERBATIM in
    the comment directly above that block.
    CURE (v2.39.2): do NOT write metrics at birth. Instead PRIME the shared
    latch from the disaster builder's own STOCK argument (44/44/5) at its
    SetItemMetrics call site 0x007E72AF - the same thing the sub-flyout twin
    does at :3320 - so the latch can never see a scaled value. Both builders
    pass 44/44/5, so whichever arrives first sets the identical base.
  - ALSO MINE: an attempt to disable the path via the ini silently did nothing,
    because the guard tested for the SUBSTRING "BornScale" and matched the
    existing SubBornScale key. I would have reported it off while it was on.

  - v2.39.3 THE DOCK, measured exactly: born (63,688) -> docked (6,502), a
    186px jump one tick later, which was the whole of the remaining open-jump.
    The target is a pure function of the already-scaled toolbar plus two ini
    offsets, identical every tick, so the sweep now CACHES it and birth applies
    it - no tree walk, no toolbar read inside the game's own call. The sweep
    then finds the window already at target and its `cl != targetL` test makes
    the later move a no-op. Separate [Disaster] BornDock lever.
  - v2.39.4 THE SCROLL ARROW. User: "not there when you first load, only after
    you scroll does it appear." It was never missing - it was UNPAINTED. The
    container paints its first frame before both vtable swaps are in; once the
    chrome goes live nothing asks it to repaint, so the stale frame stays until
    the user scrolls. Scrolling was doing by hand what the fix now does
    automatically: ONE forced repaint at the moment the chrome becomes live.
    This is a forced repaint, NOT paint suppression - FlashGuard blanked windows
    and stays permanently banned; this asks an already-correct window to draw
    itself again. ONE-SHOT, pointer-keyed: that block runs on EVERY sweep tick
    while the flyout is open (809 times in the measured session).
  - Levers: [Disaster] BornScale / BornDock, both live. NOTE the ini key
    BornScale is NOT present in the shipped ini - it must be ADDED under
    [Disaster] (line ~245) to take effect; the DLL default is 1 = enabled.
  - #76 GUARDS HELD THROUGHOUT: session SUBCLAIM count 0, SUBHEAL never fired,
    DCLAIM 53 -> 106 still firing, no second orange bar.

v2.38.2/.3 2026-07-31 ** THE MODEL WAS BLIND TWICE, AND BOTH BLIND SPOTS
                         WERE HIDING SHIPPED DEFECTS **
  - The offline model is the thing we plan against ("model first, game last"),
    so before touching the last three defects it was audited. It had two
    measured blind spots, and NEITHER was theoretical.
  - (a) THE BUILDER CENSUS SCANNED 18% LESS THAN ITS OWN COMMENT CLAIMED.
    tools\uimap\census.py listed byte patterns for `call [reg+0xD4/0xDC/0xE0]`
    while its comment said "d4/d8/dc/e0": there was NO 0xD8 pattern for any
    register, and `ebp` was absent entirely. Measured: 180 of 1024 window-rect
    call sites unscannable, 73 at edx+0xD8 alone. The patterns were also only
    3 bytes, so `call [eax+0x1D4]` matched as if it were +0xD4.
    FIX: generate the set from VT_NAME x a register table (RECT_CALL_PATS,
    7 regs x 4 offsets, full 6-byte form) so it cannot drift from its own
    documentation again.
    RESULT: discovered builders 101 -> 116 (+20 real, -5 false positives, each
    loss verified individually as a +0x1D4/0x1DC/0x1E0 mis-match).
    ⚠ THE 20 INCLUDE sub_7EAEB0 (the sub-flyout builder we fixed BY HAND in
    v2.36.0), sub_7A04F0 (the Data Views re-lay, v2.37.0) and sub_78DFF0 (the
    generic message-box builder) - builders we had already shipped fixes for
    and that this model had never once seen. TREAT EVERY PRE-v2.38.2 "the
    offline model found nothing" NULL AS WORTHLESS. That warning now lives in
    the generator so it survives regeneration of BUILDER-CENSUS.md.
  - (b) BOTH DAT BUILDERS ANALYSED A STOCK-ONLY CORPUS - 330 scripts from the
    game archives, no plugin script in it - so every "exclusive => safe to
    double 2x in place" judgement was computed over a corpus that cannot see a
    plugin referrer. That is the #79c blind spot, structural.
    NEW: tools\uiscripts\winning_corpus.py resolves the true load-order winner
    per .UI TGI across all 147 archives, extracts third-party winners into
    extracted-plugins\, and writes WINNING-CORPUS.md + winning-corpus.json.
    Self-check that it models load order correctly: 12 third-party scripts
    exist but only 9 WIN - the other 3 are exactly the ones we already took
    over (SaveWarningUI x2, ThirdPartyUI x1).
  - ⛔ IT IMMEDIATELY FOUND SIX LIVE DEFECTS, ALL CAM. CAM Core replaces NINE
    stock .UI scripts and SIX are dialog-static TARGETS, so we shipped doubled
    copies of scripts the game never loads:
      ca8cbf0f generic 1-btn popup  300x166 -> CAM 500x175  (wrong SHAPE)
      8aa9aa14 startup splash       4 nodes -> CAM 6 nodes
      2a554f6d query panel          292x284 -> CAM 300x480, 21 -> 45 nodes

      ca8b8564 query panel          moved (246,202) -> (570,200)
      ea565970 query panel          292x275 -> CAM 304x297
  - FIX (v2.38.3): zzz-SC4UIScale\z_SC4UIScale_CamUI-<tier>.dat, 6 entries,
    built from CAM'S OWN scripts (never the stock ones - that reverts CAM's
    layouts), gated on BOTH CAM_Extended_Essentials.dat (2817430) and
    CAM_Intro.dat (1001294). Remove CAM -> the package disables itself and our
    root stock copies take over. Update CAM -> the fingerprint fails, our stale
    copies disable, dialogs fall back to runtime scaling.
    ONE PACKAGE PER OWNING MOD, deliberately: a copy of mod A's script must not
    ride in a package gated on mod B.
  - The builder now ASSERTS that a TARGET's winning script is the one it read,
    with a reasoned KNOWN_THIRD_PARTY_TARGETS list (empty today - all six are

    source cannot see them. The report now IMPORTS the builder's real TARGETS -
    two instruments disagreeing about one fact is the failure being removed.
  - The LEFT1X guard was too strict and was corrected by measurement: a
    DANGLING art ref (no 1x source in any archive, e.g. {46a006b0,ea7f0eae},
    referenced by BOTH the stock and CAM splash) is harmless - there is nothing

    out of ScaleTier.cpp (so the test cannot drift from the shipped gate) and

    kThirdPartyDeps shifted ThirdPartyUI from [1] to [2] while its call site
    still read depOk[1] - CoriBoom's package would have been gated on CAM.
    Gates are now looked up BY PACKAGE NAME (DepOkByName), removing the class.
    Also caught by the compiler: a scripted edit wrote L"...\z_..." with a
    single backslash (warning C4129), which would have made every lookup miss
    and every gate silently return "ungated".

v2.38.1 2026-07-31 ** THE FIRST-OPEN JUMP WAS OUR OWN CENTRING (task #79c) **
                      USER-CONFIRMED WORKING.
  - v2.38.0 killed the size flash and a POSITION jump took its place: first
    open of a session moved 213px, every open after that perfect. Textbook
    uninitialised latch (the v2.36.2 law) - opens #2+ were not faster, they
    INHERITED the moved position. The latch was one WE created.
      open #1: born 540x324 at (930,425) -> moved to (930,638)   <- the jump
      open #2: born 540x324 at (930,638) -> already there        <- silent
  - SC4's placement rule, READ OUT OF THE EXE rather than inferred:
      0078E3DF  sub edi,eax; mov eax,0x55555556; imul edi; ...  y = (H-h)/3
      0078E409  cdq; sar eax,1                                  x = (W-w)/2
    Horizontally centred, vertically ONE THIRD down - deliberately a little
    above centre. Checked against three measured births first: h=162 -> 479,
    h=175 -> 475 (the Save box), h=324 -> 425, all exactly (1600-h)/3.
  - So (930,425) was never wrong - it is exactly where stock SC4 puts a
    540x324 modal. THE v2.37.5 SCREEN-CENTRING WAS THE DEFECT. It was added
    when the dialog was landing at (795,317) from the creep AND
    scale-about-own-centre, and only became visible as a jump once the dialog
    started being born at its true size.
  - CURE: delete the centring (user's call: keep stock parity). Both paths now
    use the game's own rule, so the data-born path and the runtime path land on
    the same pixel and cannot disagree. Still drift-proof by construction.
  - LAW 14 ADDED: before correcting a window the game just placed, check that
    the game was WRONG. A first-open jump is as likely to be our correction as
    the game's error, and the cure for one is never a faster correction.
  - DLGBORN now prints the game's rule beside the actual birth, so a future
    mismatch is visible instead of silently corrected.

                      SCRIPT (task #79c) **
  - The two in-city confirms were born at stock 270x162 and snapped to 540x324
    a tick later. Since 2026-07-26 this was recorded as "the game builds them
    through a code path that BYPASSES the DBPF override". THAT WAS WRONG.
    Nothing was bypassed: a THIRD-PARTY MOD supplies both scripts and beats us
    on load order.
      Plugins\150-mods\cyclone-boom.save-warning.1.0.sc4pac\
        SaveWarning_Disable_Exit_Quit.dat   {0,96A006B0,6A553AA4} + {..0A55161D}
    Root Plugins files load BEFORE subfolders (the LOAD-ORDER LAW we proved in
    task #44 and built zzz-SC4UIScale\ for), so our root DialogStatic copy could
    never win. The same failure class, already solved once, not recognised here.
  - THE FINGERPRINT, one pixel: stock 6a553aa4 is area=(332,232,602,393) =
    270x161; the MOD's is (332,232,602,394) = 270x162; the LIVE dialog logged
    270x162. The live rect matched NEITHER stock NOR our staged copy - which is
    the tell that a third file owns the TGI, and is now a TRIAGE row plus a new
    permanent tool, tools\dbpf\who_owns_tgi.py (prints every holder in load
    order; the last line wins).
  - FIX 1, DATA: zzz-SC4UIScale\z_SC4UIScale_SaveWarningUI-<tier>.dat, built
    from the MOD'S OWN scripts through the identical DialogStatic transform
    (build_dialog_static.py TP_TARGETS + thirdparty-src\). Never the stock
    scripts - that would silently revert the mod's function. Scripts only:
    {46a006b0,144161e4/eb} are already 2x IN PLACE in the root package and the
    mod ships no art. 2 entries per tier.
  - FIX 2, THE GATE (this is what makes BOTH mod states correct): ScaleTier
    gains kThirdPartyDeps. A package built from another mod's data is enabled
    ONLY while that mod is installed - found by NAME recursively, because
    sc4pac folder names carry a version - plus a 2408-byte fingerprint so a mod
    UPDATE also disables our now-stale copy. z_SC4UIScale_ThirdPartyUI
    (CoriBoom) is gated the same way, which fixes a LATENT BUG IN SHIPPED CODE:
    measured 2026-07-31, with that mod deleted our zzz- copy (532x640) still
    beat the stock script (531x406) - our override was keeping a REMOVED mod's
    UI alive. That trap was written down at MAYOR-MODE.md:126 and had only ever
    been applied inside a manual test script.
    CoriBoom is gated on PRESENCE ONLY (name prefix, no size check): that
    package feeds a runtime-scaled panel, so disabling it on a version bump
    would reintroduce the task #44 corruption. Gate only on the case that is
    unambiguously wrong.
  - FIX 3, THE GUARD THAT WAS ONLY EVER A COMMENT: designW has sat in
    kCityDialogIds since v2.25.6 with three comment blocks describing a
    w < designW*5/4 guard, and NOTHING EVER READ IT. Now implemented, scoped to
    0xAA921F4F / 0x6AAEEC4A. It keys on the size the dialog ARRIVES at, never on
    which package supplied it - so mod-installed, mod-removed and mod-updated
    are all correct with no state test anywhere:
      mod installed -> our zzz- override  -> born 540x324
      mod removed   -> root DialogStatic  -> born 540x322  (the 2px tell)
      mod updated   -> gate trips, nobody -> born 1x -> runtime scale as before
  - TWO LATENT BUGS DIED WITH IT. (a) 0x6AAEEC4A IS data-doubled by the root

    dialog size, no term from the current position, so it cannot compound. That

    CODE_BOUND_TGIS could ever reach it. Nine of the bubble's ten arts were
    already 2x - this was the tenth.
  - WHY IT DOUBLED, from the class's own draw (vtable 0x00AB64B8 +0x160 =
    0x00797CC0): the SOURCE rect's width is taken from the WINDOW, not the
    art - src.L = (imgW-winW)>>1 (D1 FF sar edi,1), src.R = winW+src.L
    (03 CF add ecx,edi), dst = the full window rect. Stock 102-wide art in a
    204-wide window gives src.L=-51, src.R=153: a 204-wide read across a
    102-wide image = two copies. At 204x52 it is src.L=0, src.R=204, exact
    1:1, and the row divisor (imgH-1) scales with the sheet so the fill level
    is unchanged.
  - A THIRD BLIT BEHAVIOUR, now in the SDK (law 35): dst-follows-src (GZWinBMP
    plain), stretch (9-slice), and now SRC-FOLLOWS-DST - the only one where
    under-sized art TILES instead of shrinking.
  - TWO FALSE NEGATIVES RETIRED, and they are why this sat open since
    2026-07-30 marked "not ours / outside the SDK": RGKID never printed the
    bar (it stops one level above it - and law 20 had ALREADY recorded this
    exact bar as skipped), and the RatingArrowPatch=0 A/B tested the HUD
    controller 0x7E86C0-0x7E8A80, which this class never touches. Both nulls
    were structural. Law 34 minted: two blind instruments agreeing is worth
    exactly as much as one. The wrong row is deleted from SC4-UI-ENGINE.md's
    boundary table, with the post-mortem left in its place.

v2.37.0 2026-07-31 ** DATA VIEWS LEGEND BORN CORRECT (task #78) **
  - The Data Views legend JUMPED on every view switch: the game re-lays it
    on each selection from 1x origin constants and our DVPIN sweep pass
    could only correct it one presented frame later. Cured by scaling the
    origins inside the game's own re-lay - the legend is now laid down
    already scaled and there is nothing left to correct.
  - sub_007A04F0 is provably the ONE choke point: a whole-image scan of the
    7,876,608-byte exe finds the window-id literals 0x8A909E00 / 0x8A909E10
    at exactly four addresses, all inside it (13 direct callers, no vtable
    slot). Per legend entry it does
      chip[0x8A909E10+k].SetArea(371, edi+61, GetW()+371, GetH()+edi+61)
      row [0x8A909E00+k].SetArea(278, edi+24, GetW()+278, GetH()+edi+24)
      edi += 18*ceil(h/18)          ; h = MEASURED text height
    so this is ENGINE 4.7 ROW 3 - patch the builder's constants - and the
    patch is 8 sites: four origins, each in TWO encodings (the L/T write
    and the add/lea computing R/B). All eight expected byte strings were
    dumped from the shipping exe and matched before a line was written.
  - THE PITCH IS NEVER PATCHED. It is composed at runtime from the measured
    row height and already lands on 36 with the 2x font; patching it would
    double-scale it.
  - A SECOND, PERSISTENT DEFECT FIXED WITH IT. kDVPins is a fixed uniform
    18px-pitch table, but the game's step is per-row, so a label that wraps
    to two lines gets a 72px slot. Measured 09:32:19.577: the game laid nine
    rows at 24,60,96,132,168,240,276,312,348 and the pin wrote ...192,228,
    264..., dragging EIGHT windows up by 36px. That was a wrong layout on
    screen, not a one-frame artifact. Patching the origin leaves the game's
    own deltas untouched, so both defects go together. The 18 legend ids are
    now skipped by the pin when all 8 sites take; where a site cannot take
    (3x: 61*3=183 overflows the lea disp8) the fallback SHIFTS by the origin
    delta instead of writing the table, and is idempotent because the game
    restarts its accumulator at 0 so index 0 sits at exactly the stock
    origin until we move it.
  - NEW INSTRUMENT DVLEG, and the reason it exists: "zero DVPIN lines" is
    also what a pass that never ran prints. DVLEG reads back what the GAME
    laid down (resolved row/chip counts + the row-Y list, change-only), so
    the null has a positive control and a flattened list is visible.
  - Both runbook entries that had recorded the BUG as the pass criterion
    ("a burst of DVPIN lines then silence" - REGRESSION.md, RUN-SHEET 1.8)
    are rewritten. An acceptance test that never looks at the transient
    frame cannot see a transient defect.
  - DLL-only; no data packages move. Escape hatch [UiSpike]
    DataViewLegendPatch=0 + restart restores v2.36 behaviour exactly.

  - Diagnostic-only builds. No behaviour change; all probe levers ship at 0.

    had already seen, so a window CREATED on pause was silently baselined and
    never reported. Since the pause badge visibly appears, "zero flips" had
    ruled out only ONE of the two ways a window can arrive. NEW windows now

    ⚠ Its zero is NOT evidence: with the hook armed, every destination was

    not have seen a full-screen border in principle.

    two gold thresholds); the 9-slice helper 0x8D8800 has exactly 6 callers,
    all ordinary UI widgets; both anonymous full-screen classes are excluded
    by their own code (vt 0x00AB8CD0 = animating list, vt 0x00AB8F50 = a
    3-line delegating wrapper).

    path, outside every mechanism this project owns - same category as #72.

    Only remaining foothold if resumed: hook the DirectDraw PRIMARY SURFACE.
  - LESSON, now in METHOD.md: six probes this session returned nulls that were
    really blind spots (one root, two levels, flips-only, unarmed hook, wrong
    surface). A null is evidence ONLY after proving the instrument could have
    seen the thing.

v2.36.2 2026-07-30 ** FIRST-OPEN CHROME FIXED AT BIRTH - user-confirmed **
  - USER, with a screenshot: "The very first sub flyout I select when loading
    a map for the first time does this... only on the very first opening on
    that city since last save (notice the misaligned ring and detachment)."
  - NOT the size jump - that stayed fixed. The container was already 258 wide
    on its first paint. What was late was the CHROME STATE: the claim
    promotion and the instance draw hooks. Measured across ten consecutive
    opens: birth -> SUBCLAIM was 159ms (9 frames) on open #1 and 30-48ms on
    #2-#10. Opens #2+ never showed it because they INHERIT the latched state
    from the previous open - they are not faster, they are pre-warmed. Open #1
    of a city has nothing to inherit.
  - FIX: InstallSubFlyoutHooksNow(sub, strip) runs the SUBHOOK/SUBCLAIM
    install AT BIRTH from inside the Place detour - the sweep's own sequence,
    unchanged, behind the same kHookParents crash guard and the same
    0x00AB6AA8 / 0x00AB6D88 vtable checks.
  - ORDER IS THE SAFETY ARGUMENT: [0xE0] is DUAL-USE (hit-claim width AND a
    Plot layout inset). SlotThunk<88> presents the latched 1x value to the
    draw group. Promote [0xE0] before installing that thunk and the game
    paints a SECOND orange bar (v2.11.24). Container thunks first, always.
  - VERIFIED (user: "Tested it and it looked correct"). Log proof: the
    SUBBORNHOOK line precedes the container's first DCBUF by 8ms (was 159),
    and the sweep's SUBCLAIM count for the whole session is ZERO - it found
    the claim already at 106 and skipped, which is the idempotency proof.
    SUBHEAL also never fired once.
  - Instrument note worth keeping: the sweep's "SUBHOOK ... installed" line
    logs every sweep while a menu is open (194 times in that session), not
    once per install - the install is gated separately. That line is what
    made the first timing read look like an install gap. SUBCLAIM was the
    honest signal. Fix the wording next time the file is touched.

v2.36.1 2026-07-30 ** FLYOUTS SCALED AT OPEN - user-confirmed (task #50) **
  - v2.36.0 fixed the NESTED container and the user still saw the jump. The
    log settled it: the born-scale hook fired ZERO times and the id
    0x8A6E61E0 never appeared - the menus being tested were the FIRST-LEVEL
    tool flyouts. Their own log lines carried the cost: "mayor flyout
    0x699306ED ... +10 win (docked)" = 10 windows scaled at the moment the
    flyout was ALREADY on screen. One 18.34ms frame at 1x.
  - The code's own comment had stopped at the constraint: these are
    "DESTROYED AND RECREATED on every open rather than hidden, so there is
    no pre-scale while hidden to do". The answer is the same as for the
    nested container - act at the OPEN, not on the next tick.
  - ONE FUNNEL. All seven call sites naming a tool-flyout id (Zones x2,
    Civic, Transportation, Utilities, Landscape x2) end in
    call sub_7E5C10, whose arg2 IS the flyout id (the function compares it
    against [this+0x200] - its own click-again-to-close test). __thiscall,
    ret 0x10. Hooking the funnel also picked up 0xCA35CBED and the
    Emergency flyout with no new id list.
  - WHAT RUNS THERE IS THE EXISTING PASS, UNCHANGED. ScaleGodFlyouts is
    built to run every ~16ms and is idempotent via scaleMap, so this changes
    only WHEN - no new geometry path, nothing that can double-scale.
    Guarded by the inPass latch. Lever: [Flyout] BornOnOpen=0.
  - VERIFIED IN GAME (user: "looking pretty good"). The proof is the
    timestamps: every "+N win" line is now followed by its FLYOPEN line in
    the SAME millisecond, and no scale line appears at a later timestamp
    anywhere in the session. The offline model also predicted the nested
    rects exactly, including n=7 (258x776) and n=8 (258x874) - two item
    counts never measured live before.
  - Known cosmetic (log only): sub_7E5C10 is also the CLOSER, so a close
    logs "FLYOPEN ... now (-1,-1) -1x-1". Harmless no-op; suppress next build.

v2.36.0 2026-07-30 ** SUB-FLYOUT BORN-SCALED - the flash fix (task #50) **
  - USER'S CORRECTION that pointed at the cure: "It's not a flash it shows
    the prescaled version for a split second. You fixed this issue before
    when fixing the Avatar faces." The avatar/advisor cure was pre-scale
    while HIDDEN so the window is BORN correct. The sub-flyout could never
    use it because it does not exist until the click - so we scale it at
    the equivalent instant for a runtime-built window: its CONSTRUCTION.
  - MECHANISM. The container (0x8A6E61E0) is built fresh on every open and
    is born VISIBLE at 1x, so the game paints 1-2 genuine stock-size
    frames (20-36ms) before the sweep's next tick. A detour on its own
    Place (0x0079AD00) now scales the FINISHED rects on return: the
    container, the strip rect still sitting in [0x108..0x114] where
    GetStripRect reads it four instructions later, the strip's item
    metrics (after Place - scaling them before feeds GetDesiredSize and
    yields 432 where the live height is 482), and the dock delta.
  - NOT the v2.34/v2.35 approach: no builder constant, no field, no exe
    byte is touched. Those promoted the constants in vf10 (SetLayout) and
    drove [+0xEC] = artH - 2*[+0xE8] to -47. Here the game computes its
    whole 1x layout undisturbed and we scale the output.
  - OFFLINE PROOF FIRST (tools\uimap\emu\emu_subflyout.py, new): runs the
    game's OWN sub_79AD00 under Unicorn for n=1..8 at f=1/1.5/2/3 and
    asserts born == sweep == the six measured live rects, plus the 129
    width invariant and the n=1 Freight floor of 103. 71 checks, PASS.
    born == sweep is an IDENTITY, so the settled state cannot change -
    only the first 1-2 frames do.
  - Two traps defused, both general: (1) scaleMap is keyed on the window
    POINTER, so a born-scaled window looks Fresh to the sweep and would be
    scaled twice (129->258->516) - DrainBornScaleRecords() hands both
    windows over as AlreadyScaled; (2) SlotThunk2<88> LATCHES the 1x item
    metrics and writes base*f every Plot - born-scale first and the latch
    becomes 88, so it would write 176. The base is now primed from the
    builder's own SetItemMetrics argument.
  - Levers, live-tunable in [Flyout]: SubBornScale (1) and SubBornDock (1).
    Dock is separable on purpose - the sweep can only dock one tick AFTER
    it scales (its law needs a ring blit at the new size), which is the
    second settle; if a menu ever lands wrong, SubBornDock=0 isolates
    placement from size with no rebuild.
  - STATUS: built, offline-proven, deployed (hash-verified). NOT yet
    eyes-on confirmed.

v2.30-v2.35.1 2026-07-30 ** THE FLASH: DECODED, NOT FIXED (task #50) **
  - USER'S PRECISE REPORT (this corrected the whole target): "ALL OF THE
    FLASHING HAPPENS IN SUB PANELS AND THEIR SUB PANELS AND THEIR SUB
    PANELS NEVER THE MAIN 3 OF GOD / MAYOR / MY SIM." The HUD and the
    three main toolbars are fine.

    which is the window's OWN bit - and both cGZWin ctors set
    [this+0xC8]=0x8903, so EVERY window is born "visible". Corrected to an
    ancestor walk (the engine's own on-screen test). It then measured 12
    panels scaled while genuinely on screen. BUT the instrument sits at
    UiSpike.cpp:4321 while the sub-flyout skip does `continue` at 4269, so
    it was STRUCTURALLY BLIND to the flyouts the user was describing.
  - v2.32.0 SetFlag show hook (vt+0x110 = 0x0099DB6B; note vt+0x10C is
    GetFlag, a READER - project lore was wrong). Shipped LOG-ONLY and
    measured: the ids that fire are DISJOINT from the ids that flash,
    because on-demand windows are born visible and never transition.
    Hook code retained, ShowHook=0.
  - v2.33.0 DATA pre-scale of the 8 HUD roots -> BROKE MAYOR MODE (panels
    cut off and shifted). Reverted v2.33.1 the same session. Composed HUD
    panels re-lay at runtime and have game-created children: Data Views
    has 152 live windows vs 146 scripted area= entries, and that 4% gap
    was the tell - noted, and shipped anyway.
  - v2.34.0/v2.35.0 sub-flyout BORN-2x (builder sub_7EAEB0 constants, then
    constants + the 2x bar atlas). The bar is a 9-slice whose middle is
    [+0xEC] = artHeight - 2*[+0xE8]: constants alone drove it to -47 and
    it rendered as a sliver; shipping BOTH halves together still broke it,
    so a THIRD term is unidentified. Fully reverted in v2.35.1.
  - WHAT IS NOW KNOWN, and it is a lot: the sub-flyout items are NOT
    windows (they are blits into the container's paint buffer, so no
    sweep can ever reach them); the window rect is corrected within ~1ms
    but the PAINT BUFFER is still 1x on Plot #1, giving 20-36ms = 1-2
    frames at 54.5fps; and no sweep cadence can fix that (the sweep
    already runs every ~16ms - the "4x/sec" in older notes is stale).
    Builder, closed-form geometry and the full constant table are in
    SC4-UI-ENGINE.md 4.6b + tools\uimap\SUBFLYOUT-*.md.
  - NEXT, deliberately small: a gForceRecreate buffer path already exists
    - move that recreate earlier (before Plot #1) instead of rebuilding
    construction. Acceptance test: DOBS srcBuf == window rect on Plot #1.
  - LAWS 29-33 minted: prize vs blast radius (the prize here was 20-36ms
    and the change was rebuilding working menus - refuse that trade); a
    constant is never alone; two halves computed from each other ship
    together or not at all; a shared setter serves more than one builder
    (discriminate by return address); "it worked for panel X" is not
    evidence about panel Y.
